Wówczas takie wykorzystanie tych informacji odbywa się z zastrzeżeniem wszelkich ograniczeń ustanowionych przez ten organ.
Artykuł 11 Biegli i świadkowie Urzędnik reprezentujący organ, do którego kierowany jest wniosek, może zostać upoważniony do stawienia się, w ramach przyznanego upoważnienia, jako biegły lub świadek w postępowaniu administracyjnym lub sądowym, dotyczącym spraw objętych niniejszym protokołem oraz może przedstawić potrzebne w tym postępowaniu przedmioty, dokumenty lub ich uwierzytelnione odpisy. Wniosek skierowany do urzędnika składa organ występujący z wnioskiem i musi on wskazywać wyraźnie, przed jakim organem administracyjnym lub sądowym będzie musiał stawić się urzędnik, w jakiej sprawie oraz z jakiego tytułu lub kwalifikacji prawnej.
Artykuł 12 Wydatki związane z udzieleniem pomocy Strony zrzekają się wszystkich wzajemnych roszczeń dotyczących zwrotu wydatków poniesionych w związku z wykonaniem niniejszego protokołu, z wyjątkiem w stosowanych przypadkach wydatków poniesionych na rzecz biegłych i świadków oraz tłumaczy ustnych i pisemnych niebędących pracownikami administracji publicznej.
Artykuł 13 Wykonanie
1. Wykonanie niniejszego protokołu powierza się odpowiednio, z jednej strony, organom celnym Republiki Kazachstanu, a z drugiej strony, właściwym służbom Komisji Europejskiej i organom celnym państw członkowskich Unii Europejskiej. Decydują one w sprawach związanych ze wszystkimi środkami praktycznymi i uzgodnieniami niezbędnymi do stosowania niniejszego protokołu, z uwzględnieniem obowiązujących przepisów, zwłaszcza w dziedzinie ochrony danych.
2. Strony konsultują się wzajemnie, a następnie na bieżąco przekazują sobie informacje na temat szczegółowych przepisów wykonawczych przyjętych zgodnie z postanowieniami niniejszego protokołu.
Artykuł 14 Inne umowy
1. Uwzględniając odpowiednie kompetencje Unii Europejskiej i państw członkowskich Unii Europejskiej, postano wienia niniejszego protokołu:
a) nie wpływają na zobowiązania Stron wynikające z jakiejkolwiek innej umowy lub konwencji międzynarodowej;
b) są uznawane za uzupełniające w stosunku do umów o wzajemnej pomocy, które zostały lub mogą zostać zawarte pomiędzy poszczególnymi państwami członkowskimi Unii Europejskiej a Republiką Kazachstanu; oraz
c) nie mają wpływu na przepisy Unii Europejskiej regulujące przekazywanie między właściwymi służbami Komisji Europejskiej i organami celnymi państw członkowskich Unii Europejskiej jakichkolwiek informacji otrzymanych na podstawie niniejszego protokołu, które mogłyby być przedmiotem zainteresowania Unii Europejskiej oraz państw członkowskich Unii Europejskiej.
2. Niezależnie od ust. 1 niniejszego artykułu, postanowienia niniejszego protokołu mają pierwszeństwo przed postanowieniami jakiejkolwiek umowy dwustronnej w sprawie wzajemnej pomocy, która została lub może zostać zawarta między poszczególnymi państwami członkowskimi Unii Europejskiej i Republiką Kazachstanu w zakresie, w jakim postanowienia takiej umowy dwustronnej są sprzeczne z postanowieniami niniejszego protokołu.
3. W sprawach związanych ze stosowaniem niniejszego protokołu Strony konsultują się wzajemnie w celu rozstrzyg nięcia sprawy w ramach regularnego dialogu pomiędzy Stronami dotyczącego spraw celnych.
ENHANCED PARTNERSHIP AND COOPERATION AGREEMENT between and its Member States, one part, and Republic Kazakhstan, other part Contracting Parties to Treaty on and Treaty on Functioning Union, hereinafter referred to as ‘the Member States’, and one part, and other part, hereinafter jointly referred to as ‘the Parties’, strong links between Parties and their common values, and their wish to further strengthen and extend links established in past through implementation Partnership and Cooperation Agreement between Communities and their Member States and Republic Kazakhstan, signed in Brussels on 23 January 1995, and — Central Asia Strategy for a New Partnership adopted by Council in June 2007 as well as Republic Kazakhstan's state programme ‘Path to Europe’ adopted in 2008;
commitment Parties to full implementation principles and provisions Charter United Nations (‘the UN Charter’), Universal Declaration Human Rights, and Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe (OSCE), in particular Helsinki Final Act, as well as other generally recognised norms international law;
strong commitment Parties to strengthen promotion, protection and implementation fundamental freedoms and human rights, and respect for democratic principles, rule law, and good governance;
strong adherence Parties to following principles in their cooperation in human rights and democracy: promotion shared goals, open and constructive political dialogue, transparency, and respect for inter national human rights standards;
commitment Parties to adhere to principles a free market economy;
growing importance trade and investment relations between and Republic Kazakhstan;
that Agreement will further strengthen close economic relationship between Parties and create a new climate and better conditions for further development trade and investment between them, including in field energy;
objective enhancing trade and investment, in all sectors, on an enhanced legal basis, in particular this Agreement and Agreement Establishing World Trade Organisation (‘the WTO Agreement’);
commitment Parties to promote international peace and security and peaceful settlement disputes, notably by cooperating in an effective manner to that end within framework UN and OSCE;
willingness Parties to further develop regular political dialogue on bilateral and international issues mutual interest;
commitment Parties to international obligations to fight against proliferation weapons mass destruction and their means delivery and to cooperate in areas non-proliferation, and nuclear safety and security;
commitment Parties to combat illicit trade and accumulation small arms and light weapons and bearing in mind adoption Arms Trade Treaty (‘the ATT’) by UN General Assembly;
importance active participation Republic Kazakhstan in implementation — Central Asia Strategy for a New Partnership;
commitment Parties to combat organised crime and trafficking in human beings and to step up cooperation on counter-terrorism;
commitment Parties to step up their dialogue and cooperation on migration-related issues, with a comprehensive approach aiming at cooperation on legal migration and tackling irregular migration and trafficking in human beings, and recognising importance readmission clause this Agreement;
ensuring balanced conditions in bilateral trade relations between and Republic Kazakhstan;
commitment Parties to compliance with rights and obligations arising from membership World Trade Organization (‘the WTO’), and to transparent and non-discriminatory implemen tation those rights and obligations;
commitment Parties to respect principle sustainable development, including by promoting implementation multilateral international agreements and regional cooperation;
enhancing mutually beneficial cooperation in all fields mutual interest and strengthening its framework as appropriate;
need for enhanced energy cooperation, security energy supply and facilitating development appropriate infrastructure, building on Memorandum Understanding on cooperation in field energy between and Republic Kazakhstan done in Brussels on 4 December 2006, and in context Energy Charter Treaty;
that all cooperation in peaceful uses nuclear energy is governed by Cooperation Agreement between Atomic Energy Community and Republic Kazakhstan in field nuclear safety, signed in Brussels on 19 July 1999, and does not fall under this Agreement;
commitment Parties to improve level public health safety and protection human health as a precondition for sustainable development and economic growth;
commitment Parties to enhanced people-to-people contacts, including through cooperation and exchanges in fields science and technology, innovation development, education and culture;
that Parties shall promote mutual understanding and convergence their legislation and regulatory framework, in order to further strengthen mutually beneficial links and sustainable development;
that in case Parties decided, within framework this Agreement, to enter into specific agreements in area freedom, security and justice which were to be concluded by pursuant to Title V Part Three Treaty on Functioning Union, provisions such future agreements would not bind United Kingdom and/or Ireland unless Union, simultaneously with United Kingdom and/or Ireland as regards their respective previous bilateral relations, notifies Republic Kazakhstan that United Kingdom and/or Ireland has/have become bound by such agreements as part in accordance with Protocol (No 21) on position United Kingdom and Ireland in respect area Freedom, Security and Justice annexed to Treaty on and Treaty on Functioning Union. Likewise, any subsequent EU-internal measures which were to be adopted pursuant to above mentioned Title V to implement this Agreement would not bind United Kingdom and/or Ireland unless they have notified their wish to take part or accept such measures in accordance with Protocol No 21. Also noting that such future agreements or such subsequent EU-internal measures would fall within Protocol (No 22) on position Denmark annexed to said Treaties, Article 1 General principles Respect for democratic principles and human rights as laid down in Universal Declaration Human Rights, OSCE Helsinki Final Act and Charter Paris for a New Europe, and other relevant international human rights instruments, and for principle rule law, underpins internal and international policies both Parties and constitutes an essential element this Agreement.
The Parties reiterate their commitment to principles a free market economy, promoting sustainable development and economic growth.
The implementation this Agreement shall be based on principles dialogue, mutual trust and respect, equal partnership, and mutual benefit and full respect for principles and values enshrined in UN Charter.
Article 2 Aims this Agreement
1. This Agreement establishes an enhanced partnership and cooperation between Parties within limits their respective competences, based on common interest and on deepening relationship in all areas its application.
2. This cooperation is a process between Parties that contributes to international and regional peace and stability and to economic development, and is structured around principles that Parties reaffirm also by their international commitments notably under UN and OSCE.
Article 3 Cooperation in regional and international organisations The Parties agree to cooperate and exchange views in framework regional and international fora and organisations.
Article 4 Political dialogue The Parties shall further develop and strengthen effective political dialogue in all areas mutual interest in order to promote international peace, stability and security, including in Eurasian continent, on basis international law, effective cooperation within multilateral institutions and shared values.
The Parties shall cooperate with a view to strengthening role UN and OSCE, and to improve efficiency relevant international and regional organisations.
The Parties shall deepen cooperation and dialogue on issues international security and crisis management in order to respond to current global and regional challenges and major threats.
The Parties undertake to strengthen cooperation on all subjects common interest and in particular observance international law, strengthening respect for democratic principles, rule law, human rights and good governance.
The Parties agree to work towards improving conditions for further regional cooperation, notably with regard to Central Asia and beyond.
Article 5 Democracy and rule law The Parties agree to cooperate in promotion and effective protection human rights and rule law, including through relevant international human rights instruments.
Such cooperation shall be achieved through activities mutually agreed upon by Parties, including by strengthening respect for rule law, further enhancing existing human rights dialogue, further developing democratic institutions, promoting human rights awareness, and enhancing cooperation within human rights bodies UN and OSCE.
Article 6 Foreign and security policy The Parties shall intensify their dialogue and cooperation in area foreign and security policy and shall address, in particular, issues conf lict prevention and crisis management, regional stability, non-proliferation, disarmament and arms control, nuclear security and export control arms and dual-use goods.
Cooperation shall be based on common values and mutual interests, aiming to increase effectiveness and rapprochement policy and to make use bilateral, regional and international fora.
The Parties reaffirm their commitment to principles respect for territorial integrity, inviolability borders, sovereignty and independence, as established in UN Charter and OSCE Helsinki Final Act, and their commitment to promote those principles in their bilateral and multilateral relations.
Article 7 Space security The Parties shall promote enhancement safety, security and sustainability all space-related activities, and agree to work together at bilateral, regional and international levels with aim safeguarding peaceful uses outer space.
Both Parties note importance preventing an arms race in outer space.
Article 8 Serious crimes international concern The Parties reaffirm that most serious crimes concern to international community as a whole should not go unpunished and that their prosecution should be ensured by taking measures at domestic or international level, including through International Criminal Court.
Giving due regard to preserving integrity Rome Statute, Parties agree to conduct a dialogue on, and shall seek to take steps towards universal adherence to, Rome Statute in accordance with their respective laws, including provision assistance for capacity building.
Article 9 Conf lict prevention and crisis management The Parties shall enhance cooperation on conf lict prevention, settlement regional conf licts and crisis management in order to create an environment peace and stability.
Article 10 Regional stability The Parties shall intensify their joint efforts to promote stability and security in Central Asia as well as to improve conditions for further regional cooperation, on basis principles established by UN Charter, OSCE Helsinki Final Act and other relevant multilateral documents, to which both Parties adhere.
Article 11 Countering proliferation weapons mass destruction The Parties consider that proliferation weapons mass destruction (WMD) and their means delivery, both to state and non-state actors, represents one most serious threats to international stability and security.
The Parties shall cooperate and contribute to countering proliferation WMD and their means delivery through full compliance with and implementation their respective international treaty obligations and other relevant internat ional obligations in field disarmament and non-proliferation. The Parties agree that this provision constitutes an essential element this Agreement.
Cooperation in this area is implemented, including by: (a) further developing export control systems in respect military and dual-use goods and technologies;
(b) establishing a regular political dialogue on issues covered by this Article.
Article 12 Small arms and light weapons The Parties shall cooperate and ensure coordination, complementarity and synergy in their efforts to fight against illicit trade in small arms and light weapons, including their ammunition, at all relevant levels, and agree to continue a regular political dialogue, including in multilateral framework.
This cooperation shall be implemented by Parties in full compliance with existing international agreements and UN Security Council resolutions, as well as their commitments within framework other international instruments applicable in this area to which Parties adhere. Both Parties are convinced in this regard value ATT.
Article 13 Counter-terrorism The Parties agree to work together at bilateral, regional and international levels to prevent and combat terrorism in full accordance with rule law, international law, international human rights standards, humanitarian law and relevant UN decisions, including UN Global Counter-Terrorism Strategy.
Cooperation between Parties shall aim to: (a) implement, as appropriate, UN resolutions, UN Global Counter-Terrorism Strategy, and their commitments under other international conventions and instruments on countering terrorism;
(b) exchange information on planned and committed acts terrorism, forms and methods carrying these out, and terrorist groups that plan, commit or have committed a crime in territory another Party, in accordance with international law and domestic legislation;
(c) exchange experience in prevention all forms terrorism, including public provocation on internet to commit a terrorist offence, as well as experience with means and methods combating terrorism, experience in technical areas, and training, offered or paid by institutions, bodies and agencies Union;
(d) intensify common efforts against financing terrorism and exchange views about processes radicalisation and recruitment; and (e) exchange best practices in area protection human rights in fight against terrorism.
Trade in goods Article 14 Most-favoured-nation treatment
1. Each Party shall accord most-favoured-nation treatment to goods other Party in accordance with Article I General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade 1994 (GATT 1994), including its interpretative notes, which are incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis.
2. Paragraph 1 shall not apply in respect preferential treatment accorded by either Party to goods another country in accordance with GATT 1994.
Article 15 National treatment Each Party shall accord national treatment to goods other Party in accordance with Article III GATT 1994, including its interpretative notes, which are incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis .
Article 16 Import and export customs duties Each Party shall apply import and export customs duties in accordance with its WTO tariff commitments.
Article 17 Import and export restrictions Neither Party may institute or maintain any prohibition or restriction other than duties, taxes or other charges, whether made effective through quotas, import or export licences or other measures, on importation any good other Party or on exportation or sale for export any good destined for territory other Party, in accordance with Article XI GATT 1994, including its interpretative notes, which are incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis .
Article 18 Temporary admission goods Each Party shall grant other Party exemption from import charges and duties on goods admitted temporarily, in instances and according to procedures stipulated by any international convention on temporary admission goods binding upon it. This exemption shall be applied pursuant to legislation Party granting exemption.
Article 19 Transit The Parties agree that principle freedom transit is an essential condition attaining objectives this Agreement. In that regard, each Party shall provide for freedom transit through its territory goods consigned from or destined for customs territory other Party in accordance with Article V GATT 1994, including its in terpretative notes, which are incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis.
Article 20 Safeguard measures Nothing in this Agreement shall prejudice or affect rights and obligations either Party under Article XIX GATT 1994 and WTO Agreement on Safeguards.
Article 21 Special agriculture safeguard Nothing in this Agreement shall prejudice or affect rights and obligations either Party under Article 5 (Special Safeguard Provisions) WTO Agreement on Agriculture.
Article 22 Anti-dumping and countervailing measures
1. Nothing in this Agreement shall prejudice or affect rights and obligations either Party under Article VI GATT 1994, WTO Agreement on Implementation Article VI GATT 1994 and WTO Agreement on Subsidies and Countervailing Measures (‘SCM Agreement’).
2. Before final determination is made, Parties shall ensure disclosure all essential facts under consideration which form basis for decision to apply measures, without prejudice to Article 6.5 WTO Agreement on Implementation Article VI GATT 1994 and Article 12.4 SCM Agreement. Disclosures shall allow interested parties sufficient time to make their comments.
3. Provided it does not unnecessarily delay conduct investigation, each interested party shall be granted possibility to be heard in order to express their views during anti-dumping or countervailing investigations.
4. The provisions this Article shall not be subject to Dispute Settlement provisions in this Agreement.
Article 23 Pricing Each Party shall ensure that undertakings or entities to which it grants special or exclusive rights or which it controls, and which sell goods on domestic market and which also export same product, maintain separate accounts so that following shall be clearly ascertained: (a) costs and revenues associated with domestic and international activities; and (b) full details methods by which costs and revenues are assigned or allocated to domestic and international activities.
These separate accounts shall be based on accounting principles causality, objectivity, transparency and consistency, according to internationally recognised accounting standards, and be based on audited data.
Article 24 Exceptions
1. The Parties affirm that their existing rights and obligations under Article XX GATT 1994 and its interpretat ive notes shall apply to trade in goods covered by this Agreement, mutatis mutandis. To that end, Article XX GATT 1994 and its interpretative notes are incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis.
2. The Parties understand that before taking any measures provided for in subparagraphs (i) and (j) Article XX GATT 1994, Party intending to take measures shall supply other Party with all relevant information, with a view to seeking a solution acceptable to Parties. The Parties may agree on any means needed to put an end to difficulties. If no agreement is reached within 30 days supplying such information, Party may apply measures under this Article on good concerned. Where exceptional and critical circumstances requiring immediate action make prior information or examination impossible, Party intending to take measures may apply forthwith precautionary measures necessary to deal with situation and shall inform other Party immediately thereof.
3. The Republic Kazakhstan may maintain certain measures inconsistent with Articles 14, 15 and 17 this Agreement, identified in Protocol on Accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO, until expiration transition periods provided for those measures in that Protocol.
Customs Article 25 Customs cooperation
1. The Parties shall strengthen cooperation in area customs in order to ensure a transparent trade environment, facilitate trade, enhance supply-chain security, promote safety consumers, stem f lows goods infringing intellectual property rights and fight smuggling and fraud.
2. In order to implement those objectives and within limits available resources, Parties shall cooperate, inter alia, to: (a) improve customs law, harmonise and simplify customs procedures, in accordance with international conventions and standards applicable in field customs and trade facilitation, including those developed by (including Customs Blueprints), World Trade Organisation and World Customs Organisation (in particular Revised Kyoto Convention);
(b) establish modern customs systems, including modern customs clearance technologies, provisions for authorised economic operators, automated risk-based analysis and controls, simplified procedures for release goods, postclearance controls, transparent customs valuation, and provisions for customs-to-business partnerships;
(c) encourage highest standards integrity in area customs, in particular at border, through application measures ref lecting principles set out in Arusha Declaration World Customs Organisation;
(d) exchange best practices, and provide training and technical support for planning and capacity building and for ensuring highest standards integrity;
(e) exchange, where appropriate, relevant information and data whilst respecting Parties' rules on confidentiality sensitive data and on personal data protection;
(f) engage in coordinated customs actions between customs authorities Parties;
(g) establish, where relevant and appropriate, mutual recognition authorised economic operators' programmes and customs controls, including equivalent trade facilitation measures;
(h) pursue, where relevant and appropriate, possibilities for interconnectivity respective customs transit systems.
3. The Cooperation Council shall establish a Subcommittee on Customs Cooperation.
4. A regular dialogue shall take place on issues covered by this Chapter. The Cooperation Committee may establish rules for conduct such dialogue.
Article 26 Mutual administrative assistance Without prejudice to other forms cooperation envisaged in this Agreement, in particular in Article 25, Parties shall provide each other with mutual administrative assistance in customs matters in accordance with Protocol to this Agreement on Mutual Administrative Assistance in Customs Matters.
Article 27 Customs valuation The Agreement on Implementation Article VII GATT 1994 shall govern customs valuation goods in trade between Parties. Its provisions are hereby incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis .
Technical Barriers to Trade Article 28 W TO Agreement on Technical Barriers to Trade The Parties affirm that in their relations they will respect rights and obligations WTO Agreement on Technical Barriers to Trade (‘TBT Agreement’) which is incorporated into and made part this Agreement, mutatis mutandis .
Article 29 Technical regulation, standardisation, metrology, accreditation, market surveillance and conformity assessment
1. The Parties agree to: (a) reduce differences which exist between them in fields technical regulation, standardisation, legal metrology, accreditation, market surveillance and conformity assessment, including by encouraging use internationally agreed instruments in those fields;
(b) promote use accreditation in accordance with international rules in support conformity assessment bodies and their activities; and (c) promote participation and, where possible, membership Republic Kazakhstan and its relevant bodies in organisations activity which relates to standardisation, metrology, conformity assessment and related functions.
2. The Parties aim to set up and maintain a process through which gradual alignment their technical regulations, standards and conformity assessment procedures will be achieved.
3. For areas in which alignment has been achieved, Parties may consider negotiation agreements on conformity assessment and acceptance industrial products.
Article 30 Transparency
1. Without prejudice to provisions Chapter 13 (Transparency) this Title, each Party shall ensure that its procedures for development technical regulations and conformity assessment procedures allow for public consultation interested parties at an early appropriate stage when comments resulting from public consultation can still be introduced and taken into account, except where this is not possible because an emergency or a threat thereof related to safety, health, environmental protection or national security.
2. In accordance with Article 2.9 TBT Agreement, each Party shall allow a period for comments at an early appropriate stage following notification proposed technical regulations or conformity assessment procedures.
Where a consultation process on proposed drafts technical regulations or conformity assessment procedures is open to public, each Party shall permit other Party, or natural or legal persons located in territory other Party, to participate on terms no less favourable than those accorded to natural or legal persons located in territory that Party.
3. Each Party shall ensure that its adopted technical regulations and conformity assessment procedures are publicly available.
Sanitary and phytosanitary matters Article 31 Objective The objective this Chapter is to set out principles applicable to sanitary and phytosanitary (SPS) measures and animal welfare issues in trade between Parties. These principles shall be applied by Parties in a manner which further facilitates trade, while preserving each Party's level protection human, animal or plant life or health.
Article 32 Principles
1. The Parties shall ensure that SPS measures are developed and applied on basis principles propor tionality, transparency, non-discrimination and scientific justification.
2. A Party shall ensure that its SPS measures do not arbitrarily or unjustifiably discriminate between its own territory and territory other Party to extent that identical or similar conditions prevail. SPS measures shall not be applied in a manner which would constitute a disguised restriction on trade.
3. The Parties shall ensure that SPS measures, procedures or controls are implemented and requests for information are addressed by relevant authorities each Party without undue delay, and in a manner no less favourable to imported products than to like domestic products.
Article 33 Import Requirements
1. The import requirements importing Party shall be applicable to entire territory exporting Party, subject to Article 35 this Chapter. The import requirements set out in certificates are based on principles Codex Alimentarius Commission (‘Codex’), World Organisation for Animal Health (OIE) and International Plant Protection Convention (IPPC), unless import requirements are supported by a science-based risk assessment conducted in accordance with applicable international rules as provided for in WTO Agreement on Application Sanitary and Phytosanitary Measures (‘the SPS Agreement’).
2. The requirements set out in import permits shall not contain more stringent sanitary and veterinary conditions than conditions laid down in certificates under paragraph 1 this Article.
Article 34 Equivalence Upon request by exporting Party and subject to a satisfactory evaluation by importing Party, equivalence shall be recognised by Parties, following relevant international procedures, in relation to an individual measure and/or groups measures and/or systems applicable in general or to a sector or part a sector.
Article 35 Measures linked to animal and plant health
1. The Parties shall recognise concept pest-free or disease-free areas and areas low pest or disease prevalence in accordance with SPS Agreement and relevant standards, guidelines or recommendations Codex, OIE and IPPC.
2. When determining pest-free or disease-free areas and areas low pest or disease prevalence, Parties shall consider factors such as geographical location, ecosystems, epidemiological surveillance, and effectiveness sanitary or phytosanitary controls in such areas.
Article 36 Trade facilitation
1. The Parties shall develop and apply trade facilitation tools on basis recognition by importing Party inspection and certification systems exporting Party.
2. Such trade facilitation tools aim at avoiding inspection by importing Party each consignment or each exporting establishment in territory exporting Party according to existing legislation. They may include an approval an exporting establishment and establishment lists exporting establishments in territory exporting Party based on guaranties given by exporting Party.
Article 37 Inspections and Audits Inspections and audits carried out by importing Party in territory exporting Party to evaluate latter's inspection and certification systems shall be performed in accordance with relevant international standards, guidelines and recommendations. The costs inspections and audits shall be borne by Party carrying out audits and inspections.
Article 38 Exchange information and cooperation
1. The Parties shall discuss and exchange information on existing SPS and animal welfare measures and on their development and implementation. Such discussions and exchange information shall, as appropriate, take into account SPS Agreement and standards, guidelines or recommendations Codex, OIE and IPPC.
2. The Parties agree to cooperate on animal and plant welfare through exchange information, expertise and experience with objective building up capacity in this field. Such cooperation shall be specific to needs a Party and be conducted with aim assisting each Party in complying with other Party's legal framework.
3. The Parties shall establish a timely dialogue on SPS issues upon request by either Party to consider matters relating to SPS and other urgent issues covered by this Chapter. The Cooperation Committee may adopt rules for conduct such dialogues.
4. The Parties shall designate and regularly update contact points for communication on matters covered by this Chapter.
Trade in services and establishment Section 1 General prov isions Article 39 Objective, scope and coverage
1. The Parties, reaffirming their respective commitments under WTO Agreement, hereby lay down necessary arrangements in view improving reciprocal conditions for trade in services and establishment.
2. Nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as imposing any obligation with respect to government procurement subject to provisions Chapter 8 (Government Procurement) this Title.
3. The provisions this Chapter shall not apply to subsidies granted by Parties.
4. Consistent with provisions this Agreement, each Party retains right to regulate and to introduce new regulations to meet legitimate policy objectives.
5. This Chapter shall not apply to measures affecting natural persons seeking access to employment market or Republic Kazakhstan, nor shall it apply to measures regarding citizenship, residence or employment on a permanent basis.
6. Nothing in this Chapter shall prevent Parties from applying measures to regulate entry natural persons into, or their temporary stay in, their territory, including those measures necessary to protect integrity of, and to ensure orderly movement natural persons across, their borders, provided that such measures are not applied in such a manner as to nullify or impair benefits accruing to any Party under provisions this Chapter ( ).
7. This Chapter does not apply to measures adopted or maintained by Parties affecting trade in services and establishment in audiovisual sector.
Article 40 Definitions For purposes this Chapter: (a) ‘measure’ means any measure by a Party, whether in form a law, regulation, rule, procedure, decision, administrative action or any other form;
(b) ‘measures adopted or maintained by a Party’ means measures taken by: (i) central, regional or local governments or authorities a Party; and (ii) non-governmental bodies a Party in exercise powers delegated by central, regional or local governments or authorities a Party;
(c) ‘natural person Union’ or ‘natural person Republic Kazakhstan’ means a national one Member States or Republic Kazakhstan according to their respective legislation;
(d) ‘juridical person’ means any legal entity duly constituted or otherwise organised under applicable law, whether for profit or otherwise, and whether privately owned or governmentally owned, including any corporation, trust, partnership, joint venture, sole proprietorship or association;
(e) ‘juridical person a Party’ means a juridical person or Republic Kazakhstan set up in accordance with law a Member State or Republic Kazakhstan, respectively, and having its registered office, its central administration, or its principal place business in territory to which Treaty on Functioning applies or in territory Republic Kazakhstan, respectively.
Should juridical person set up in accordance with law a Member State or Republic Kazakhstan, have only its registered office or central administration in territory to which Treaty on Functioning applies or in territory Republic Kazakhstan, respectively, it shall not be considered as a juridical person or Republic Kazakhstan, respectively, unless it engages in substantive business operations in territory to which Treaty on Functioning applies or in territory Republic Kazakhstan, respectively;
(f) Notwithstanding point (e), with regard to international maritime transport, including intermodal operations involving a sea leg, shipping companies established outside or Republic Kazakhstan and controlled by nationals a Member State or Republic Kazakhstan, respectively, shall also be beneficiaries provisions this Chapter if their vessels are registered in accordance with respective legislation in that Member State or in Republic Kazakhstan and f ly f lag a Member State or Republic Kazakhstan;
(g) an ‘economic integration agreement’ means an agreement substantially liberalising trade in services, including establishment, in accordance with General Agreement on Trade in Services (GATS), in particular Articles V and V bis GATS, and/or containing provisions substantially liberalising establishment in other economic activities meeting, mutatis mutandis , criteria Articles V and V bis GATS in respect such activities;
(h) ‘economic activities’ shall include activities an economic nature except economic activities performed in exercise governmental authority;
(i) ‘economic activities performed in exercise governmental authority’ means activities carried out neither on a commercial basis nor in competition with one or more economic operators;
(j) ‘operations’ means pursuit and maintenance economic activities;
(k) ‘subsidiary’ a juridical person means a juridical person which is effectively controlled by another juridical person that Party ( );
(l) ‘branch’ a juridical person means a place business not having legal personality which has appearance permanency, such as extension a parent body, has a management and is materially equipped to negotiate business with third parties so that such third parties, although knowing that there will, if necessary, be a legal link with parent body head office which is abroad, do not have to deal directly with such parent body but may transact business at place business constituting extension;
(m) ‘establishment’ means any type business or commercial presence, including (i) constitution, acquisition or maintenance a juridical person ( ); or (ii) creation or maintenance a branch or representative office ( ) within territory a Party for purpose performing an economic activity;
(n) ‘investor’ a Party means a natural or juridical person that seeks to perform or performs an economic activity through setting up an establishment;
(o) ‘services’ includes any service ( ) in any sector except services supplied in exercise governmental authority;
(p) ‘service supplied in exercise governmental authority’ means any service which is supplied neither on a commercial basis, nor in competition with one or more service suppliers;
(q) ‘services supplier’ means any natural or juridical person that supplies a service;
(r) ‘supply a service’ includes production, distribution, marketing, sale and delivery a service.
Section 2 Establishment and cross-border supply services Article 41 Scope and coverage
1. This Subsection applies to measures by Parties affecting establishment in all economic activities and crossborder supply services.
2. The Parties confirm their respective rights and obligations arising from their commitments under GATS.
For greater certainty, in regard to services, Parties' respective GATS schedules specific commitments ( ), including reservations and lists most-favoured-nation exemptions, shall be incorporated into and made part this Agreement and shall apply.
Article 42 Progressive improvement conditions for establishment
1. The Cooperation Committee meeting in trade configuration shall make recommendations to Parties for further liberalisation establishment in context this Agreement.
2. The Parties shall endeavour to avoid adoption any measure which renders conditions for establishment more restrictive than situation existing on day preceding date signature this Agreement.
Article 43 Progressive improvement conditions for cross-border supply services
1. The Parties fully recognise importance liberalising cross-border supply services between Parties.
2. The Cooperation Committee meeting in trade configuration shall make recommendations to Parties for further liberalisation cross-border supply services in context this Agreement.
Article 44 Scope and coverage This Subsection applies to measures by Parties affecting establishment in all economic activities other than services.
Article 45 Most-favoured-nation treatment
1. Each Party shall grant to juridical persons other Party treatment no less favourable than that it accords to juridical persons any third country with regard to their establishment.
2. Each Party shall grant to juridical persons other Party treatment no less favourable than that it accords to juridical persons any third country with regard to operation juridical persons other Party established in former Party's territory.
3. Any advantage, favour, privilege or immunity granted, in relation with local content requirements, by Republic Kazakhstan to juridical persons a WTO member established in Republic Kazakhstan in form a juridical person shall be accorded immediately and unconditionally to juridical persons established in Republic Kazakhstan in form a juridical person.
4. The treatment granted in accordance with paragraphs 1 and 2 shall not apply to treatment accorded by a Party pursuant to economic integration agreements, free trade agreements, agreements for avoidance double taxation and agreements primarily governing taxation matters, nor shall it be construed as extending to investment protection, other than treatment deriving from Article 46, including investor-to-state dispute settlement procedures.
5. Notwithstanding paragraph 4, as regards strategic resources and objects, Republic Kazakhstan shall in no case accord to subsidiaries juridical persons established in Republic Kazakhstan in form a juridical person, less favourable treatment than that accorded after date on which this Title starts to apply, to subsidiaries juridical persons any third country established in Republic Kazakhstan in form a juridical person.
Article 46 National treatment Subject to Parties' reservations set out in Annex I, (a) each Party shall grant to subsidiaries juridical persons other Party established in former Party's territory treatment no less favourable than that granted to that Party's own juridical persons with respect to their operations;
(b) Republic Kazakhstan shall grant to juridical persons and branches treatment no less favourable than that accorded to juridical persons and branches Republic Kazakhstan, respectively, in respect their establishment and operations for economic activities other than services. National treatment granted by Republic Kazakhstan is without prejudice to terms Protocol on Accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO.
Section 3 Temporar y presence natural persons f or business pur poses Article 47 Coverage and definitions
1. This Section applies to measures by Parties concerning entry into, and temporary stay in, their territories business visitors for establishment purposes, intra-corporate transferees, and contractual services suppliers in accordance with Article 39(5) and (6).
2. For purposes this Section: (a) ‘business visitors for establishment purposes’ means natural persons, employed in a senior position by a juridical person a Party, who are responsible for setting up an establishment in territory other Party. They do not offer or provide services or engage in any other economic activities than required for establishment purposes.
They do not receive remuneration from a source located within host Party;
(b) ‘intra-corporate transferees’ means natural persons who have been employed by a juridical person a Party or have been partners in it ( ) for at least one year and who are temporarily transferred to an establishment that may be a subsidiary, branch or head company juridical person a Party in territory other Party.
The natural person concerned must belong to one categories as defined in Parties' respective GATS schedules, which for purposes this Section shall apply to all economic activities;
(c) ‘contractual service supplier’ means a natural person employed by a juridical person a Party which itself is not an agency for placement and supply services personnel, nor acting through such agencies, and which has no establishment in territory other Party and which has concluded a bona fide contract ( ) to supply services with a final consumer in latter Party, requiring presence on a temporary basis its employees in that latter Party, in order to fulfil contract to provide services;
(d) ‘qualifications’ means diplomas, certificates and other evidence formal qualification issued by an authority, designated pursuant to legislative, regulatory or administrative provisions, certifying successful completion professional training.
Article 48 Intra-corporate transferees and business visitors for establishment purposes
1. For services, Parties reaffirm their respective obligations arising from their commitments under GATS as regards entry and temporary stay intra-corporate transferees or business visitors for establishment purposes. The reservations listed therein apply ( ).
2. For economic activities other than services and subject to reservations set out in Annex II: (a) each Party shall allow investors engaged in production goods on territory other Party to transfer intracorporate transferees, as defined in Article 47(2)(b), and business visitors for establishment purposes, as defined in Article 47(2)(a). The entry and temporary stay shall be permitted for a period up to three years for intra-corporate transferees, and 90 days in any 12-month period for business visitors for establishment purposes;
(b) neither Party shall maintain or adopt measures defined as limitations on total number natural persons that an investor may transfer as intra-corporate transferees or business visitors for establishment purposes in form numerical quotas or a requirement an economic needs test and as discriminatory limitations.
Article 49 Contractual service suppliers
1. The Republic Kazakhstan shall allow supply services in its territory by juridical persons through presence natural persons who are citizens Member States Union, subject to following conditions: (a) natural persons entering Republic Kazakhstan shall possess: (i) a university degree or an advanced technical qualification demonstrating knowledge an equivalent level; and (ii) professional qualifications where this is required to exercise an activity in sector concerned pursuant to law, regulations or requirements Republic Kazakhstan;
(b) natural persons shall not receive remuneration for provision services, other than remuneration paid by juridical person Union, during their stay in Republic Kazakhstan.
(c) natural persons entering Republic Kazakhstan shall have been employed by juridical person for at least year preceding date submission an application for entry into Republic Kazakhstan. In addition, natural persons shall possess, at date submission an application for entry into Republic Kazakhstan, at least five years professional experience in sector activity which is subject contract;
(d) Republic Kazakhstan may apply economic needs test and an annual quota for work permits reserved for contractual service suppliers gaining access to services market Republic Kazakhstan. The total number contractual service suppliers entering services market Republic Kazakhstan shall not exceed 800 persons per year;
(e) after expiration a five-year period following accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO, economic needs test shall not be applied ( ). During period when Republic Kazakhstan applies economic needs test ( ), entry and temporary stay natural persons within Republic Kazakhstan pursuant to fulfilment contract shall be for a cumulative period not more than four months in any 12-month period or for duration contract, whichever is less. After expiration a five-year period following accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO, entry and temporary stay shall be for a cumulative period not more than six months in any 12-month period or for duration contract, whichever is less. The juridical persons shall be responsible for timely departure their employees from territory Republic Kazakhstan.
2. The Republic Kazakhstan shall allow supply services into its territory by juridical persons through presence natural persons if service contract fulfils following conditions: (a) contract to provide services: (i) has been concluded directly between juridical person and final consumer, which is a juridical person Republic Kazakhstan;
(ii) requires temporary presence on territory Republic Kazakhstan employees that juridical person in order to provide service; and (iii) complies with laws, regulations and requirements Republic Kazakhstan;
(b) contract to provide services is concluded in one following sectors activity which are included and defined in Republic Kazakhstan's GATS schedule commitments: (i) legal services (ii) accounting and bookkeeping services (iii) taxation services (iv) architectural services (v) engineering services (vi) integrated engineering services (vii) urban planning and landscape architecture services (viii) computer and related services (ix) advertising services (x) market research services (xi) management consulting services (xii) services related to management consulting (xiii) technical testing and analysis services (xiv) advisory and consulting services incidental to mining (xv) related scientific and technical consulting services (xvi) translation and interpretation services (xvii) maintenance and repair equipment, including transportation equipment, in context an after-sales services contract (xviii) environmental services.
(c) Access accorded under this paragraph relates only to service activity which is subject contract; it does not confer entitlement to exercise professional title in territory Republic Kazakhstan.
3. The reaffirms its respective obligations arising from commitments under GATS as regards entry and temporary stay contractual service suppliers. The reservations listed therein apply ( ).
Article 50 Most-favoured-nation treatment
1. Treatment accorded by to contractual service suppliers Republic Kazakhstan shall be no less favourable than that accorded to contractual service suppliers any third country.
2. Treatment granted under other agreements concluded by with a third country which have been notified under Article V GATS or which benefit from coverage GATS list most-favoured-nation exemptions, shall be excluded from scope paragraph 1. Treatment deriving from harmonisation regulations based on agreements concluded by providing for mutual recognition in accordance with Article VII GATS shall also be excluded from scope paragraph 1.
3. If Republic Kazakhstan grants more favorable treatment than that provided for in this Agreement to contractual service suppliers any other WTO member, except to countries Commonwealth Independent States (CIS) and countries which are parties to economic integration agreements with Republic Kazakhstan, that treatment shall be applied to contractual service suppliers Union. Treatment deriving from harmoni sation regulations based on agreements concluded by Republic Kazakhstan providing for mutual recognition in accordance with Article VII GATS shall also be excluded from this provision.
Article 51 Progressive improvement conditions for temporary presence natural persons for business purposes The Cooperation Committee meeting in trade configuration shall make recommendations to Parties for further liberalisation temporary presence natural persons for business purposes.
Section 4 Domestic regulation Article 52 Scope and coverage
1. The disciplines set out in Article 53 shall apply to measures by Parties relating to licensing and qualification procedures that affect: (a) cross-border supply services;
(b) establishment;
(c) supply a service through presence a natural person in territory other Party in accordance with Section 3 this Chapter.
2. The disciplines set out in Article 53 shall apply to all economic activities falling under scope this Chapter.
For services, they shall apply to extent relevant Party's specific commitments under GATS ( ). The disciplines do not apply to measures to extent that they constitute limitations subject to scheduling under Article XVI or XVII GATS.
Article 53 Licensing and qualification
1. Each Party shall ensure that licensing and qualification procedures for purpose obtaining an authorisation to supply a service or to establish are reasonable, clear and relevant to underlying policy objectives, taking into account nature requirements to be met and criteria to be assessed, and do not in themselves constitute a restriction on supply services or establishment.
2. Where specific time periods for applications exist, an applicant shall be allowed a reasonable period for submission an application. The competent authority shall initiate processing an application without undue delay. Where possible, applications should be accepted in electronic format under same conditions authenticity as applications submitted in paper format.
3. Authenticated copies should be accepted, where possible, in place original documents.
4. Each Party shall ensure that processing an application, including reaching a final decision, is completed within a reasonable period specified in its legislation or, in any event, without undue delay. Each Party shall endeavour to establish normal timeframe for processing an application. Each Party shall ensure that a licence or an auth orisation, once granted, enters into effect without undue delay in accordance with terms and conditions specified therein.
5. Each Party shall ensure that licensing fees ( ) are reasonable in terms costs incurred by competent authority, and do not in themselves restrict supply service or establishment.
6. Where competent authority considers that an application is incomplete or determines that it needs additional information, it shall, within a reasonable period time: (a) inform applicant;
(b) to extent practicable, identify information required; and (c) to extent practicable, provide opportunity to correct deficiencies.
7. If competent authority rejects an application, it shall inform applicant without undue delay and, to extent practicable, in writing. The competent authority should inform applicant, upon request, reasons for rejection application and, where possible, any deficiencies that have been identified. It should inform applicant procedures for appeal against decision in accordance with relevant legislation. The competent authority should permit an applicant to submit a new application in accordance with relevant authority's established procedures, except where relevant authority limits number licences or qualification determinations.
8. Each Party shall ensure that procedures used by, and decisions of, competent authority in licensing or authorisation procedure are impartial with respect to all applicants. The competent authority should reach its decision in an independent manner and not be accountable to any services supplier or investor for which licence or authorisation is required.
Section 5 Sector specif ic prov isions Article 54 International maritime transport
1. This Article sets out principles for liberalisation international maritime transport services. This Article is without prejudice to rights and obligations deriving from each Party's GATS commitments.
2. For purposes this Article, ‘international maritime transport’ includes door to door and multi-modal transport operations, which is carriage goods using more than one mode transport, involving a sea leg, under a single transport document and, to this effect, including right international maritime transport suppliers to directly contract with providers other modes transport.
3. With respect to activities referred to in paragraph 4, undertaken by shipping agencies for provision services with regards to international maritime transport, each Party shall permit juridical persons other Party to establish subsidiaries or branches in its territory, under conditions establishment and operation no less favourable than those accorded to its own subsidiaries or branches or to subsidiaries or branches any third country, whichever are better.
This paragraph does not apply to establishment for purpose operating a f leet under national f lag a Member State or Republic Kazakhstan.
4. Such activities include, but are not limited to: (a) marketing and sales maritime transport and related services through direct contact with customers, from quotation to invoicing, whether these services are operated or offered by service supplier itself or by service suppliers with which service seller has established standing business agreements;
(b) purchase and use, on their own account or on behalf their customers (and resale to their customers) any transport and related services, including transport services by any inland mode, necessary for supply an intermodal service;
(c) preparation documentation concerning transport documents, customs documents, or other documents related to origin and character goods transported;
(d) provision business information by any means, including computerised information systems and electronic data interchange (subject to any non-discriminatory restrictions concerning telecommunications);
(e) setting up any business arrangement with other shipping agencies, including participation company's stock and appointment personnel recruited locally (or, in case foreign personnel, subject to relevant provisions this Agreement), with any locally established shipping agency;
(f) acting on behalf juridical persons, inter alia, in organising call vessel or taking over cargoes when required.
5. In view existing level liberalisation concerning cross-border supply services between Parties in international maritime transport: (a) Parties shall apply effectively principle unrestricted access to international markets and trades on a commercial and non-discriminatory basis;
(b) each Party shall grant to ships operated by service suppliers other Party treatment no less favourable than that accorded to its own ships or those any third country, whichever are better with regard to, inter alia, access to ports, use infrastructure and services ports, and use maritime auxiliary services, as well as related fees and charges, customs facilities and assignment berths and facilities for loading and unloading.
6. In applying principles set out in paragraph 5, Parties shall: (a) not apply, as from entry into force this Agreement, any cargo-sharing provisions bilateral agreements between any Member State and Republic Kazakhstan;
(b) not introduce cargo-sharing clauses into future bilateral agreements with third countries, other than in those exceptional circumstances where liner shipping companies from one or other Party to this Agreement would not otherwise have an effective opportunity to ply for trade to and from third country concerned;
(c) prohibit cargo-sharing arrangements in future bilateral agreements concerning dry and liquid bulk trade;
(d) upon entry into force this Agreement, abolish and abstain from introducing any unilateral measures and administrative, technical and other obstacles which could constitute a disguised restriction or have discriminatory effects on free supply services in international maritime transport.
7. Natural and juridical persons providing international maritime transport services shall be free to provide international sea-river services in inland waterways Republic Kazakhstan and vice versa.
8. The Parties shall make available to international maritime transport suppliers other Party on reasonable and non-discriminatory terms and conditions following services at port: pilotage, towing and tug assistance, provisioning, fuelling and watering, garbage collecting and ballast water disposal, port captain's services, navigation aids, shore-based operational services essential to ship operations, including communications, water and electricity supplies, emergency repair facilities, anchorage, berth and berthing services.
9. If Republic Kazakhstan grants more favourable treatment for maritime transport to any other WTO member, except for coastal states Caspian Sea and for CIS countries, those terms shall be applied to natural and juridical persons Union.
Article 54 bis Road, rail, inland waterways and air transport With a view to ensuring coordinated development transport between Parties adapted to their reciprocal commercial needs, conditions mutual market access in road, rail and inland waterways and, if applicable, in air transport may be dealt with by potential specific agreements negotiated between Parties after entry into force this Agreement.
Section 6 Exc eptions Article 55 General exceptions
1. Subject to requirement that such measures are not applied in a manner which would constitute a means arbitrary or unjustifiable discrimination between countries where like conditions prevail, or a disguised restriction on establishment, including operations, or cross-border supply services, nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as preventing adoption or enforcement by any Party measures: (a) necessary to protect public security or public morals or to maintain public order ( );
(b) necessary to protect human, animal or plant life or health;
(c) relating to conservation exhaustible natural resources if such measures are applied in conjunction with restrictions on domestic investors or on domestic supply or consumption services;
(d) necessary for protection national treasures artistic, historic or archaeological value;
(e) necessary to secure compliance with laws or regulations which are not inconsistent with this Title, including those relating to: (i) prevention deceptive and fraudulent practices, or those necessary to deal with effects a default on contracts;
(ii) protection privacy individuals in relation to processing and dissemination personal data and protection confidentiality individual records and accounts;
(iii) safety;
(f) inconsistent with Article 46, provided that difference in treatment is aimed at ensuring effective or equitable imposition or collection direct taxes in respect economic activities, investors or services suppliers other Party ( ).
2. This Chapter shall not apply to Parties' respective social security systems or to activities in territory each Party, which are connected, even occasionally, with exercise governmental authority.
Section 7 Inve s tment Article 56 Review and consultations In order to identify any barriers to investment, Parties shall jointly review investment legal framework no later than three years after date on which this Title starts to apply. On basis this review, they shall consider opportunity to start negotiations to address such barriers, with a view to supplementing this Agreement, including with respect to general principles investment protection.
Capital movements and payments Article 57 Current account Each Party shall authorise, in a freely convertible currency and in accordance with Articles Agreement Inter national Monetary Fund (IMF), as applicable, all payments and transfers on current account balance-ofpayments between Parties.
Article 58 Movement capital
1. With regard to transactions on capital and financial account balance-of-payments and without prejudice to other provisions this Agreement, Parties undertake not to impose restrictions on free movement capital relating to direct investments made in accordance with laws host country, to economic activities covered by Chapter 5 (Trade in Services and Establishment) this Title and to liquidation and repatriation such invested capital and any profit generated therefrom.
2. With regard to transactions on capital and financial account balance-of-payments not covered by paragraph 1, and without prejudice to other provisions this Agreement, each Party shall ensure, in accordance with its laws, free movement capital relating to, inter alia: (a) credits relating to commercial transactions, including provision services, in which a resident a Party is participating;
(b) financial loans and credits; or (c) capital participation in a juridical person with no intention establishing or maintaining lasting economic links.
3. Without prejudice to other provisions this Agreement, Parties shall not introduce any new restrictions on movement capital between residents Parties and shall not make existing arrangements more restrictive.
4. The Parties may hold consultations with a view to further facilitating movement capital between them.
Article 59 Exceptions Subject to requirement that such measures are not applied in a manner which would constitute a means arbitrary or unjustifiable discrimination between countries where like conditions prevail, or a disguised restriction on capital movements, nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as preventing adoption or enforcement by either Party measures: (a) necessary to protect public security and public morals or to maintain public order; or (b) necessary to secure compliance with laws or regulations which are not inconsistent with provisions this Title, including those relating to: (i) prevention criminal or penal offences, deceptive and fraudulent practices, or those necessary to deal with effects a default on contracts (bankruptcy, insolvency and protection right creditors);
(ii) measures adopted or maintained to ensure integrity and stability a Party's financial system;
(iii) issuing, trading or dealing in securities, options, futures or other derivatives;
(iv) financial reporting or record keeping transfers when necessary to assist law enforcement or financial regulatory authorities; or (v) ensuring compliance with orders or judgements in juridical or administrative proceedings.
Article 60 Temporary safeguard measures with regard to capital movements, payments or transfers In exceptional circumstances serious difficulties for operation monetary and exchange rate policy, in case Republic Kazakhstan, or for operation economic and monetary union, in case Union, or threat thereof, safeguard measures that are strictly necessary may be taken by concerned Party with regard to capital movements, payments or transfers for a period not exceeding one year. The Party which maintains or adopts such measures shall inform other Party forthwith and present, as soon as possible, a time schedule for their removal.
Intellectual Property Article 61 Objectives The objectives this Chapter are to: (a) facilitate production and commercialisation innovative and creative products between Parties; and (b) achieve an adequate and effective level protection and enforcement intellectual property rights.
Section 1 Pr inciples Article 62 Nature and scope obligations
1. The Parties recall their obligation to ensure adequate and effective implementation international agreements dealing with intellectual property to which they are parties, including WTO Agreement on Trade-Related Aspects Intellectual Property Rights (‘the TRIPS Agreement’). The provisions this Chapter shall complement and further specify rights and obligations between Parties under TRIPS Agreement and other international agreements in field intellectual property.
2. For purposes this Agreement, term ‘intellectual property’ refers, inter alia, to all categories intellectual property referred to in Articles 65 to 96.
3. Protection intellectual property includes protection against unfair competition as referred to in Article 10 bis Paris Convention for Protection Industrial Property 1883, as revised and amended (‘the Paris Convention’).
4. This Chapter shall not prevent Parties from applying provisions in their law providing for higher standards for protection and enforcement intellectual property rights, provided that those provisions do not contravene provisions this Chapter.
Article 63 Transfer technology
1. The Parties agree to exchange views and information on their law and international practices on protection and enforcement intellectual property rights, affecting transfer technology. This shall, in particular, include exchanges on measures to facilitate information f lows, business partnerships, and voluntary licensing and subcontracting agreements. Particular attention shall be paid to conditions necessary to create an adequate enabling environment for technology transfer in host countries, including issues such as domestic legal framework and development human capital.
2. When measures are taken with regard to technology transfer, legitimate interests intellectual property right holders shall be protected.
Article 64 Exhaustion Each Party shall apply a national or regional ( ) exhaustion regime intellectual property rights, in accordance with its respective domestic law, in respect copy right and related rights, designs, and trademarks.
Section 2 Standards concerning intellectual property rights Article 65 Protection granted Each Party shall comply with rights and obligations set out in following international agreements: (a) Berne Convention for Protection Literary and Artistic Works (Berne Convention);
(b) International Convention for Protection Performers, Producers Phonograms and Broadcasting Organi zations (Rome Convention);
(c) World Intellectual Property Organization (WIPO) Copyright Treaty;
(d) WIPO Performances and Phonograms Treaty;
(e) TRIPS Agreement.
Article 66 Authors Each Party shall, as regards authors, provide for exclusive right to authorise or prohibit: (a) direct or indirect, temporary or permanent, reproduction by any means and in any form, in whole or in part, their works;
(b) any form distribution to public by sale or otherwise original their works or copies thereof;
(c) any communication to public their works, by wire or wireless means, including making available to public their works in such a way that members public may access them from a place and at a time individually chosen by them.
Article 67 Performers Each Party shall, as regards performers, provide for exclusive right to authorise or prohibit: (a) fixation ( ) their performances;
(b) direct or indirect, temporary or permanent, reproduction by any means and in any form, in whole or in part, fixations their performances;
(c) distribution to public, by sale or otherwise, fixations their performances;
(d) making available to public, by wire or wireless means, in such a way that members public may access them from a place and at a time individually chosen by them, fixations their performances;
(e) broadcasting by wireless means and communication to public their performances, except where performance is itself already a broadcast performance or is made from a fixation.
Article 68 Producers phonograms Each Party shall, as regards phonogram producers, provide for exclusive right to authorise or prohibit: (a) direct or indirect, temporary or permanent, reproduction by any means and in any form, in whole or in part, their phonograms;
(b) distribution their phonograms to public, by sale or otherwise, including copies thereof;
(c) making available their phonograms to public, by wire or wireless means, in such a way that members public may access them from a place and at a time individually chosen by them.
Article 69 Broadcasting organisations Each Party shall, as regards broadcasting organisations, provide for exclusive right to authorise or prohibit: (a) fixation their broadcasts;
(b) reproduction fixations their broadcasts;
(c) making available to public, by wire or wireless means, fixations their broadcasts in such a way that members public may access them from a place and at a time individually chosen by them; and (d) rebroadcasting their broadcasts by wireless means, as well as communication to public their broadcasts if such communication is made in places accessible to public against payment an entrance fee.
Article 70 Broadcasting and communication to public Each Party shall provide for right to ensure that a single equitable remuneration is paid by user, if a phonogram published for commercial purposes, or a reproduction such phonogram, is used for broadcasting by wireless means or for any communication to public, and to ensure that that remuneration is shared between relevant performers and phonogram producers. Each Party may, in absence agreement between performers and phonogram producers, lay down conditions as to sharing that remuneration between them.
Article 71 Term protection
1. The rights an author a literary or artistic work within meaning Article 2 Berne Convention shall run for life author and for no less than 70 years after his death.
2. In case a work joint authorship, term referred to in paragraph 1 shall be calculated from death last surviving author.
3. The rights performers shall expire no less than 50 years after date performance. However, if a fixation performance is lawfully published or lawfully communicated to public within this period, rights shall expire no less than 50 years after date first such publication or first such communication to public, whichever is earlier.
4. The rights producers phonograms shall expire no less than 50 years after fixation is made. However, if phonogram has been lawfully published within this period, said rights shall expire no less than 50 years after date first lawful publication. If no lawful publication has taken place within period mentioned in first sentence, and if phonogram has been lawfully communicated to public within this period, said rights shall expire no less than 50 years after date first lawful communication to public.
5. The rights broadcasting organisations shall expire no less than 50 years after first transmission a broadcast, whether that broadcast is transmitted by wire or wireless means, including by cable or satellite.
6. The terms laid down in this Article shall be calculated from first January year following event which gives rise to them.
7. The terms protection may exceed terms laid down in this Article.
Article 72 Protection technological measures
1. Each Party shall provide adequate legal protection against circumvention any effective technological measures, which person concerned carries out in knowledge, or with reasonable grounds to know, that he is pursuing that objective.
2. Each Party shall provide adequate legal protection against manufacture, import, distribution, sale, rental, advertisement for sale or rental, or possession for commercial purposes devices, products or components or provision services which are mainly directed at or enable circumvention any effective technological measures.
3. For purposes this Agreement, expression ‘technological measures’ means any technology, device or component that, in normal course its operation, is designed to prevent or restrict acts, in respect works or other subject matter, which are not authorised by right holder any copyright or related right as provided for by domestic law. Technological measures shall be deemed ‘effective’ where use a work or other subject matter is controlled by right holders through application an access control or protection process, such as encryption, scrambling or other transformation work or other subject matter or a copy control mechanism, which achieves protection objective.
Article 73 Protection rights-management information
1. Each Party shall provide adequate legal protection against any person performing without authority any following acts: (a) removal or alteration any electronic rights-management information;
(b) distribution, importation for distribution, broadcasting, communication or making available to public works or other subject matter protected under this Agreement from which electronic rights-management information has been removed or altered without authority, if that person knows, or has reasonable grounds to know, that by so doing he is inducing, enabling, facilitating or concealing an infringement any copyright or related right as provided for by domestic law.
2. For purposes this Chapter, expression ‘rights-management information’ means any information provided by right holders which identifies work or other subject matter protected by copyright or related rights, author or any other right holder, or information about terms and conditions use work or other subject matter, and any numbers or codes that represent such information.
3. Paragraph 1 shall apply when any items information referred to in paragraph 2 is associated with a copy of, or appears in connection with communication to public of, a work or other subject matter protected by copyright or related rights.
Article 74 Exceptions and limitations
1. In accordance with conventions and international agreements to which they are parties, each Party may provide for limitations or exceptions to rights set out in Articles 66 to 70 only in certain special cases which do not conf lict with a normal exploitation works or other subject matter and do not unreasonably prejudice legitimate interests right holders.
2. Each Party shall provide that temporary acts reproduction referred to in Articles 66 to 70, which are transient or incidental, which are an integral and essential part a technological process, and sole purpose which is to enable: (a) a transmission in a network between third parties by an intermediary, or (b) a lawful use a work or other subject matter to be made, and which have no independent economic significance, shall be exempted from reproduction right provided for in Articles 66 to 69.
Article 75 Resale right Each Party shall provide, for benefit author an original work art who is a national other Party and for benefit his successor in title, a resale right, to be defined as an inalienable right which cannot be waived, even in advance, to receive a royalty based on sale price obtained for resale work subsequent to first transfer work by author. The thresholds and rates collection royalties shall be established according to domestic law Party where resale takes place ( ).
Article 76 Cooperation on collective management rights The Parties shall take such reasonable measures as may be available to them to facilitate establishment arrangements between their respective collective management societies for purpose ensuring easier mutual access and delivery works and other protected subject matter between territories Parties, as well as transfer royalties between them for use such works or other protected subject matter. The Parties shall also take such reasonable measures as may be available to them to achieve a high level rationalisation and transparency with regard to execution tasks their respective collective management societies.
Article 77 International agreements Each Party shall: (a) comply with Protocol Relating to Madrid Agreement Concerning International Registration Marks and with WIPO Trademark Law Treaty; and (b) make all reasonable efforts to accede to Singapore Treaty on Law Trademarks.
Article 78 Registration procedure
1.
