Each Party shall provide for a system for registration trademarks in which each final decision taken by relevant competent trademark authority shall be duly reasoned and communicated in writing to applicant who will have opportunity to contest it before competent trademark authority and to appeal it before a court.
2. Each Party shall provide for possibility right holders to oppose trademark applications or registrations. The proceedings in case opposition shall be adversarial.
3. Each Party shall provide a publicly available electronic database trademark registrations.
Article 79 Well-known trademarks The Parties shall cooperate with aim making protection well-known trademarks, as referred to in Article 6 bis Paris Convention and in Article 16(2) and (3) TRIPS Agreement, effective.
Article 80 Exceptions to rights conferred by a trademark Each Party shall provide for limited exceptions to rights conferred by a trademark, such as fair use descriptive terms, use geographical indications, or other limited exceptions, which take account legitimate interests owner trademark and third parties.
Article 81 Def inition Geographical indications are, for purposes this Agreement, indications which identify a good as originating in territory a Party, or a region or locality in that territory, where a given quality, reputation or other characteristic good is essentially attributable to its geographical origin.
Article 82 Principles protection geographical indications
1. Each Party shall ensure adequate and indefinite protection geographical indications, by way a sui generis system protection and in accordance with domestic law, as long as geographical indication enjoys legal protection in country origin.
2. To that end, Parties shall cooperate in area geographical indications on basis this Article, which complements minimum standards set in relevant provisions TRIPS Agreement.
3. Each Party shall ensure that its geographical indications protection system is open to registration geographical indications other Party. Each Party shall provide for a publicly available electronic database registered geographical indications.
4. In respect geographical indications protected in its respective territory, each Party shall prohibit and prevent: (a) any direct or indirect commercial use a registered name in respect products not covered by registration in so far as: (i) those products are comparable to products protected under that name, or (ii) such use exploits reputation protected name;
(b) any misuse, imitation or evocation a registered name, even if true origin product is indicated or if protected name is translated, transcribed, transliterated or accompanied by an expression such as ‘style’, ‘type’, ‘method’, ‘as produced in’, ‘kind’, ‘imitation’ or similar;
(c) any other false or misleading indication as to provenance, origin, nature or essential qualities product, on inner or outer packaging, advertising material or documents relating to product concerned, and packing product in a container liable to convey a false impression as to its origin; or (d) any other practice liable to mislead consumer as to true origin good.
5. Each Party shall enforce protection provided for in Articles 81 to 83, including upon request from an interested party, by appropriate administrative enforcement in accordance with domestic law.
6. Each Party shall ensure that protected geographical indications may be used by any operator marketing a good conforming to corresponding specification.
7. Each Party shall ensure that names that they have protected according to domestic law do not become generic.
8. The Parties shall have no obligation to register a geographical indication where, in light a reputed or wellknown trademark, registration is liable to mislead consumers as to true identity good.
9. Without prejudice to this Article, each Party shall protect geographical indications also where a prior trademark exists. A ‘prior trademark’ shall mean a trademark use which corresponds to one situations referred to in paragraph 4, which has been applied for, registered or established by use, if that possibility is provided for by domestic law, before date on which application for registration geographical indication is filed with competent authority that Party. Such prior trademark may continue to be used and renewed notwithstanding protection geographical indication, provided that no grounds for trademark's invalidity or revocation exist in trademark law Party where it is registered or used.
Article 83 Negotiations The Parties shall, no later than seven years after date on which this Title starts to apply, commence negotiations with a view to concluding an agreement on protection geographical indications in their respective territories.
Article 84 International agreements The reaffirms its commitment to Geneva Act Hague Agreement Concerning International Registration Industrial Designs 1999. The Republic Kazakhstan shall make reasonable efforts to accede to it.
Article 85 Requirements for protection registered designs
1. Each Party shall provide for protection independently created designs that are new and original. This protection shall be provided by registration and shall confer an exclusive right upon holder a registered design in accordance with domestic law. For purposes this Article, a Party may consider that a design having individual character is original.
2. A design applied to or incorporated in a product which constitutes a component part a complex product shall only be considered to be new and to have individual character: (a) if component part, once it has been incorporated into complex product, remains visible during normal use latter, excluding maintenance, servicing or repair work, and (b) to extent that those visible features component part fulfil in themselves requirements as to novelty and individual character.
Article 86 Rights conferred by registration The holder a registered design shall have exclusive right to use it and to prevent any third party not having holder's consent from, inter alia, making, offering for sale, selling, importing, exporting, stocking or using a product bearing or embodying protected design when such acts are undertaken for commercial purposes.
Article 87 Protection conferred to unregistered designs The Republic Kazakhstan shall, no later than seven years after date on which this Title starts to apply, effectively provide for legal protection against copying unregistered designs, on condition that has, no later than two years before end that seven-year period, provided adequate training for representatives authorised bodies, organisations and judges.
Article 88 Term protection The duration protection as from date filing application shall amount to at least 10 years. Each Party may provide that right holder may have term protection renewed for one or more periods five years each, up to maximum term protection established according to domestic law.
Article 89 Exceptions
1. Each Party may provide for limited exceptions to protection designs, provided that such exceptions do not unreasonably conf lict with normal exploitation protected designs and do not unreasonably prejudice legitimate interests owner protected design, taking account legitimate interests third parties.
2. Design protection shall neither extend to features appearance dictated solely by technical functions product nor to features appearance a product that are necessary to ensure interoperability with another product ( ).
3. A design right shall not subsist in a design which is contrary to public policy or to accepted principles morality.
Article 90 Relationship to copyright A design protected by a design right registered in a Party shall also be eligible for protection under copyright law that Party as from date on which design was created or fixed in any form. The extent to which, and conditions under which, such a protection is conferred, including level originality required, shall be determined by each Party.
Article 91 International agreements Each Party shall make all reasonable efforts to comply with Articles 1 to 16 Patent Law Treaty.
Article 92 Patents and public health
1. The Parties recognise importance Declaration Ministerial Conference WTO on TRIPS Agreement and Public Health, adopted on 14 November 2001. In interpreting and implementing rights and obligations under this Chapter, each Party shall ensure consistency with that Declaration.
2. Each Party shall respect Decision WTO General Council 30 August 2003 on paragraph 6 Declaration referred to in paragraph 1.
Article 93 Supplementary protection certificates
1. The Parties recognise that medicinal and plant protection products protected by a patent on their respective territory may be subject to an administrative authorisation procedure before being put on market. They recognise that period that elapses between filing application for a patent and first authorisation to place product on market, as defined for that purpose by relevant domestic law, may shorten period effective protection under patent.
2. Each Party shall provide for a further period protection for a medicinal or plant protection product which is protected by a patent and which has been subject to an administrative authorisation procedure, that period being equal to period referred to in second sentence paragraph 1, reduced by a period five years.
3. Notwithstanding paragraph 2, duration further period protection may not exceed five years.
Article 94 Protection data submitted to obtain an authorisation to put a pharmaceutical product ( ) on market
1. Each Party shall implement a comprehensive system to guarantee confidentiality, non-disclosure and nonreliance data submitted for purpose obtaining an authorisation to put a pharmaceutical product on market.
2. Each Party shall ensure that any information submitted to obtain an authorisation to put a pharmaceutical product, as referred to in Article 39(3) TRIPS Agreement, on market remains undisclosed to third parties and benefits from a period at least six years protection against unfair commercial use starting from date grant a marketing authorisation in either Party.
To that end, (a) during a period at least six years starting from date grant a marketing authorisation, no person or entity, whether public or private, other than person or entity who submitted such undisclosed data, shall be allowed to rely directly or indirectly on such data, without explicit consent person or entity who submitted this data, in support an application for authorisation to put a pharmaceutical product on market;
(b) during a period at least six years starting from date grant a marketing authorisation, any subsequent application for authorisation to put a pharmaceutical product on market shall not be granted, unless subsequent applicant submits his own data, or data used with authorisation holder first authorisation, meeting same requirements as first applicant. During this six-year period, products registered without submission such data shall be removed from market until requirements are met.
Article 95 Data protection on plant protection products and rules on avoidance duplicative testing
1. The Parties shall determine safety and efficacy requirements before authorising placing on market plant protection products.
2. Each Party shall recognise a temporary data protection right to owner a test or study report submitted for first time to obtain a marketing authorisation for a plant protection product.
During period validity data protection right, test or study report shall not be used for benefit any other person aiming to achieve a marketing authorisation for a plant protection product, except when explicit consent owner is provided. This right shall be hereinafter referred to as ‘data protection’.
3. The test or study report shall: (a) be necessary for authorisation or for an amendment an authorisation in order to allow use on other crops, and (b) be certified as compliant with principles good laboratory practice or good experimental practice.
4. The term data protection for plant protection products in a Party shall be 10 years starting on date first authorisation in that Party. Longer periods may be granted by each Party in order to encourage authorisation of, for instance, low risk plant protection products or minor uses.
5. A test or study shall also be protected if it was necessary for renewal or review an authorisation.
6. The Parties shall lay down rules on avoidance duplicative testing on vertebrate animals. Any applicant intending to perform tests and studies involving vertebrate animals shall take necessary measures to verify that those tests and studies have not already been performed or initiated.
7. The prospective applicant and holder or holders relevant authorisations shall make every effort to ensure that they share tests and studies involving vertebrate animals. The costs sharing test and study reports shall be determined in a fair, transparent and non-discriminatory way. The prospective applicant shall only be required to share costs information he is required to submit to meet authorisation requirements.
8. Where prospective applicant and holder or holders relevant authorisations plant protection products cannot reach agreement on sharing test and study reports involving vertebrate animals, prospective applicant shall inform competent authority Party concerned.
9. The failure to reach agreement on sharing test and study reports involving vertebrate animals shall not prevent competent authority Party concerned from using those reports for purpose application prospective applicant.
The holder or holders relevant authorisation shall have a claim on prospective applicant for a fair share costs incurred by him. The Party may direct parties involved to resolve matter by formal and binding arbitration administered under domestic law.
Article 96 Plant varieties The reaffirms its commitment to International Convention for Protection New Varieties Plants (the UPOV Convention), to which Republic Kazakhstan shall make reasonable efforts to accede.
Section 3 Enfor cement intellectual proper ty r ights Article 97 General obligations
1. The Parties reaffirm their commitments under TRIPS Agreement, in particular Part III thereof, and shall provide for complementary measures, procedures and remedies set out in this Section, which are necessary to ensure enforcement intellectual property rights ( ).
2. Those measures, procedures and remedies shall be fair and equitable, and shall not be unnecessarily complicated or costly, or entail unreasonable time limits or unwarranted delays. They shall also be effective, proportionate and dissuasive and shall be applied in such a manner as to avoid creation barriers to legitimate trade and to provide for safeguards against their abuse.
Article 98 Entitled applicants Each Party shall recognise as persons entitled to seek application measures, procedures and remedies referred to in this Section and in Part III TRIPS Agreement: (a) holders intellectual property rights in accordance with provisions domestic law;
(b) all other persons authorised to use those rights, in particular licensees, in so far as permitted by and in accordance with provisions domestic law;
(c) intellectual property collective rights management bodies which are regularly recognised as having a right to represent holders intellectual property rights, in so far as permitted by and in accordance with provisions domestic law;
(d) professional defence bodies or other persons which are recognised as having a right to represent holders intellectual property rights, in so far as permitted by and in accordance with provisions domestic law.
Article 99 Evidence
1. The judicial authorities each Party shall have authority, where a party has presented reasonably available evidence sufficient to support its claims and has in substantiating its claims specified evidence which lies in control opposing party, to order that this evidence be produced by opposing party, subject to protection confidential information.
2. Under conditions referred to in paragraph 1, each Party shall take such measures as are necessary, in case an infringement an intellectual property right committed on a commercial scale, to enable competent judicial authorities to order, where appropriate and following an application, communication banking, financial or commercial documents under control opposing party, subject to protection confidential information.
Article 100 Measures for preserving evidence
1. Each Party shall ensure that even before commencement proceedings on merits case, competent judicial authorities may, on application by a right holder who has presented reasonably available evidence to support his claims that his intellectual property right has been infringed or is about to be infringed, order prompt and effective provisional measures to preserve relevant evidence in respect alleged infringement, subject to protection confidential information.
2. Such measures may include detailed description, with or without taking samples, or physical seizure alleged infringing goods, and, in appropriate cases, materials and implements used in production and/or distribution those goods and documents relating thereto. Those measures shall be taken, if necessary without other party being heard, in particular where any delay is likely to cause irreparable harm to right holder or where there is a demonstrable risk evidence being destroyed.
Article 101 Right information
1. Each Party shall ensure that, in context proceedings concerning an infringement an intellectual property right and in response to a justified and proportionate request claimant, competent judicial authorities may order that information on origin and distribution networks goods or services which infringe an intellectual property right be provided by infringer and/or any other person who: (a) was found in possession infringing goods on a commercial scale;
(b) was found to be using infringing services on a commercial scale;
(c) was found to be providing on a commercial scale services used in infringing activities; or (d) was indicated by person referred to in point (a), (b) or (c) as being involved in production, manufacture or distribution goods or provision services.
2. The information referred to in paragraph 1 shall comprise: (a) names and addresses producers, manufacturers, distributors, suppliers and other previous holders goods or services, as well as intended wholesalers and retailers;
(b) information on quantities produced, manufactured, delivered, received or ordered, as well as price obtained for goods or services in question.
3. Paragraphs 1 and 2 shall apply without prejudice to other statutory provisions which: (a) grant right holder rights to receive more detailed information;
(b) govern use in civil or criminal proceedings information communicated pursuant to this Article;
(c) govern responsibility for misuse right information;
(d) provide for opportunity to refuse to provide information which would force person referred to in paragraph 1 to admit to his own participation or that his close relatives in an infringement an intellectual property right; or (e) govern protection confidentiality information sources or processing personal data.
Article 102 Provisional and precautionary measures
1. Each Party shall ensure that judicial authorities may, at request applicant, issue against alleged infringer an interlocutory injunction intended to prevent any imminent infringement an intellectual property right, or to forbid, on a provisional basis and subject to, where appropriate, a recurring penalty payment where provided for by domestic law, continuation alleged infringements that right, or to make such continuation subject to lodging guarantees intended to ensure compensation right holder. An interlocutory injunction may also be issued, under same conditions and in accordance with domestic law, against an intermediary whose services are being used by a third party to infringe an intellectual property right.
2. An interlocutory injunction may also be issued to order seizure or delivery goods suspected infringing an intellectual property right so as to prevent their entry into or movement within channels commerce.
3. In case an infringement committed on a commercial scale, each Party shall ensure that if applicant demonstrates circumstances likely to endanger recovery damages, judicial authorities may, in accordance with domestic law, order precautionary seizure or arrest movable and/or immovable property alleged infringer, including blocking their bank accounts and other assets. To that end, judicial authorities may order provision bank, financial or commercial documents, or appropriate access to relevant information.
Article 103 Corrective measures
1. Each Party shall ensure that competent judicial authorities may order, at request applicant and without prejudice to any damages due to right holder by reason infringement, and without compensation any sort, recall, definitive removal from channels commerce, or destruction goods that they have found to be infringing an intellectual property right. If appropriate, competent judicial authorities may also order destruction materials and implements predominantly used in creation or manufacture those goods.
2. The judicial authorities each Party shall have power to order that those measures shall be carried out at expense infringer, unless particular reasons are invoked for not doing so.
Article 104 Injunctions Each Party shall ensure that, where a judicial decision is taken finding an infringement an intellectual property right, judicial authorities may issue against infringer an injunction aimed at prohibiting continuation infringement. Where provided for by domestic law, non-compliance with an injunction shall, where appropriate, be subject to a recurring penalty payment, with a view to ensuring compliance. Each Party shall also ensure that right holders are in a position to apply for an injunction against intermediaries whose services are used by a third party to infringe an intellectual property right.
Article 105 Alternative measures Each Party, in accordance with domestic law, may provide that, in appropriate cases and at request person liable to be subject to measures provided for in Article 103 and/or Article 104, competent judicial authorities may order pecuniary compensation to be paid to injured party instead applying measures provided for in Article 103 and/or Article 104 if that person acted unintentionally and without negligence, if execution measures in question would cause him disproportionate harm and if pecuniary compensation to injured party appears reasonably satisfactory.
Article 106 Damages
1. Each Party shall ensure that when judicial authorities set damages: (a) they shall take into account all appropriate aspects, such as negative economic consequences, including lost profits, which injured party has suffered, any unfair profits made by infringer and, in appropriate cases, elements other than economic factors, including moral prejudice caused to right holder by infringement;
or (b) they may, in appropriate cases, set damages as a lump sum on basis elements such as at least amount royalties or fees which would have been due if infringer had requested authorisation to use intellectual property right in question.
2. Where infringer did not, knowingly or with reasonable grounds to know, engage in infringing activity, each Party may lay down that judicial authorities may order recovery profits or payment damages, which may be pre-established, to injured party.
Article 107 Legal costs Each Party shall ensure that reasonable and proportionate legal costs and other expenses incurred by successful party shall, as a general rule, be borne by unsuccessful party, unless equity does not allow this.
Article 108 Publication judicial decisions Each Party shall ensure that, in legal proceedings instituted for infringement an intellectual property right, judicial authorities may order, at request applicant and at expense infringer, appropriate measures for dissemination information concerning decision, including displaying decision and publishing it in full or in part.
Article 109 Presumption authorship or ownership For purposes applying measures, procedures and remedies provided for in this Section, it shall be sufficient for name an author a literary or artistic work, in absence proof to contrary and in order to be regarded as author and consequently to be entitled to institute infringement proceedings, to appear on work in usual manner. This shall also apply, mutatis mutandis, to holders rights related to copyright with regard to their protected subject matter.
Article 110 Administrative procedures To extent that any civil remedy may be ordered as a result administrative procedures on merits a case, such procedures shall conform to principles equivalent in substance to those provided for in relevant provisions this Section.
Article 111 Border measures
1. When implementing border measures for enforcement intellectual property rights each Party shall ensure compliance with its obligations under GATT 1994 and TRIPS Agreement.
2. In order to ensure protection intellectual property rights in customs territory each Party, customs authorities, within limits their authority, shall adopt a range approaches to identify shipments containing goods suspected infringing intellectual property rights referred to in paragraphs 3 and 4. These approaches include riskanalysis techniques based, inter alia, on information provided by right holders, intelligence gathered and cargo inspections.
3. The customs authorities shall have power to take measures, upon application by right holder, to detain or suspend release goods under customs control which are suspected infringing trademarks, copyright and related rights, or geographical indications.
4. The customs authorities Republic Kazakhstan shall, no later than three years after date on which this Title starts to apply, have power to take measures, upon application by right holder, to detain or suspend release goods under customs control which are suspected infringing patents, utility models, industrial designs, topographies integrated circuits or plant variety rights, on condition that provides, before end second year this three-year period, adequate training for representatives authorised bodies, such as customs officials, prosecutors, judges and other personnel, as appropriate.
5. The customs authorities shall have power to detain or suspend, upon their own initiative, release goods under customs control which are suspected infringing trademarks, copyright and related rights, or geographical indications.
6. The customs authorities Republic Kazakhstan shall, no later than five years after date on which this Title starts to apply, have power to detain or suspend, upon their own initiative, release goods under customs control which are suspected infringing patents, utility models, industrial designs, topographies integrated circuits or plant variety rights, on condition that provides, no later than two years before end this five-year period, adequate training for representatives authorised bodies, such as customs officials, prosecutors, judges and other personnel, as appropriate.
7. Notwithstanding paragraphs 3 to 6, there shall be no obligation to apply detention or suspension measures to imports goods that have been put on market in another country by, or with consent of, right holder.
8. The Parties agree to effectively implement Article 69 TRIPS Agreement in respect international trade in goods suspected infringing intellectual property rights. For that purpose, each Party shall be ready to establish and notify to other Party a contact point in its customs administration in order to facilitate cooperation. Such cooperation may include exchanges information regarding mechanisms for receiving information from right holders, best practices, and experiences with risk management strategies, as well as information to aid in identification shipments suspected containing infringing goods.
9. The customs authorities each Party shall be ready to cooperate, upon request from other Party or upon their own initiative, in order to provide relevant available information to customs authorities other Party, in particular with regard to goods in transit through territory a Party destined for, or originating in, other Party.
10. Without prejudice to other forms cooperation, Protocol on Mutual Administrative Assistance in Customs Matters shall be applicable with regard to paragraphs 8 and 9 this Article with respect to breaches customs law relating to intellectual property rights.
11. Without prejudice to powers Cooperation Council, Subcommittee on Customs Cooperation referred to in Article 25(3) shall be responsible for ensuring proper functioning and implementation this Article.
The Subcommittee on Customs Cooperation shall set priorities and provide for adequate procedures for cooperation between competent authorities Parties.
Section 4 Liability inter mediar y ser vice prov iders Article 112 Use intermediaries' services The Parties recognise that services intermediaries could be used by third parties for infringing activities. To ensure free movement information services and at same time enforce intellectual property rights in digital environment, each Party shall provide for measures described in this Section concerning intermediary service providers, where those providers are in no way involved with information transmitted.
Article 113 Liability intermediary service providers: ‘mere conduit’
1. Where an information society service is provided that consists transmission in a communication network information provided by a recipient service, or provision access to a communication network, each Party shall ensure that service provider is not liable for information transmitted, on condition that provider: (a) does not initiate transmission;
(b) does not select receiver transmission; and (c) does not select or modify information contained in transmission.
2. The acts transmission and provision access referred to in paragraph 1 include automatic, intermediate and transient storage information transmitted in so far as this takes place for sole purpose carrying out transmission in communication network, and provided that information is not stored for any period longer than is reasonably necessary for transmission.
3. This Article shall not affect possibility for a court or administrative authority, in accordance with domestic law, requiring service provider to terminate or prevent an infringement.
Article 114 Liability intermediary service providers: ‘caching’
1. Where an information society service is provided that consists transmission in a communication network information provided by a recipient service, each Party shall ensure that service provider is not liable for automatic, intermediate and temporary storage that information, performed for sole purpose making more efficient information's onward transmission to other recipients service upon their request, on condition that: (a) provider does not modify information;
(b) provider complies with conditions on access to information;
(c) provider complies with rules regarding updating information, specified in a manner widely recognised and used by industry;
(d) provider does not interfere with lawful use technology, widely recognised and used by industry, to obtain data on use information; and (e) provider acts expeditiously to remove or to disable access to information it has stored upon obtaining actual knowledge fact that information at initial source transmission has been removed from network, or access to it has been disabled, or that a court or an administrative authority has ordered such removal or disablement.
2. This Article shall not affect possibility for a court or an administrative authority, in accordance with domestic law, requiring service provider to terminate or prevent an infringement.
Article 115 Liability intermediary service providers: ‘hosting’
1. Where an information society service is provided that consists storage information provided by a recipient service, each Party shall ensure that service provider is not liable for information stored at request a recipient service, on condition that: (a) provider does not have actual knowledge illegal activity or information and, as regards claims for damages, is not aware facts or circumstances from which illegal activity or information is apparent; or (b) provider, upon obtaining such knowledge or awareness, acts expeditiously to remove or to disable access to information.
2. Paragraph 1 shall not apply when recipient service is acting under authority or control provider.
3. This Article shall not affect possibility for a court or an administrative authority, in accordance with domestic law, requiring service provider to terminate or prevent an infringement, nor does it affect possibility for a Party establishing procedures governing removal or disabling access to information.
Article 116 No general obligation to monitor
1. The Parties shall not impose a general obligation on providers, when providing services covered by Articles 113 to 115, to monitor information which they transmit or store, nor shall they impose a general obligation to actively seek facts or circumstances indicating illegal activity.
2. A Party may establish obligations for information society service providers to promptly inform competent public authorities alleged illegal activities undertaken or information provided by recipients their service. A Party may also establish an obligation for information society service providers to communicate to competent authorities, at their request, information enabling identification recipients their service with whom they have storage agreements.
Article 117 Date application Articles 112 to 116 The Republic Kazakhstan shall fully implement obligations provided for in Articles 112 to 116 within five years from date on which this Title starts to apply.
Article 118 Cooperation
1. The Parties shall encourage development cooperation among trade or professional associations or organ isations aimed at protection and enforcement intellectual property rights.
2. The Parties agree to cooperate with a view to supporting implementation obligations undertaken under this Chapter. Areas cooperation include, but are not limited to, following activities: (a) exchange information on their respective legal frameworks concerning intellectual property rights and relevant rules protection and enforcement; exchange experiences on legislative progress in those areas;
(b) exchange experience on protection and enforcement intellectual property rights;
(c) exchange experience on protection and enforcement among customs, police, administrative and judiciary bodies and interested organisations; coordination to prevent exports counterfeit goods;
(d) capacity building; and (e) promotion and dissemination information and knowledge on intellectual property rights in, inter alia, business circles and civil society; promotion public awareness and knowledge consumers and right holders.
Government procurement Article 119 Definitions For purposes this Chapter: (a) ‘commercial goods or services’ means goods or services a type generally sold or offered for sale in commercial marketplace to, and customarily purchased by, non-governmental buyers for non-governmental purposes;
(b) ‘construction service’ means a service that has as its objective realisation by whatever means civil or building works, based on Division 51 United Nations Provisional Central Product Classification (‘CPCprov’);
(c) ‘days’ means calendar days;
(d) ‘electronic auction’ means a repetitive process involving an electronic device for presentation new prices, revised downwards, and/or new values concerning certain elements tenders, which occurs after an initial full evaluation tenders, enabling them to be ranked using automatic evaluation methods. Consequently, certain service contracts and certain works contracts having as their subject matter intellectual performances, such as design works, may not be object electronic auctions;
(e) ‘in writing’ or ‘written’ means any worded or numbered expression that can be read, reproduced and later communicated. It may include electronically transmitted and stored information;
(f) ‘limited tendering’ means a procurement method whereby procuring entity contacts a supplier or suppliers its choice;
(g) ‘measure’ means any law, regulation, procedure, administrative guidance or practice, or any action a procuring entity relating to a covered procurement;
(h) ‘multi-use list’ means a list suppliers that a procuring entity has determined satisfy conditions for participa tion in that list, and that procuring entity intends to use more than once;
(i) ‘notice intended procurement’ means a notice published by a procuring entity inviting interested suppliers to submit a request for participation, a tender, or both;
(j) ‘open tendering’ means a procurement method whereby all interested suppliers may submit a tender;
(k) ‘person’ means a natural person or a juridical person;
(l) ‘procuring entity’ means an entity covered under Parts 1 to 3 Annex III;
(m) ‘qualified supplier’ means a supplier that a procuring entity recognises as having satisfied conditions for parti cipation;
(n) ‘selective tendering’ means a procurement method whereby only qualified suppliers are invited by procuring entity to submit a tender;
(o) ‘services’ includes construction services, unless otherwise specified;
(p) ‘standard’ means a document approved by a recognised body that provides for common and repeated use, rules, guidelines or characteristics for goods or services, or related processes and production methods, with which compliance is not mandatory. It may also include or deal exclusively with terminology, symbols, packaging, marking or labelling requirements as they apply to a good, service, process or production method;
(q) ‘supplier’ means a person or group persons that provides or could provide goods or services;
(r) ‘technical specification’ means a tendering requirement that: (i) lays down characteristics goods or services to be procured, including quality, performance, safety and dimensions, or processes and methods for their production or provision; or (ii) addresses terminology, symbols, packaging, marking or labelling requirements, as they apply to a good or service.
Article 120 Scope and coverage Application this Chapter
1. This Chapter applies to any measure regarding covered procurement, whether or not it is conducted exclusively or partially by electronic means.
2. For purposes this Chapter, ‘covered procurement’ means procurement for governmental purposes: (a) goods, services, or any combination thereof: (i) as specified in Annex III; and (ii) not procured with a view to commercial sale or resale, or for use in production or supply goods or services for commercial sale or resale;
(b) by any contractual means, including: purchase; lease; and rental or hire purchase, with or without an option to buy;
(c) for which value equals or exceeds relevant threshold specified in Annex III, at time publication a notice in accordance with Article 124;
(d) by a procuring entity; and (e) that is not otherwise excluded from coverage in paragraph 3 this Article or in Annex III.
If value a procurement is uncertain, it shall be estimated in accordance with paragraphs 6 to 8.
3. Except where provided otherwise in Annex III, this Chapter does not apply to: (a) acquisition or rental land, existing buildings or other immovable property or rights thereon;
(b) non-contractual agreements or any form assistance that a Party provides, including cooperative agreements, grants, loans, equity infusions, guarantees and fiscal incentives;
(c) procurement or acquisition fiscal agency or depository services, liquidation and management services for regulated financial institutions or services related to sale, redemption and distribution public debt, including loans and government bonds, notes and other securities;
(d) public employment contracts;
(e) procurement conducted: (i) for specific purpose providing international assistance, including development aid;
(ii) under particular procedure or condition an international agreement relating to stationing troops or relating to joint implementation by signatory countries a project; or (iii) under particular procedure or condition an international organisation, or funded by international grants, loans or other assistance where applicable procedure or condition would be inconsistent with this Chapter.
4. Annex III specifies for each Party following information: (a) in Part 1, central government entities whose procurement is covered by this Chapter;
(b) in Part 2, sub-central government entities whose procurement is covered by this Chapter;
(c) in Part 3, all other entities whose procurement is covered by this Chapter;
(d) in Part 4, goods covered by this Chapter;
(e) in Part 5, services, other than construction services, covered by this Chapter;
(f) in Part 6, construction services covered by this Chapter; and (g) in Part 7, any General Notes.
5. Where a procuring entity, in context covered procurement, requires persons not covered under Annex III to procure in accordance with particular requirements, Article 122 shall apply mutatis mutandis to such requirements.
Valuation
6. In estimating value a procurement for purpose ascertaining whether it is a covered procurement, a procuring entity shall: (a) neither divide a procurement into separate procurements nor select or use a particular valuation method for estimating value a procurement with intention totally or partially excluding it from application this Chapter; and (b) include estimated maximum total value procurement over its entire duration, whether awarded to one or more suppliers, taking into account all forms remuneration, including: (i) premiums, fees, commissions and interest; and (ii) where procurement provides for possibility options, total value such options.
7. Where an individual requirement for a procurement results in award more than one contract, or in award contracts in separate parts (hereinafter referred to as ‘recurring contracts’), calculation estimated maximum total value shall be based on: (a) value recurring contracts same type good or service awarded during preceding 12 months or procuring entity's preceding fiscal year, adjusted, where possible, to take into account anticipated changes in quantity or value good or service being procured over following 12 months; or (b) estimated value recurring contracts same type good or service to be awarded during 12 months following initial contract award or procuring entity's fiscal year.
8. In case procurement by lease, rental or hire purchase goods or services, or procurement for which a total price is not specified, basis for valuation shall be: (a) in case a fixed-term contract: (i) where term contract is 12 months or less, total estimated maximum value for its duration; or (ii) where term contract exceeds 12 months, total estimated maximum value, including any estimated residual value;
(b) where contract is for an indefinite period, estimated monthly instalment multiplied by 48; and (c) where it is not certain whether contract is to be a fixed-term contract, basis for valuation under point (b) shall be used.
Article 121 General exceptions Subject to requirement that such measures are not applied in a manner that would constitute a means arbitrary or unjustifiable discrimination between Parties or a disguised restriction on international trade, nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as preventing a Party from imposing or enforcing measures: (a) necessary to protect public morals, order or safety;
(b) necessary to protect human, animal or plant life or health;
(c) necessary to protect intellectual property; or (d) relating to goods or services persons with disabilities, philanthropic institutions or prison labour.
Article 122 General principles Non-discrimination
1. With respect to any measure regarding covered procurement, each Party, including its procuring entities, shall unconditionally accord, to goods and services other Party and to suppliers other Party offering such goods or services, treatment no less favourable than treatment Party, including its procuring entities, accords to its own goods, services and locally established suppliers.
2. With respect to any measure regarding covered procurement, a Party, including its procuring entities, shall not: (a) treat a locally established supplier less favourably than another locally established supplier on basis degree foreign affiliation or ownership; or (b) discriminate against a locally established supplier on basis that goods or services offered by that supplier for a particular procurement are goods or services other Party.
Use Electronic Means
3. When conducting covered procurement by electronic means, a procuring entity shall: (a) ensure that procurement is conducted using information technology systems and software, including those related to authentication and encryption information, that are generally available and interoperable with other generally available information technology systems and software; and (b) maintain mechanisms that ensure integrity requests for participation and tenders, including establishment time receipt and prevention inappropriate access.
Conduct Procurement
4. A procuring entity shall conduct covered procurement in a transparent and impartial manner that: (a) is consistent with this Chapter, using methods such as open tendering, selective tendering, limited tendering and electronic auctions;
(b) avoids conf licts interest; and (c) prevents corrupt practices.
Rules Origin
5. A Party shall not, for purposes covered procurement, apply rules origin to goods or services imported from or supplied from other Party that are different from rules origin Party applies at same time in normal course trade to imports or supplies same goods or services from same Party.
Measures Not Specific to Procurement
6. Paragraphs 1 and 2 shall not apply to: customs duties and charges any kind imposed on, or in connection with, importation; method levying such duties and charges; other import regulations or formalities and measures affecting trade in services other than measures governing covered procurement.
Article 123 Information on procurement system
1. Each Party shall: (a) promptly publish any law, regulation, judicial decision, administrative ruling general application, standard contract clause mandated by law or regulation and incorporated by reference in notices or tender documentation and procedure regarding covered procurement, and any modifications thereof, in an officially designated electronic or paper medium that is widely disseminated and remains readily accessible to public; and (b) provide an explanation thereof to other Party, on request.
2. Part 1 Annex IV lists: (a) electronic or paper media in which each Party publishes information described in paragraph 1 this Article;
(b) electronic or paper media in which each Party publishes notices required by Article 124, Article 126(7) and Article 133(2); and (c) website address or addresses where each Party publishes its notices concerning awarded contracts pursuant to Article 133(2).
3. Each Party shall promptly notify Cooperation Committee any modification to Party's information listed in Part 1 Annex IV. The Cooperation Committee shall regularly adopt decisions ref lecting modifications to Part 1 Annex IV.
Article 124 Notices Notice Intended Procurement
1. For each covered procurement, a procuring entity shall publish a notice intended procurement in appropriate paper or electronic medium listed in Part 2 Annex IV, except in circumstances described in Article 130. Such medium shall be widely disseminated and such notices shall remain readily accessible to public, at least until expiration time period indicated in notice. The notices shall, for procuring entities covered under Parts 1, 2 or 3 Annex III, be accessible by electronic means free charge through a single point access, for at least any minimum period time specified in Part 2 Annex IV.
2. Except as otherwise provided for in this Chapter, each notice intended procurement shall include: (a) name and address procuring entity and other information necessary to contact procuring entity and obtain all relevant documents relating to procurement, and their cost and terms payment, if any;
(b) a description procurement, including nature and quantity goods or services to be procured or, where quantity is not known, estimated quantity;
(c) for recurring contracts, an estimate, if possible, timing subsequent notices intended procurement;
(d) a description any options;
(e) time frame for delivery goods or services or duration contract;
(f) procurement method that will be used and whether it will involve negotiation or electronic auction;
(g) where applicable, address and any final date for submission requests for participation in procurement;
(h) address and final date for submission tenders;
(i) language or languages in which tenders or requests for participation may be submitted, if they may be submitted in a language other than an official language Party procuring entity;
(j) a list and brief description any conditions for participation suppliers, including any requirements for specific documents or certifications to be provided by suppliers in connection therewith, unless such requirements are included in tender documentation that is made available to all interested suppliers at same time as notice intended procurement; and (k) where, pursuant to Article 126, a procuring entity intends to select a limited number qualified suppliers to be invited to tender, criteria that will be used to select them and, where applicable, any limitation on number suppliers that will be permitted to tender.
Summary Notice
3. For each case intended procurement, a procuring entity shall publish a summary notice that is readily accessible, at same time as publication notice intended procurement, in English or French. The summary notice shall contain at least following information: (a) subject matter procurement;
(b) final date for submission tenders or, where applicable, any final date for submission requests for participation in procurement or for inclusion on a multi-use list; and (c) address from which documents relating to procurement may be requested.
Notice Planned Procurement
4. Procuring entities are encouraged to publish in appropriate paper or electronic medium listed in Part 2 Annex IV, as early as possible in each fiscal year, a notice regarding their future procurement plans (hereinafter referred to as ‘notice planned procurement’). The notice planned procurement should include subject matter procurement and planned date publication notice intended procurement.
5. A procuring entity covered under Part 3 Annex III may use a notice planned procurement as a notice intended procurement provided that notice planned procurement includes as much information referred to in paragraph 2 this Article as is available to entity and a statement that interested suppliers should express their interest in procurement to procuring entity.
Article 125 Conditions for participation
1. A procuring entity shall limit any conditions for participation in a procurement to those that are essential to ensure that a supplier has legal and financial capacities and commercial and technical abilities to undertake relevant procurement.
2. In establishing conditions for participation, a procuring entity: (a) shall not impose condition that, in order for a supplier to participate in a procurement, supplier has previously been awarded one or more contracts by a procuring entity a Party;
(b) may require relevant prior experience where essential to meet requirements procurement; and (c) shall not impose condition that, in order for a supplier a Party to participate in a procurement or be awarded a contract, supplier has previously been awarded one or more contracts by a procuring entity other Party or that supplier has prior experience in territory that Party, except when prior experience is essential to meet requirements procurement.
3. In assessing whether a supplier satisfies conditions for participation, a procuring entity shall: (a) evaluate financial capacity and commercial and technical abilities a supplier on basis that supplier's business activities both inside and outside territory Party procuring entity; and (b) base its evaluation on conditions that procuring entity has specified in advance in notices or tender documentation.
4. Where there is supporting evidence, a Party, including its procuring entities, may exclude a supplier on grounds such as: (a) bankruptcy;
(b) false declarations;
(c) significant or persistent deficiencies in performance any substantive requirement or obligation under a prior contract or contracts;
(d) final judgments in respect serious crimes or other serious offences;
(e) professional misconduct or acts or omissions that adversely ref lect on commercial integrity supplier; or (f) failure to pay taxes.
Article 126 Qualif ication suppliers Registration Systems and Qualification Procedures
1. A Party, including its procuring entities, may maintain a supplier registration system under which interested suppliers are required to register and provide certain information.
2. Each Party shall ensure that: (a) its procuring entities make efforts to minimise differences in their qualification procedures; and (b) where its procuring entities maintain registration systems, entities make efforts to minimise differences in their registration systems.
3. A Party, including its procuring entities, shall not adopt or apply any registration system or qualification procedure with purpose or effect creating unnecessary obstacles to participation suppliers other Party in its procurement.
Selective Tendering
4. Where a procuring entity intends to use selective tendering, entity shall: (a) include in notice intended procurement at least information specified in Article 124(2)(a), (b), (f), (g), (j) and (k) and invite suppliers to submit a request for participation; and (b) provide, by commencement time period for tendering, at least information in Article 124(2)(c), (d), (e), (h) and (i) to qualified suppliers that it notifies as specified in Article 128(3)(b).
5. A procuring entity shall allow all qualified suppliers to participate in a particular procurement, unless procuring entity states in notice intended procurement any limitation on number suppliers that will be permitted to tender and criteria for selecting limited number suppliers.
6. Where tender documentation is not made publicly available from date publication notice referred to in paragraph 4, a procuring entity shall ensure that those documents are made available at same time to all qualified suppliers selected in accordance with paragraph 5.
Multi-Use Lists
7. A procuring entity may maintain a multi-use list suppliers, provided that a notice inviting interested suppliers to apply for inclusion on list is: (a) published annually; and (b) where published by electronic means, made available continuously, in appropriate medium listed in Part 2 Annex IV.
8. The notice provided for in paragraph 7 shall include: (a) a description goods or services, or categories thereof, for which list may be used;
(b) conditions for participation to be satisfied by suppliers for inclusion on list and methods that procuring entity will use to verify that a supplier satisfies conditions;
(c) name and address procuring entity and other information necessary to contact entity and obtain all relevant documents relating to list;
(d) period validity list and means for its renewal or termination, or where period validity is not provided, an indication method by which notice termination use list will be given;
(e) an indication that list may be used for procurement covered by this Chapter.
9. Notwithstanding paragraph 7, where a multi-use list will be valid for three years or less, a procuring entity may publish notice referred to in paragraph 7 only once, at beginning period validity list, provided that notice: (a) states period validity and that further notices will not be published; and (b) is published by electronic means and is made available continuously during period its validity.
10. A procuring entity shall allow suppliers to apply at any time for inclusion on a multi-use list and shall include on list all qualified suppliers within a reasonably short time.
11. Where a supplier that is not included on a multi-use list submits a request for participation in a procurement based on a multi-use list and all required documents, within time period provided for in Article 128(2), a procuring entity shall examine request. The procuring entity shall not exclude supplier from consideration in respect procurement on grounds that entity has insufficient time to examine request, unless, in exceptional cases, due to complexity procurement, entity is not able to complete examination request within time period allowed for submission tenders.
Entities covered under Part 3 Annex III
12. A procuring entity covered under Part 3 Annex III may use a notice inviting suppliers to apply for inclusion on a multi-use list as a notice intended procurement, provided that: (a) notice is published in accordance with paragraph 7 this Article and includes information required under paragraph 8 this Article, as much information required under Article 124(2) as is available and a statement that it constitutes a notice intended procurement or that only suppliers on multi-use list will receive further notices procurement covered by multi-use list; and (b) entity promptly provides, to suppliers that have expressed an interest in a given procurement to entity, sufficient information to permit them to assess their interest in procurement, including all remaining information required in Article 124(2), to extent such information is available.
13. A procuring entity covered under Part 3 Annex III may allow a supplier that has applied for inclusion on a multi-use list in accordance with paragraph 10 this Article to tender in a given procurement, where there is sufficient time for procuring entity to examine whether supplier satisfies conditions for participation.
Information on Procuring Entity Decisions
14. A procuring entity shall promptly inform any supplier that submits a request for participation in a procurement or application for inclusion on a multi-use list procuring entity's decision with respect to request or application.
15. Where a procuring entity rejects a supplier's request for participation in a procurement or application for inclusion on a multi-use list, ceases to recognise a supplier as qualified, or removes a supplier from a multi-use list, entity shall promptly inform supplier and, on request supplier, promptly provide supplier with a written explanation reasons for its decision.
Article 127 Technical specif ications and tender documentation Technical Specifications
1. A procuring entity shall not prepare, adopt or apply any technical specification or prescribe any conformity assessment procedure with purpose or effect creating unnecessary obstacles to international trade.
The technical specifications must allow equal access suppliers and must not have effect creating unjustified obstacles to opening procurement markets to competition.
2. In prescribing technical specifications for goods or services being procured, a procuring entity shall, where appropriate: (a) set out technical specification in terms performance and functional requirements, rather than design or descriptive characteristics; and (b) base technical specification on international standards, where such exist; otherwise, on national technical regulations, recognised national standards or building codes.
3. Where design or descriptive characteristics are used in technical specifications, a procuring entity should indicate, where appropriate, that it will consider tenders equivalent goods or services that demonstrably fulfil requirements procurement by including words such as ‘or equivalent’ in tender documentation.
4. A procuring entity shall not prescribe technical specifications that require or refer to a particular trademark or trade name, patent, copyright, design, type, specific origin, producer or supplier, unless there is no other sufficiently precise or intelligible way describing procurement requirements and provided that, in such cases, entity includes words such as ‘or equivalent’ in tender documentation.
5. A procuring entity shall not seek or accept, in a manner that would have effect precluding competition, advice that may be used in preparation or adoption any technical specification for a specific procurement from a person that may have a commercial interest in procurement.
6. For greater certainty, a Party, including its procuring entities, may, in accordance with this Article, prepare, adopt or apply technical specifications to promote conservation natural resources or protect environment.
Tender Documentation
7. A procuring entity shall make available to suppliers tender documentation that includes all information necessary to permit suppliers to prepare and submit responsive tenders. Unless already provided in notice intended procurement, such documentation shall include a complete description of: (a) procurement, including nature and quantity goods or services to be procured or, where quantity is not known, estimated quantity and any requirements to be fulfilled, including any technical specifi cations, conformity assessment certification, plans, drawings or instructional materials;
(b) any conditions for participation suppliers, including a list information and documents that suppliers are required to submit in connection with conditions for participation;
(c) all evaluation criteria entity will apply in awarding contract, and, except where price is sole criterion, relative importance such criteria;
(d) where procuring entity will conduct procurement by electronic means, any authentication and encryption requirements or other requirements related to submission information by electronic means;
(e) where procuring entity will hold an electronic auction, rules, including identification elements tender related to evaluation criteria, on which auction will be conducted;
(f) where there will be a public opening tenders, date, time and place for opening and, where appropriate, persons authorised to be present;
(g) any other terms or conditions, including terms payment and any limitation on means by which tenders may be submitted, such as whether in paper format or by electronic means; and (h) any dates for delivery goods or supply services.
8. In establishing any date for delivery goods or supply services being procured, a procuring entity shall take into account such factors as complexity procurement, extent subcontracting anticipated and realistic time required for production, de-stocking and transport goods from point supply or for supply services.
9. The evaluation criteria set out in notice intended procurement or tender documentation may include, among others, price and other cost factors, quality, technical merit, environmental characteristics and terms delivery.
10. A procuring entity shall promptly: (a) make available tender documentation to ensure that interested suppliers have sufficient time to submit responsive tenders;
(b) provide, on request, tender documentation to any interested supplier; and (c) reply to any reasonable request for relevant information by any interested or participating supplier, provided that such information does not give that supplier an advantage over other suppliers.
Modifications
11. Where, prior to award a contract, a procuring entity modifies criteria or requirements set out in notice intended procurement or tender documentation provided to participating suppliers, or amends or reissues a notice or tender documentation, it shall transmit in writing all such modifications or amended or re-issued notice or tender documentation: (a) to all suppliers that are participating at time modification, amendment or re-issuance, where such suppliers are known to entity, and in all other cases, in same manner as original information was made available; and (b) in adequate time to allow such suppliers to modify and re-submit amended tenders, as appropriate.
Article 128 Time periods General
1. A procuring entity shall, consistent with its own reasonable needs, provide sufficient time for suppliers to prepare and submit requests for participation and responsive tenders, taking into account such factors as: (a) nature and complexity procurement;
(b) extent subcontracting anticipated; and (c) time necessary for transmitting tenders by non-electronic means from foreign as well as domestic points where electronic means are not used.
Such time periods, including any extension time periods, shall be same for all interested or participating suppliers.
Deadlines
2. A procuring entity that uses selective tendering shall establish that final date for submission requests for participation shall not, in principle, be less than 25 days from date publication notice intended procurement. Where a state urgency duly substantiated by procuring entity renders this time period impracticable, time period may be reduced to not less than 10 days.
3. Except as provided for in paragraphs 4, 5, 7 and 8, a procuring entity shall establish that final date for submission tenders shall not be less than 40 days from date on which: (a) in case open tendering, notice intended procurement is published; or (b) in case selective tendering, entity notifies suppliers that they will be invited to submit tenders, whether or not it uses a multi-use list.
4. A procuring entity may reduce time period for tendering established in accordance with paragraph 3 to not less than 10 days where: (a) procuring entity has published a notice planned procurement as described in Article 124(4) at least 40 days and not more than 12 months in advance publication notice intended procurement, and notice planned procurement contains: (i) a description procurement;
(ii) approximate final dates for submission tenders or requests for participation;
(iii) a statement that interested suppliers should express their interest in procurement to procuring entity;
(iv) address from which documents relating to procurement may be obtained; and (v) as much information that is required for notice intended procurement under Article 124(2), as is available;
(b) procuring entity, for procurements a recurring nature, indicates in an initial notice intended procurement that subsequent notices will provide time periods for tendering based on this paragraph; or (c) a state urgency duly substantiated by procuring entity renders time period for tendering established in accordance with paragraph 3 impracticable.
5. A procuring entity may reduce time period for tendering established in accordance with paragraph 3 by seven days in each one following circumstances: (a) notice intended procurement is published by electronic means;
(b) all tender documentation is made available by electronic means from date publication notice intended procurement; and (c) entity accepts tenders by electronic means.
6. The use provisions paragraph 5, in conjunction with paragraph 4, shall in no case result in reduction time period for tendering established in accordance with paragraph 3 to less than seven days from date on which notice intended procurement is published.
7. Notwithstanding any other provision in this Article, where a procuring entity purchases commercial goods or services, or any combination thereof, it may reduce time period for tendering established in accordance with paragraph 3 to not less than 13 days, provided that it publishes by electronic means, at same time, both notice intended procurement and tender documentation. In addition, where entity accepts tenders for commercial goods or services by electronic means, it may reduce time period established in accordance with paragraph 3 to not less than seven days.
8. Where a procuring entity covered under Part 3 Annex III has selected all or a limited number qualified suppliers, time period for tendering may be fixed by mutual agreement between procuring entity and selected suppliers. In absence agreement, period shall not be less than seven days.
Article 129 Negotiation
1. A Party may provide for its procuring entities to conduct negotiations: (a) where entity has indicated its intent to conduct negotiations in notice intended procurement required under Article 124(2); or (b) where it appears from evaluation that no tender is obviously most advantageous in terms specific evaluation criteria set out in notice intended procurement or tender documentation.
2. A procuring entity shall: (a) ensure that any elimination suppliers participating in negotiations is carried out in accordance with evaluation criteria set out in notice intended procurement or tender documentation; and (b) where negotiations are concluded, provide a common deadline for remaining participating suppliers to submit any new or revised tenders.
Article 130 Limited tendering
1. Provided that it does not use this provision for purpose avoiding competition among suppliers or in a manner that discriminates against suppliers other Party or protects domestic suppliers, a procuring entity may use limited tendering and may choose not to apply Articles 124, 125, 126, 127 (paragraphs 7 to 11), 128, 129, 131 and 132 only under any following circumstances: (a) provided that requirements tender documentation are not substantially modified, where: (i) no tenders were submitted or no suppliers requested participation;
(ii) no tenders that conform to essential requirements tender documentation were submitted;
(iii) no suppliers satisfied conditions for participation; or (iv) tenders submitted have been collusive;
(b) where goods or services can be supplied only by a particular supplier and no reasonable alternative or substitute goods or services exist for any following reasons: (i) requirement is for a work art;
(ii) protection patents, copyrights or other exclusive rights; or (iii) due to an absence competition for technical reasons;
(c) for additional deliveries by original supplier goods or services that were not included in initial procurement where a change supplier for such additional goods or services: (i) cannot be made for economic or technical reasons such as requirements inter-changeability or interoperability with existing equipment, software, services or installations procured under initial procurement; and (ii) would cause significant inconvenience or substantial duplication costs for procuring entity;
(d) insofar as is strictly necessary where, for reasons extreme urgency brought about by events unforeseeable by procuring entity, goods or services could not be obtained in time using open tendering or selective tendering;
(e) for goods purchased on a commodity market;
(f) where a procuring entity procures a prototype or a first good or service that is developed at its request in course of, and for, a particular contract for research, experiment, study or original development. Original development a first good or service may include limited production or supply in order to incorporate results field testing and to demonstrate that good or service is suitable for production or supply in quantity to acceptable quality standards, but does not include quantity production or supply to establish commercial viability or to recover research and development costs;
(g) for purchases made under exceptionally advantageous conditions that only arise in very short term in case unusual disposals such as those arising from liquidation, receivership or bankruptcy, but not for routine purchases from regular suppliers; or (h) where a contract is awarded to a winner a design contest provided that: (i) contest has been organised in a manner that is consistent with principles this Chapter, in particular relating to publication a notice intended procurement; and (ii) participants are judged by an independent jury with a view to a design contract being awarded to a winner.
2. A procuring entity shall prepare a report in writing on each contract awarded under paragraph 1. The report shall include name procuring entity, value and kind goods or services procured and a statement indicating circumstances and conditions described in paragraph 1 that justified use limited tendering.
Article 131 Electronic auctions
1. Procuring entities may use electronic auctions.
2. In open, limited or negotiated procedures, a procuring entity may decide that award a contract shall be preceded by an electronic auction when contract specifications can be established with precision.
The electronic auction shall be based: (a) solely on prices when contract is awarded to lowest price; or (b) on prices and/or on new values features tenders indicated in specification when contract is awarded to most advantageous tender.
3. A procuring entity which decides to hold an electronic auction shall state that fact in notice intended procurement.
The specifications shall include, inter alia, following details: (a) features, values for which will be subject electronic auction, provided that such features are quantifiable and can be expressed in figures or percentages;
(b) any limits on values which may be submitted, as they result from specifications relating to subject procurement;
(c) information which will be made available to tenderers in course electronic auction and, where appropriate, when it will be made available to them;
(d) relevant information concerning electronic auction process;
(e) conditions under which tenderers will be able to bid and, in particular, minimum differences which will, where appropriate, be required when bidding;
(f) relevant information concerning electronic equipment used and arrangements and technical specifications for connection.
4. Before proceeding with an electronic auction, a procuring entity shall make a full initial evaluation tenders in accordance with award criterion or criteria set and with weighting fixed for them. All tenderers who have submitted admissible tenders shall be invited simultaneously by electronic means to submit new prices and/or new values; invitation shall contain all relevant information concerning individual connection to electronic equipment being used and shall state date and time start electronic auction. The electronic auction may take place in a number successive phases. The electronic auction may not start sooner than two working days after date on which invitations are sent out.
5. When contract is to be awarded on basis most advantageous tender, invitation shall be accompanied by outcome a full evaluation relevant tenderer. The invitation shall also state mathematical formula to be used in electronic auction to determine automatic re-rankings on basis new prices and/or new values submitted. That formula shall incorporate weighting all criteria fixed to determine most advantageous tender, as indicated in contract notice or in specifications; for that purpose, any ranges shall, however, be reduced beforehand to a specified value.
6. Throughout each phase an electronic auction contracting authorities shall instantaneously communicate to all tenderers at least sufficient information to enable them to ascertain their relative rankings at any moment. They may also communicate other information concerning other prices or values submitted, provided that that is stated in specifications. They may also at any time announce number participants in that phase auction. In no case, however, may they disclose identities tenderers during any phase an electronic auction.
7. A procuring entity shall close an electronic auction in one or more following manners: (a) by indicating in invitation to take part in auction date and time fixed in advance;
(b) when it receives no more new prices or new values which meet requirements concerning minimum differences, by stating in invitation to take part in auction time which it will allow to elapse after receiving last submission before it closes electronic auction;
(c) when number phases in auction, fixed in invitation to take part in auction, has been completed.
8. When procuring entity has decided to close an electronic auction in accordance with point (c) paragraph 7, possibly in combination with arrangements laid down in point (b) that paragraph, invitation to take part in auction shall indicate timetable for each phase auction.
9. After closing an electronic auction a procuring entity shall award contract in accordance with Article 132 on basis results electronic auction.
10. Procuring entities may not have improper recourse to electronic auctions nor may they use them in such a way as to prevent, restrict or distort competition or to change subject matter contract, as put up for tender in published notice intended procurement and defined in specification.
Article 132 Treatment tenders and awarding contracts Treatment Tenders
1. A procuring entity shall receive, open and treat all tenders under procedures that guarantee fairness and impartiality procurement process, and confidentiality tenders.
2. A procuring entity shall not penalise any supplier whose tender is received after time specified for receiving tenders if delay is due solely to mishandling on part procuring entity.
3. Where a procuring entity provides a supplier with an opportunity to correct unintentional errors form between opening tenders and awarding contract, procuring entity shall provide same opportunity to all participating suppliers.
Awarding Contracts
4. To be considered for an award, a tender shall be submitted in writing and shall, at time opening, comply with essential requirements set out in notices and tender documentation and be from a supplier that satisfies conditions for participation.
5. Unless a procuring entity determines that it is not in public interest to award a contract, entity shall award contract to supplier that entity has determined to be capable fulfilling terms contract and that, based solely on evaluation criteria specified in notices and tender documentation, has submitted: (a) most advantageous tender; or (b) where price is sole criterion, lowest price.
6. Where a procuring entity receives a tender with a price that is abnormally lower than prices in other tenders submitted, it may verify with supplier that it satisfies conditions for participation and is capable fulfilling terms contract.
7. A procuring entity shall not use options, cancel a procurement or modify awarded contracts in a manner that circumvents obligations under this Chapter.
Article 133 Transparency procurement information Information Provided to Suppliers
1. A procuring entity shall promptly inform participating suppliers entity's contract award decisions and, at request a supplier, shall do so in writing. Subject to Article 134(2) and (3), a procuring entity shall, on request, provide an unsuccessful supplier with an explanation reasons why entity did not select its tender and relative advantages successful supplier's tender.
Publication Award Information
