2. No later than 72 days after award each contract covered by this Chapter, a procuring entity shall publish a notice in appropriate paper or electronic medium listed in Part 2 Annex IV. Where entity publishes notice only in an electronic medium, information shall remain readily accessible for a reasonable period time. The notice shall include at least following information: (a) a description goods or services procured;
(b) name and address procuring entity;
(c) name and address successful supplier;
(d) value successful tender or highest and lowest offers taken into account in award contract;
(e) date award; and (f) type procurement method used, and in cases where limited tendering was used in accordance with Article 130, a description circumstances justifying use limited tendering.
Maintenance Documentation, Reports and Electronic Traceability
3. Each procuring entity shall, for a period at least three years from date it awards a contract, maintain: (a) documentation and reports tendering procedures and contract awards relating to covered procurement, including reports required under Article 130; and (b) data that ensure appropriate traceability conduct covered procurement by electronic means.
Article 134 Disclosure information Provision Information to Parties
1. On request other Party, a Party shall provide promptly any information necessary to determine whether a procurement was conducted fairly, impartially and in accordance with this Chapter, including information on charac teristics and relative advantages successful tender. In cases where release information would prejudice competition in future tenders, Party that receives information shall not disclose it to any supplier, except after consulting with, and obtaining agreement of, Party that provided information.
Non-Disclosure Information
2. Notwithstanding any other provision this Chapter, a Party, including its procuring entities, shall not provide to any particular supplier information that might prejudice fair competition between suppliers.
3. Nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as requiring a Party, including its procuring entities, authorities and review bodies, to disclose confidential information where disclosure: (a) would impede law enforcement;
(b) might prejudice fair competition between suppliers;
(c) would prejudice legitimate commercial interests particular persons, including protection intellectual property; or (d) would otherwise be contrary to public interest.
Article 135 Domestic review procedures
1. Each Party shall provide a timely, effective, transparent and non-discriminatory administrative or judicial review procedure through which a supplier may, in context a covered procurement, in which that supplier has, or has had, an interest, challenge: (a) a breach this Chapter; or (b) where supplier does not have a right to challenge directly a breach this Chapter under domestic law a Party, a failure to comply with a Party's measures implementing this Chapter.
2. The procedural rules for all challenges pursuant to paragraph 1 shall be in writing and made generally available.
3. In event a complaint by a supplier, arising in context covered procurement in which supplier has, or has had, an interest, that there has been a breach or a failure as referred to in paragraph 1, Party procuring entity conducting procurement shall encourage entity and supplier to seek resolution complaint through consultations. The entity shall accord impartial and timely consideration to any such complaint in a manner that is not prejudicial to supplier's participation in ongoing or future procurement or its right to seek corrective measures under administrative or judicial review procedure.
4. Each supplier shall be allowed a sufficient period time to prepare and submit a challenge, which shall in no case be less than 10 days from time when basis challenge became known or reasonably should have become known to supplier.
5. Each Party shall establish or designate at least one impartial administrative or judicial authority that is independent its procuring entities to receive and review a challenge by a supplier arising in context a covered procurement.
6. Where a body other than an authority referred to in paragraph 5 initially reviews a challenge, Party shall ensure that supplier may appeal initial decision to an impartial administrative or judicial authority that is independent procuring entity whose procurement is subject challenge.
7. Each Party shall ensure that a review body that is not a court shall have its decision subject to judicial review or have procedures that provide that: (a) procuring entity shall respond in writing to challenge and disclose all relevant documents to review body;
(b) participants to proceedings (hereinafter referred to as ‘participants’) shall have right to be heard prior to a decision review body being made on challenge;
(c) participants shall have right to be represented and accompanied;
(d) participants shall have access to all proceedings;
(e) participants shall have right to request that proceedings take place in public and that witnesses may be presented; and (f) review body shall make its decisions or recommendations in a timely fashion, in writing, and shall include an explanation basis for each decision or recommendation.
8. Each Party shall adopt or maintain procedures that provide for: (a) rapid interim measures to preserve supplier's opportunity to participate in procurement; and (b) where a review body has determined that there has been a breach or a failure as referred to in paragraph 1, corrective action or compensation for loss or damages suffered, which may be limited to either costs for preparation tender or costs relating to challenge, or both.
9. The rapid interim measures referred to in point (a) paragraph 8 may result in suspension procurement process. The procedures referred to in paragraph 8 may provide that overriding adverse consequences for interests concerned, including public interest, may be taken into account when deciding whether such measures should be applied. Just cause for not acting shall be provided in writing.
Article 136 Modifications and rectif ications to coverage
1. A Party may propose a modification or rectification elements in Annex III which relate to that Party.
Modifications
2. When a Party proposes a modification, that Party shall: (a) notify other Party in writing; and (b) include in notification a proposal for appropriate compensatory adjustments to other Party to maintain a level coverage comparable to that existing prior to modification.
3. Notwithstanding point (b) paragraph 2, a Party need not provide compensatory adjustments if: (a) modification in question is negligible in its effect; or (b) modification covers an entity over which Party has effectively eliminated its control or inf luence.
4. The other Party shall be deemed to have accepted modification, including for purposes Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) this Title, unless it objects in writing within 45 days receipt notification referred to in point (a) paragraph 2 that: (a) an adjustment proposed under point (b) paragraph 2 is adequate to maintain a comparable level mutually agreed coverage;
(b) modification is negligible in its effect under point (a) paragraph 3; or (c) modification covers an entity over which Party has effectively eliminated its control or inf luence under point (b) paragraph 3.
Rectifications
5. The following changes to Parts 1 to 3 Annex III shall be considered a rectification, provided that they do not affect mutually agreed coverage provided for in this Chapter: (a) a change in name an entity;
(b) a merger two or more entities listed within same Part Annex III; and (c) separation an entity into two or more entities, provided that all new entities are added to same Part Annex III as original entity.
6. The Party proposing a rectification shall notify other Party every two years, following date on which this Title starts to apply ( ).
7. A Party may notify other Party an objection to a proposed rectification within 45 days from having received notification referred to in paragraph 6. Where a Party submits an objection, it shall set out reasons why it believes proposed rectification is not a change provided for in paragraph 5, and describe effect proposed rectification on mutually agreed coverage provided for in this Agreement. If no such objection is submitted in writing within 45 days after having received notification, Party shall be deemed to have agreed to proposed rectification.
The Cooperation Committee
8. If no objection is submitted regarding a proposed modification or rectification within period provided for in paragraphs 4 and 7, Cooperation Committee shall amend Annex III to ref lect any such modification or rectification.
The modification or rectification shall be effective from day following date expiry period referred to in paragraphs 4 and 7.
9. If an objection to a proposed modification or rectification has been submitted, Cooperation Committee shall discuss matter. The Cooperation Committee may decide to approve a modification or rectification and amend Annex III accordingly.
Article 137 Transitional period This Chapter shall start to apply five years after date on which this Title starts to apply. For goods listed in Part 4 Annex III and for services covered under Part 6 Annex III, this Chapter shall start to apply eight years after date on which this Title starts to apply.
Raw materials and energy Article 138 Definitions For purposes this Chapter: (a) ‘raw materials’ means substances used in manufacture industrial products, excluding energy goods, processed fishery products or agricultural products, but including natural rubber, raw hides and skins, wood and wood pulp, silk, wool, cotton and other vegetable textile inputs;
(b) ‘energy goods’ means, based on Harmonised Commodity Description and Coding System World Customs Organisation (HS) and Combined Nomenclature Union, natural gas, liquefied natural gas, liquefied petroleum gas (LPG) (HS 27.11), electricity (HS 27.16), crude oil and oil products (HS 27.09-27.10 and 27.13-27.15) and coal and other solid fuels (HS 27.01-27.04);
(c) ‘partnership’ means any legal entity which is a commercial organisation under jurisdiction or control either Party such as, and not limited to, a corporation, trust, partnership, joint venture or association;
(d) ‘service supplier’ means a service supplier as defined in point (q) Article 40;
(e) ‘measure’ means a measure as defined in point (a) Article 40;
(f) ‘transport’ means transmission and distribution energy goods through transmission pipelines for oil and oil products and high-pressure natural gas, high-voltage electricity transmission grids and lines, railways, roadways and other facilities handling transport energy goods;
(g) ‘unauthorised taking’ means any activity consisting unlawful taking energy goods from transmission pipelines for oil and oil products and high-pressure natural gas, high-voltage electricity transmission grids and lines, railways, roadways and other facilities handling transport energy goods;
(h) ‘emergency situation’ means a situation causing a significant disruption or a physical interruption supply natural gas, oil or electric energy between and Republic Kazakhstan, including supply transiting through third countries or a situation an exceptionally high demand for energy goods within or Republic Kazakhstan in which market measures are not sufficient and non-market measures have to be additionally introduced;
(i) ‘local content requirement’ means: (i) with respect to goods, a requirement for an enterprise to purchase or use goods domestic origin or from a domestic source, whether specified in terms particular products, in terms volume or value products, or in terms a proportion volume or value its local production;
(ii) with respect to services, a requirement which restricts choice service supplier or service supplied to detriment services or service suppliers other Party;
(j) ‘state owned enterprise’ means any enterprise involved in a commercial activity over which a Party at central or subcentral level directly or indirectly owns more than 50 % enterprise's subscribed capital or votes attached to shares issued by enterprise;
(k) ‘juridical person’ means a juridical person as defined in point (d) Article 40;
(l) ‘juridical person a Party’ means a juridical person a Party as defined in point (e) Article 40.
Article 139 Price regulation
1. The Parties aim for price supply raw materials or energy goods for industrial users, if regulated by government a Party, to recover costs and provide for reasonable profit.
2. If price raw materials or energy goods sold on domestic market differs from export price same product, exporting Party shall, upon request other Party, provide information on such difference, excluding transportation costs and export taxes.
Article 140 Trading and export monopolies The Parties shall not maintain or establish a trading or export monopoly for raw materials or energy goods except when a Party exercises its priority (pre-emption) right to purchase raw and dry gas and gold.
Article 141 Access and rights to prospect, explore and produce hydrocarbons (crude oil and natural gas)
1. Nothing in this Agreement shall affect full sovereignty Parties in accordance with international law over hydrocarbon resources located in their territory and their inland, archipelagic and territorial waters, as well as sovereign rights for purposes exploring for and exploiting hydrocarbon resources located in their Exclusive Economic Zones and continental shelf.
2. The Parties retain right to determine areas within their territory, inland, archipelagic and territorial waters, Exclusive Economic Zones and continental shelf to be made available for exercise activities prospecting, exploring for and producing hydrocarbons.
3. Whenever a sovereign decision a Party, as described in paragraph 2, is taken, each Party shall ensure that enterprises other Party are not discriminated against as regards access and exercise rights to prospect, explore for and produce hydrocarbons, provided enterprise in question is established as a juridical person in territory host Party granting access.
4. Each Party may require an enterprise which has been granted an authorisation for exercise activities prospecting, exploring for and producing hydrocarbons, to pay a financial contribution or a contribution in hydrocarbons.
5. The Parties shall take necessary measures to ensure that licences or other authorisations, through which an enterprise is entitled to exercise rights to prospect, explore for and produce hydrocarbons, are granted following a published procedure or an invitation to potentially interested applicants Parties to submit applications by means a notice. The notice shall specify type licence or other authorisation, relevant geographical area and proposed date or time limit for granting a licence or other forms authorisation.
6. Paragraphs 3 to 5 are without prejudice to right a state-owned enterprise to obtain access and rights to prospect, explore for and produce hydrocarbons by means direct negotiations with its Party. When such a stateowned enterprise decides to transfer fully or partially its right to prospect, explore for and produce hydrocarbons, obligations provided for in paragraphs 3 and 5 shall apply.
7. Article 53 shall apply to licensing conditions and licensing authorisation procedure.
Article 142 Conditions for investment in raw materials and energy goods In order to foster investment in activities prospecting, exploring for, extracting and mining raw materials and energy goods, neither Party shall: (a) maintain or adopt measures providing for local content requirements affecting other Party's products, service suppliers, investors or investments unless otherwise provided for in Protocol on Accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO and GATS schedules specific commitments and its Member States;
(b) maintain or adopt measures whereby an enterprise from other Party is obliged to transfer or to share intellectual property rights in order to sell products or services or to invest on that Party's territory. The Parties are not precluded from negotiating contracts with investors seeking rights to prospect, explore for, extract and mine raw materials and energy goods for such transfers on a voluntary basis, provided that they are made under market conditions and at a market price.
Article 143 Transit
1. The Parties shall take all necessary measures to facilitate transit energy goods, consistent with principle freedom transit, and in accordance with Article 7(1) and (3) Energy Charter Treaty.
2. Each Party shall prohibit unauthorized taking raw materials and energy goods transited or transported through its territory by any entity subject to its control or jurisdiction and shall take all appropriate measures to address such unauthorised taking.
Article 144 Interruption
1. Each Party shall take all possible measures to ensure that operators main-line energy transit or transport pipelines and grids: (a) minimise risk accidental interruption, reduction or stoppage transit and/or transport;
(b) expeditiously restore normal operation such transit or transport, which has been accidentally interrupted, reduced or stopped.
2. A Party through territory which energy goods are in transit or transport, or in territory which they are in receipt and storage as part transport/transit route, shall not, in event a dispute over any matter involving Parties or one or more entities subject to control or jurisdiction a Party, interrupt or reduce, or permit any entity subject to its control or jurisdiction to interrupt or reduce, existing transit, transport, receipt and storage as part transport/transit route energy goods, except where this is specifically provided for in a contract or other agreement governing such transit, transport, receipt and storage as part transport/transit route, prior to conclusion a dispute resolution procedure under relevant contract or dispute settlement procedure set out in Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) this Title concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138.
3. A Party shall not be held liable for an interruption or reduction pursuant to this Article in force majeure situations or where that Party is unable to supply energy goods or ensure their transit as a result actions attributable to a third country or an entity under control or jurisdiction a third country.
Article 145 Access to high-voltage electric energy transmission grids and lines
1. Each Party shall provide enterprises other Party, established as juridical persons in territory Party granting access, with non-discriminatory access to high-voltage electric energy transmission grids and lines, which are partly or fully owned and regulated by Party granting access within available capacities such grids and lines. The access shall be allocated in a fair and equitable manner.
2. When applying measures relating to such transmission grids and lines, Party shall ensure that following principles are respected: (a) all legal and regulatory measures on access and transportation tariffs are fully transparent;
(b) measures do not discriminate with respect to origin electricity production within its territory and with respect to destination electricity; and (c) non-discriminatory transportation tariffs with respect to enterprises and Republic Kazakhstan are applied.
Article 146 Regulatory authorities for electricity and gas
1. Each Party shall designate and empower regulatory authorities to regulate markets for electricity and gas on its territory. Those regulatory authorities shall be legally distinct and functionally independent from any other public authorities or market participants.
2. The decisions and procedures used by a regulatory authority shall be impartial with respect to all market participants.
3. A market participant affected by any decision a regulatory authority shall have right to appeal against that decision to an appeal body. If appeal body is not independent parties involved or is not judicial in character, decisions appeal body shall be subject to a review by an impartial and independent judicial authority. The decisions appeal body and judicial authority shall set out reasons therefor and shall be in writing. The Parties shall ensure that final decision appeal body or judicial authority, whichever is final instance, is enforced effectively.
Article 147 Renewable energy sector
1. This Article shall apply to measures which may affect trade and investment between Parties related to generation energy from renewable non-fossil sources, inter alia, wind, solar power and hydropower, but not to products from which such energy is generated.
2. Each Party shall: (a) refrain from maintaining or adopting measures requiring formation partnerships with local companies, unless such partnerships are deemed necessary for technical reasons and Party maintaining or adopting such measures can demonstrate such technical reasons upon request by other Party;
(b) ensure that any rules concerning authorisation, certification and licensing procedures, if applicable, in particular with regard to equipment, plants and associated transmission network infrastructures, are objective, transparent and non-arbitrary and do not discriminate against applicants from other Party;
(c) ensure that administrative charges in renewable energy sector, such as those paid by consumers, planners, architects, builders, equipment installers and suppliers, are transparent and limited in amount to approximate cost services rendered;
(d) ensure that importation and use goods originating in other Party, or provision goods by other Party's suppliers, are subject to provisions Chapter 1 (Trade in Goods) this Title;
(e) ensure that provision services by suppliers other Party are subject to Article 53;
(f) ensure that terms, conditions and procedures for connection and access to electric energy transmission grids are transparent and do not discriminate against suppliers other Party or against electric energy from renewable sources. The Parties shall ensure that appropriate grid and market-related measures are taken to minimise curtailment (limitations) electric energy produced from renewable energy sources;
(g) refrain from imposing or maintaining a requirement for: (i) an enterprise other Party to purchase or use products domestic origin or from any domestic source Party imposing requirement, whether specified in terms particular products, in terms volume or value products, or in terms a proportion volume or value its local production; or (ii) enterprise's purchases or use imported products to be limited to amount related to volume or value local products that it exports.
3. Where international or regional standards exist with respect to equipment and systems for generation energy from renewable and non-fossil sources, Parties shall use those standards, or their relevant parts, as a basis for their technical regulations except when such international standards or relevant parts would be an ineffective or inappropriate means for fulfilment legitimate objectives pursued. For purposes applying this paragraph, International Organization for Standardization (ISO) and International Electrotechnical Commission (IEC) shall be considered relevant international standard-setting bodies.
4. Where appropriate, Parties shall specify technical regulations based on product requirements in terms performance, including environmental performance, rather than on product design or description.
5. Nothing in this Article shall be construed as preventing adoption or enforcement by either Party measures necessary for safe operation energy networks concerned, or safety energy supply, subject to requirement that such measures are not applied in a manner which would constitute a means arbitrary or unjustifiable discrimination between products, service suppliers or investors Parties where same conditions prevail, or a disguised restriction on trade and investment between Parties.
Article 148 Cooperation in raw materials and energy goods
1. Without prejudice to Articles 204 to 208, Parties agree to strengthen cooperation and promotion mutual understanding between them in field trade in raw materials and energy goods.
2. The Parties recognise that respecting principles transparency and non-discrimination and ensuring that rules are not trade distortive is best way to create an environment favourable to foreign direct investment in production and trade raw materials and energy goods. More generally, such an environment fosters efficient allocation and efficient use raw materials and energy goods.
3. Cooperation and promotion mutual understanding cover bilateral trade issues as well as issues common interest stemming from international trade. Such issues include trade distortions impacting global markets, environment and development issues specifically linked to trade in raw materials and energy goods as well as corporate social re sponsibility in accordance with internationally recognized standards such as OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises and OECD Due Diligence Guidance for Responsible Supply Chains Minerals from Conf lict-Affected and High-Risk Areas. Cooperation and promotion mutual understanding include exchange data and information on regulatory framework raw materials and energy sectors. This shall not be construed as requiring Parties to furnish any information, disclosure which they consider contrary to their respective security interests.
4. Either Party may request organisation an ad hoc meeting related to raw materials and energy goods or an ad hoc session on raw materials and energy goods during Cooperation Committee meetings. The bilateral cooperation could additionally, if appropriate, be extended to relevant plurilateral or multilateral fora in which both Parties participate.
Article 149 Early warning mechanism
1. The Parties establish an early warning mechanism to adopt practical measures aimed at preventing and rapidly reacting to an emergency situation or to a threat thereof.
2. The Parties shall jointly undertake actions for: (a) an early evaluation potential risks and problems related to supply and demand natural gas, oil or electric energy; and (b) prevention or rapid reaction in case an emergency situation or a threat thereof.
3. If either Party becomes aware an emergency situation or a situation which in its opinion could lead to an emergency situation, that Party shall notify other Party within shortest possible time.
4. For purposes this Article, Parties agree that responsible bodies are Member Commission in charge energy and Minister Republic Kazakhstan in charge respective energy matters.
5. Upon notification, Parties shall provide each other with an assessment situation.
6. Either Party may request consultations within three calendar days notification to: (a) elaborate a common evaluation situation;
(b) develop recommendations to eliminate emergency situation and to minimise impact emergency situation;
(c) create a Special Monitoring Group to, inter alia, monitor energy f lows at relevant points concerned infrastructure.
7. The Parties shall, if appropriate, cooperate with third countries to eliminate threat an emergency situation or to overcome emergency situation.
8. In case emergency situation persists, either Party may start an emergency dispute settlement procedure, in accordance with special mechanism provided for in Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) this Title.
9. The Parties shall refrain from any actions that may deepen or reinforce emergency situation, as appropriate in a given situation, from moment notification.
10. A Party shall not rely on, or introduce as evidence in dispute settlement procedures under this Agreement, following: (a) positions taken or proposals made by other Party in course a procedure under this Article; or (b) any indication from other Party its willingness to accept a solution to emergency situation referred to in this Article.
11. The Cooperation Committee may develop, as necessary, detailed implementing provisions for application this Article.
Article 150 Exceptions
1. This Chapter is without prejudice to any exceptions, reservations or restrictions provided for in this Agreement.
2. This Chapter shall not apply to research and development projects or to demonstration projects carried out on a non-commercial scale.
3. Nothing in this Chapter shall be construed as preventing adoption or enforcement by either Party measures necessary for safe operation energy infrastructure, including energy transport and production facilities concerned, in interest national security or public safety, including prevention and reaction to an emergency situation, subject to requirement that such measures are not applied in a manner which would constitute a means arbitrary or unjustifiable discrimination between products, service suppliers or investors Parties where same conditions prevail, or a disguised restriction on trade and investment between Parties.
Trade and sustainable development Article 151 Context and objectives
1. The Parties recall Agenda 21 United Nations Conference on Environment and Development 1992, International Labour Organisation's (ILO) Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work 1998, Johannesburg Plan Implementation on Sustainable Development 2002, Ministerial Declaration UN Economic and Social Council on Generating Full and Productive Employment and Decent Work for All 2006, ILO Declaration on Social Justice for a Fair Globalisation 2008 and Outcome Document UN Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, incorporated in Resolution 66/288 adopted by UN General Assembly on 27 July 2012, entitled ‘The Future We Want’.
2. The Parties reaffirm their commitment to promote development international trade in such a way as to contribute to objective sustainable development, for welfare present and future generations. The Parties shall strive to ensure that this objective is integrated and ref lected at every level their trade relationship.
Article 152 Multilateral environmental and labour standards and agreements
1. The Parties recognise value international environmental governance and agreements as a response in ternational community to global or regional environmental problems.
2. The Parties recognise that full and productive employment and decent work for all are key elements sustainable development for all countries and a priority objective international cooperation.
3. In that context, Parties reaffirm their commitment to effectively implement in their law and practice multilateral environmental agreements they are a party to and ILO conventions ratified by Member States and Republic Kazakhstan, respectively.
Article 153 Right to regulate and levels protection
1. The Parties recognise right each Party to establish its own levels domestic environmental and labour protection, and to adopt or modify its relevant law and policy accordingly, consistently with internationally recognised standards and agreements referred to in Article 152. The Parties shall aim at high levels environmental and labour protection.
2. The Parties recognise that it is inappropriate to encourage trade or investment by weakening or reducing levels protection afforded in domestic environmental or labour law.
3. A Party shall not derogate from or, through a sustained or recurring course action or inaction, fail to effectively enforce its environmental and labour law, in order to encourage trade or investment.
Article 154 Trade and investment promoting sustainable development
1. The Parties reaffirm their commitment to enhance contribution trade to goal sustainable development in its economic, social and environmental dimensions. Accordingly, they agree to promote: (a) trade and investment in environmental goods and services and in climate-friendly products and technologies;
(b) use sustainability assurance schemes, such as fair and ethical trade or eco-labelling; and (c) corporate social responsibility practices.
2. The Parties shall exchange information on, and share experience in, their actions to promote coherence and mutual supportiveness between trade, social and environmental objectives. Furthermore, Parties shall enhance their cooperation and dialogue on sustainable development issues that may arise in context their trade relations including on relevant aspects set out in Title IV (Cooperation in Area Economic and Sustainable Development).
3. The cooperation and dialogue referred to in paragraph 2 this Article shall involve relevant stakeholders, in particular social partners, as well as other civil society organisations, through civil society cooperation established under Article 251.
4. The Cooperation Committee may adopt rules for such cooperation and dialogue.
Article 155 Dispute Settlement Subsection 2 Section 3 Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) this Title does not apply to disputes under this Chapter.
For any such dispute, after arbitration panel has delivered its final report pursuant to Articles 180 and 182, Parties, taking report into account, shall discuss suitable measures to be implemented. The Cooperation Committee shall monitor implementation any such measures and shall keep matter under review, including through mechanism referred to in Article 154(3).
Competition Article 156 Principles The Parties recognise importance free and undistorted competition in their trade relations. The Parties acknowledge that anti-competitive business practices and state interventions, including subsidies, have potential to distort proper functioning markets and undermine benefits trade liberalisation.
Article 157 Antitrust and mergers legislation and its implementation
1. Each Party shall maintain in its territory comprehensive competition law which effectively addresses anticompetitive agreements, concerted practices and anti-competitive unilateral conduct enterprises with dominant market power, and which provides effective control concentrations.
2. Each Party shall maintain operationally independent authorities responsible and appropriately equipped for effective enforcement competition law as referred to in paragraph 1.
3. The Parties recognise importance applying their respective competition law in a transparent and non-dis criminatory manner, respecting principles procedural fairness and rights defence enterprises concerned.
Article 158 State monopolies, state enterprises and enterprises entrusted with special or exclusive rights or privileges
1. Nothing in this Chapter prevents a Party from designating or maintaining state monopolies or state enterprises, or from entrusting enterprises with special or exclusive rights or privileges according to its law.
2. With regard to state monopolies, state enterprises and enterprises entrusted with special or exclusive rights or privileges involved in economic activities, each Party shall ensure that such enterprises are subject to competition law as referred to in Article 157. For purposes this Chapter, an economic activity consists in offering goods and services on a market. It does not include activities performed in exercise governmental authority, namely activities carried out neither on a commercial basis nor in competition with one or more economic operators.
3. The application competition law as referred to in Article 157 should not obstruct performance, in law or in fact, particular tasks public interest assigned to enterprises in question. Exceptions should be limited and transparent. Trade and investment should not be affected to such an extent that would undermine objective this Agreement.
Article 159 Subsidies
1. For purposes this Article, a ‘subsidy’ is a measure which fulfils conditions Article 1 SCM Agreement, irrespective whether it is granted to an enterprise for production goods or for supply services, and which is specific within meaning Article 2 that Agreement.
2. Each Party shall ensure transparency in area subsidies. To that end, each Party shall report every two years from date application this Title to other Party on legal basis, including policy objective or purpose subsidy, duration or any other time limits, form and, where possible, amount or budget and recipient subsidy granted by its government or a public body. Such report is deemed to have been provided if relevant information is made available on a publicly accessible website or through WTO notification mechanism.
3. If a Party considers that a subsidy granted by other Party is negatively affecting first Party's interests, first Party may request consultations on matter. The requested Party shall accord due consideration to such a request.
The consultations should, in particular, aim at specifying policy objective subsidy, whether subsidy has an incentive effect and is proportionate, and any measures taken to limit potential distortive effect on trade and investment requesting Party ( ).
4. In order to facilitate consultations, requested Party shall provide information on subsidy in question within a period no longer than 90 days from date receipt request. If requesting Party, after receiving information on subsidy in question, considers that subsidy in question negatively affects or may negatively affect requesting Party's trade or investment interests in a disproportionate manner, requested Party shall use its best endeavours to address negative effects on requesting Party's trade or investment interests caused by subsidy in question.
5. Paragraphs 3 and 4 shall not apply to subsidies relating to fisheries and trade in goods covered by Annex 1 to WTO Agreement on Agriculture.
Article 160 Dispute settlement The provisions Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) this Title shall not apply with respect to Articles 156 to 158 and Article 159(3) and (4).
Article 161 Relationship with WTO The provisions this Chapter are without prejudice to rights and obligations a Party under WTO Agreement, in particular SCM Agreement and Understanding on Rules and Procedures Governing Settlement Disputes.
Article 162 Confidentiality When exchanging information under this Chapter, Parties shall take into account limitations imposed by requirements professional and business secrecy.
State-owned enterprises, state-controlled enterprises and enterprises granted special or exclusive rights or privileges Article 163 Definitions For purposes this Chapter: (a) ‘state-owned enterprise’ means any enterprise involved in a commercial activity over which a Party at central or subcentral level owns more than 50 % enterprise's subscribed capital or votes attached to shares issued by enterprise;
(b) ‘state-controlled enterprise’ means any enterprise involved in a commercial activity over which a Party at central or sub-central level exercises or has possibility exercising decisive inf luence, directly or indirectly, by virtue its financial participation therein or by rules or practices on its functioning, or by any other means relevant to establish such decisive inf luence. Decisive inf luence on part a Party shall be presumed when a Party, directly or indirectly, can appoint more than half members enterprise's administrative, managerial or supervisory body;
(c) ‘enterprise granted special or exclusive rights or privileges’ means any enterprise, public or private, involved in a commercial activity, that has been granted by a Party, at central or sub-central level, in law or in fact, special or exclusive rights or privileges. Such rights or privileges may include right to act as a distributor, a network provider or another intermediary for purchase or sale a good or for provision or receipt a service.
‘Enterprises granted special or exclusive rights or privileges’ covers monopolies involved in a commercial activity;
(d) a ‘monopoly’ means an entity involved in a commercial activity, including a consortium, that in a relevant market in territory a Party is designated at central or sub-central level as sole supplier or purchaser a good or service, but does not include an entity that has been granted an exclusive intellectual property right solely by reason such grant;
(e) ‘special rights’ means rights granted by a Party at central or sub-central level to a limited number enterprises within a given geographical area or a product or service market effect which is to substantially limit ability any other enterprise to carry out its activity in same geographical area under substantially equivalent conditions. The granting a licence or a permit to a limited number enterprises in allocating a scarce resource through objective, proportional and non-discriminatory criteria is not in and itself a special right;
(f) ‘non-discriminatory treatment’ means national treatment or most-favoured-nation treatment as set out in this Agreement, whichever is better;
(g) ‘in accordance with commercial considerations’ means consistent with customary business practices a privately held enterprise operating according to market economy principles in international trade;
(h) ‘designate’ means to establish or authorise a monopoly, or to expand scope a monopoly, whether in law or in fact.
Article 164 Scope
1. The Parties confirm their rights and obligations under paragraphs 1 to 3 Article XVII GATT 1994, Understanding on Interpretation Article XVII GATT 1994, as well as under paragraphs 1, 2 and 5 Article VIII GATS and Chapter on State-owned and State-controlled Enterprises and Enterprises with Special and Exclusive Privileges Protocol on Accession Republic Kazakhstan to WTO, which are hereby incorporated into and made part this Agreement and shall apply.
2. This Chapter does not apply to covered procurement by a Party or its procuring entities within meaning Article 120.
3. This Chapter shall apply to all economic activities covered by this Agreement. Services which are not listed in a Party's GATS schedule specific commitments shall not be subject to provisions Articles 166 and 167.
Article 165
1. Without prejudice to Parties' rights and obligations under this Chapter, nothing in this Chapter prevents Parties from establishing or maintaining state-owned or state-controlled enterprises or designating or maintaining monopolies or from granting enterprises special or exclusive rights or privileges.
2. Where an enterprise falls within scope application this Chapter, Parties shall not require or encourage such an enterprise to act in a manner inconsistent with this Agreement.
Article 166 Non-discrimination Unless otherwise provided for in Article 142 or in a Party's GATS schedule specific commitments or in a Party's reservations to national treatment set out in Annex I, each Party shall ensure in its territory that any enterprise satisfying conditions set out in points (c) and (d) Article 163 in its purchase or sale a good or a service accords non-dis criminatory treatment to a good other Party and/or to a service or a service supplier other Party.
Article 167 Commercial considerations Except to fulfil purpose, such as a public service obligation, for which special or exclusive rights or privileges have been granted, or in case a state-owned or state-controlled enterprise to fulfil its public mandate, and provided that enterprise's conduct in fulfilling that purpose or mandate is consistent with provisions Article 166 and Chapter 11 (Competition) this Title, each Party shall ensure that any enterprise referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 acts in accordance with commercial considerations in relevant territory in its purchases and sales goods, including with regard to price, quality, availability, marketability, transportation and other terms and conditions purchase or sale, as well as in its purchases or supply services, including when those goods or services are supplied to or by an investment an investor other Party.
Article 168 Pricing Charging different prices in different markets, or within same market, where such differences are based on normal commercial considerations, such as supply and demand conditions, is not in itself inconsistent with Articles 166 and 167.
Article 169 Corporate governance
1. The Parties shall ensure that enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 observe high standards transparency and corporate governance in accordance with 2005 OECD Guidelines on Corporate Governance State-Owned Enterprises. Further development policy corporate governance in enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 should be conducted in accordance with those Guidelines.
2. Each Party shall ensure that any regulatory body responsible for regulating enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 is legally distinct and functionally independent from, and not accountable to, any enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163.
3. Each Party shall ensure enforcement laws and regulations in a consistent and non-discriminatory manner at all levels government, be it central or local, and including on enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163.
Exemptions shall be limited and transparent.
Article 170 Exchange information
1. A Party which has a reason to believe that its interests under this Agreement are being adversely affected by operations an enterprise or enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 other Party, may request that latter Party to supply information about operations its enterprise relating to carrying out provisions this Agreement. Such information may include organisational, corporate and financial information.
2. Each Party shall, at request other Party, make available information concerning specific enterprises referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163 which do not qualify as small and medium-sized enterprises as defined in law requested Party. Requests for such information shall indicate enterprise, products or services and markets concerned, and include indications that enterprise is engaging in practices that hinder trade or investment between Parties.
3. Each Party shall, at request other Party, make available information concerning exemptions, nonconforming measures, immunities and any other measures, including more favourable treatment, applicable in territory requested Party to any enterprise referred to in points (a) to (d) Article 163.
4. Paragraphs 1 to 3 shall not require any Party to disclose confidential information which would impede law enforcement or otherwise be contrary to public interest or which would prejudice legitimate commercial interests particular enterprises.
Transparency Article 171
1. Each Party shall respond promptly to all requests by other Party for specific information on any its measures general application or international agreements which pertain to or affect this Title. Each Party shall establish one or more enquiry points to provide to interested persons other Party, upon request, specific information on all such matters ( ). The Parties shall notify each other enquiry points within three months date application this Title. Enquiry points need not be depositories laws and regulations.
2. All laws, regulations, decrees, decisions and administrative rulings general application Parties pertaining to or affecting any matter governed by this Title shall be published promptly in a manner that fulfils applicable requirements WTO Agreement, including those Article X GATT 1994, Article III GATS, and Article 63 TRIPS Agreement. The Parties shall update published resources, including websites, containing such measures, on a regular basis and make them readily available to interested persons. Such measures shall be available while they are in effect and for a reasonable period after they are no longer in effect.
3. The Parties shall publish all laws, regulations, decrees, decisions and administrative rulings general application pertaining to or affecting any matter governed by this Title, prior to their adoption. The Parties shall provide a reasonable period time, normally not less than 30 calendar days, for interested persons to comment to responsible authorities before relevant measure is finalised or submitted to authorities responsible for its adoption. Any comments received during period for comments will be taken into account.
4. No law, regulation, decree, decision or administrative ruling general application Parties pertaining to or affecting any matter governed by this Title shall become effective prior to publication.
5. Nothing in this Agreement shall require a Party to provide confidential information, disclosure which would impede law enforcement, or otherwise be contrary to public interest, or which would prejudice legitimate commercial interests particular enterprises, whether public or private.
6. Article 55 shall apply with respect to this Chapter.
Dispute settlement Section 1 Objective and scope Article 172 Objective The objective this Chapter is to establish an effective and efficient mechanism for avoiding and settling any dispute between Parties concerning interpretation and application this Agreement with a view to arriving, where possible, at a mutually agreed solution.
Article 173 Scope application This Chapter shall apply with respect to any dispute concerning interpretation and application this Title, except as otherwise provided.
Section 2 Consultations and mediation Article 174 Consultations
1. The Parties shall endeavour to resolve any dispute referred to in Article 173 by entering into consultations in good faith with aim reaching a mutually agreed solution.
2. A Party shall seek consultations by means a written request delivered to other Party, copied to Cooperation Committee, identifying measure at issue and provisions referred to in Article 173 that it considers applicable.
3. The Party to which request is made shall respond to request for consultations within 10 days date its receipt, unless otherwise provided for in this Agreement or agreed between Parties.
4. The consultations shall be held within 30 days date receipt request and take place, unless Parties agree otherwise, in territory Party to which request is made. The consultations shall be deemed concluded within 30 days date receipt request, unless both Parties agree to continue consultations. Con sultations, and in particular all information disclosed and positions taken by Parties during consultations, shall be confidential, and without prejudice to rights either Party in any further proceedings.
5. Consultations on matters urgency shall be deemed concluded within 15 days date receipt request by requested Party unless both Parties agree to continue consultations.
6. If Party to which request is made does not respond to request for consultations within 10 days date its receipt, or if consultations are not held within timeframes laid down in paragraph 3 or 4 this Article, respectively, or if Parties agree not to have consultations, or if consultations have been concluded and no mutually agreed solution has been reached, Party that sought consultations may have recourse to Article 176.
7. During consultations each Party shall provide sufficient factual information, so as to allow a complete examination manner in which measure at issue could affect operation and application this Agreement.
8. The consultations shall be deemed concluded within five working days date receipt request for consultations, unless Parties agree otherwise, when they concern emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138.
Article 175 Mediation Any Party may request other Party to enter into a mediation procedure with respect to any measure adversely affecting trade or investment between Parties pursuant to Annex VII.
Section 3 Dispute settlement procedures Article 176 Initiation arbitration procedure
1. Where Parties have failed to resolve dispute by recourse to consultations as provided for in Article 174, Party that sought consultations may request establishment an arbitration panel in accordance with this Article.
2. The request for establishment an arbitration panel shall be made by means a written request delivered to other Party and Cooperation Committee. The complaining Party shall identify in its request measure at issue, and it shall explain how such measure constitutes a breach provisions referred to in Article 173 in a manner sufficient to present legal basis for complaint clearly.
Article 177 Establishment arbitration panel
1. An arbitration panel shall be composed three arbitrators.
2. Within 10 days date delivery, to Party complained against, written request for establishment an arbitration panel, Parties shall consult in order to reach an agreement on composition arbitration panel.
3. In event that Parties are unable to agree on composition arbitration panel within time frame laid down in paragraph 2 this Article, each Party may, within five days from expiry timeframe established in paragraph 2 this Article, appoint an arbitrator from sub-list that Party contained in list established under Article 196. If either Party fails to appoint an arbitrator, arbitrator shall, upon request other Party, be selected by lot by chair Cooperation Committee, or chair's delegate, from sub-list that Party contained in list established under Article 196.
4. Unless Parties reach an agreement concerning chairperson arbitration panel within timeframe established in paragraph 2 this Article, c hair Cooperation Committee or chair's delegate shall, upon request either Party, select by lot chairperson arbitration panel from sub-list chairpersons contained in list established under Article 196.
5. The chair Cooperation Committee, or chair's delegate, shall select arbitrators within five days request by either Party referred to in paragraph 3 or 4.
6. The date establishment arbitration panel shall be last date on which all three selected arbitrators have accepted their appointment according to Rules Procedure set out in Annex V.
7. Should any lists provided for in Article 196 not be established or not contain sufficient names at time a request is made pursuant to paragraph 3 or 4 this Article, arbitrators shall be drawn by lot from individuals who have been formally proposed by one or both Parties.
8. Unless Parties agree otherwise, in respect a dispute concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138 between Parties, second sentence paragraph 3 and paragraph 4 this Article shall apply without recourse to paragraph 2 this Article, and period in paragraph 5 this Article shall be two days.
Article 178 Preliminary ruling on urgency If a Party so requests, arbitration panel shall, within 10 days its establishment, give a preliminary ruling on whether it deems case to be urgent.
Article 179 Conciliation for urgent energy disputes
1. In respect a dispute concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138 either Party may request chairperson arbitration panel to act as conciliator concerning any matter related to dispute by making a request to arbitration panel.
2. The conciliator shall seek an agreed resolution dispute or seek to agree a procedure to achieve such resolution. If within 15 days date his appointment conciliator has failed to secure such agreement, he shall recommend a resolution to dispute or a procedure to achieve such resolution and shall decide on terms and conditions to be observed from a date which he shall specify until dispute is resolved.
3. The Parties and entities under their control or jurisdiction shall respect recommendations made under paragraph 2 on terms and conditions for three months following conciliator's decision or until resolution dispute, whichever is earlier.
4. The conciliator shall respect Code Conduct for Members Arbitration Panels and Mediators (‘Code Conduct’) set out in Annex VI.
Article 180 Reports arbitration panel
1. The arbitration panel shall deliver an interim report to Parties setting out findings fact, applicability relevant provisions and basic rationale behind any findings and recommendations that it makes.
2. Any Party may deliver a written request to arbitration panel to review precise aspects interim report within 14 days its receipt.
3. After considering any written comments by Parties on interim report, arbitration panel may modify its interim report and make any further examination it considers appropriate.
4. The final report arbitration panel shall set out findings fact, applicability relevant provisions referred to in Article 173 and basic rationale behind any findings and conclusions that it makes. The final report shall include a sufficient discussion arguments made at interim review stage, and shall answer clearly to questions and observations Parties.
Article 181 Interim report arbitration panel
1. The arbitration panel shall deliver an interim report to Parties no later than 90 days after date establishment arbitration panel. When arbitration panel considers that this deadline cannot be met, chairperson arbitration panel shall notify Parties and Cooperation Committee in writing, stating reasons for delay and date on which arbitration panel plans to deliver its interim report. Under no circum stances should interim report be delivered later than 120 days after date establishment arbitration panel.
2. In cases urgency arbitration panel shall make every effort to deliver its interim report within 45 days and, in any case, no later than 60 days after date establishment arbitration panel. Any Party may deliver a written request to arbitration panel to review precise aspects interim report pursuant to Article 180(2) within seven days delivery interim report.
3. In respect a dispute concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138 between Parties, interim report shall be delivered within 20 days after date establishment arbitration panel, and any request pursuant to Article 180(2) shall be delivered within five days delivery interim report. The arbitration panel may also decide to dispense with interim report.
Article 182 Final report arbitration panel
1. The arbitration panel shall deliver its final report to Parties and to Cooperation Committee within 120 days date establishment arbitration panel. When arbitration panel considers that this deadline cannot be met, chairperson arbitration panel shall notify Parties and Cooperation Committee in writing, stating reasons for delay and date on which panel plans to deliver its final report. Under no circumstances should final report be delivered later than 150 days after date establishment arbitration panel.
2. In cases urgency, arbitration panel shall make every effort to deliver its report within 60 days after date establishment arbitration panel. Under no circumstances should final report be delivered later than 75 days after date establishment arbitration panel.
3. In respect a dispute concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138, arbitration panel shall deliver its final report within 40 days date establishment arbitration panel.
Article 183 Compliance with final report arbitration panel The Party complained against shall take necessary measures to comply promptly and in good faith with final report arbitration panel.
Article 184 Reasonable period time for compliance
1. If immediate compliance is not possible, Parties shall endeavour to agree on period time to comply with final report. In such a case, Party complained against shall, no later than 30 days after receipt final report arbitration panel, deliver a notification to complaining Party and Cooperation Committee time it will require for compliance (‘the reasonable period time’).
2. If there is disagreement between Parties on duration reasonable period time, complaining Party may, within 20 days receipt notification referred to in paragraph 1 this Article, request in writing that arbitration panel established initially pursuant to Article 177 (‘the original arbitration panel’) determine length reasonable period time. Such request shall be delivered simultaneously to other Party and to Cooperation Committee. The arbitration panel shall deliver its report to Parties and to Cooperation Committee within 20 days date receipt request.
3. The Party complained against shall notify complaining Party in writing its progress in complying with final report arbitration panel. This notification shall be provided in writing and delivered at least one month before expiry reasonable period time.
4. The reasonable period time may be extended by mutual agreement Parties.
Article 185 Review any measure taken to comply with final report arbitration panel
1. The Party complained against shall notify complaining Party and Cooperation Committee any measure that it has taken to comply with final report arbitration panel. This notification shall be delivered before end reasonable period time.
2. In event that there is disagreement between Parties concerning existence or consistency any measure notified under paragraph 1 this Article with provisions referred to in Article 173, complaining Party may deliver a written request to original arbitration panel to rule on matter. Such request shall identify specific measure at issue and explain how such measure is inconsistent with provisions referred to in Article 173, in a manner sufficient to present legal basis for complaint clearly. The arbitration panel shall deliver its report to Parties and to Cooperation Committee within 45 days date receipt request.
Article 186 Temporary remedies in case non-compliance
1. If Party complained against fails to notify any measure taken to comply with final report arbitration panel before expiry reasonable period time, or if arbitration panel rules that no measure taken to comply exists or that measure notified under Article 185(1) is inconsistent with that Party's obligations under provisions referred to in Article 173, Party complained against shall, if so requested by complaining Party and after consultations with that Party, present an offer for compensation.
2. If complaining Party decides not to request an offer for compensation under paragraph 1 this Article or, in case such request is made, if no agreement on compensation is reached within 30 days date expiry reasonable period time or delivery arbitration panel report under Article 185(2), complaining Party shall be entitled, upon notification to other Party and to Cooperation Committee, to take appropriate measures at a level equivalent to nullification or impairment ( ) caused by violation. The notification shall specify such measures. The complaining Party may implement measures at any moment after expiry a ten-day period from date receipt notification by Party complained against, unless Party complained against has requested arbitration under paragraph 3 this Article.
3. If Party complained against considers that appropriate measures are not at a level equivalent to nullifi cation or impairment caused by violation that Party's obligations under provisions referred to in Article 173, Party complained against may deliver a written request to original arbitration panel to rule on matter. Such request shall be notified to complaining Party and to Cooperation Committee before expiry ten-day period referred to in paragraph 2 this Article. The original arbitration panel shall deliver its report on measures notified by complaining Party to Parties and to Cooperation Committee within 30 days date delivery request. The complaining Party shall not make notified measures effective until original arbitration panel has delivered its report. Such measure made effective after delivery report shall be consistent with arbitration panel report.
4. The measures made effective by complaining Party and compensation foreseen in this Article shall be temporary and shall not be applied after: (a) Parties have reached a mutually agreed solution pursuant to Article 191;
(b) Parties have agreed that measure notified under Article 185(1) brings Party complained against in conformity with provisions referred to in Article 173; or (c) any measure that arbitration panel under Article 185(2) has found to be inconsistent with provisions referred to in Article 173 has been withdrawn or amended so as to bring it in conformity with those provisions.
Article 187 Review any measure taken to comply after adoption temporary remedies for non-compliance
1. The Party complained against shall notify complaining Party and Cooperation Committee measure it has taken to comply with final report arbitration panel following application compensation or taking an appropriate measure by complaining Party pursuant to Article 186, as case may be. With exception cases under paragraph 2 this Article, complaining party shall terminate measure within 30 days from receipt notification. In cases where compensation has been applied, and with exception cases under paragraph 2 this Article, Party complained against may terminate application such compensation within 30 days from receipt notification that it has complied with final report arbitration panel.
2. If Parties do not reach an agreement on whether Party complained against has complied with final report arbitration panel within 30 days from receipt notification under paragraph 1 this Article, complaining Party shall deliver a written request to original arbitration panel to rule on matter. Such a request shall be delivered simultaneously to other Party and to Cooperation Committee. The arbitration panel report shall be delivered to Parties and to Cooperation Committee within 45 days date submission request. If arbitration panel rules that Party complained against has complied with final report arbitration panel, complaining Party shall terminate appropriate measure taken under Article 186 or Party complained against shall terminate compensation, as case may be. If arbitration panel rules that Party complained against has not fully complied with final report arbitration panel, compensation or appropriate measure taken pursuant to Article 186 shall be adapted in light arbitration panel report.
Article 188 Remedies for urgent energy disputes
1. In respect a dispute concerning emergency situations as defined in point (h) Article 138 between Parties, this Article shall apply.
2. By way derogation from Articles 184, 185 and 186, complaining Party may take appropriate measures to a level equivalent to nullification or impairment caused by a Party failing to bring itself into compliance with final report arbitration panel within 15 days its release. Those measures may take effect immediately. Such measures may be maintained as long as Party complained against has not complied with final report arbitration panel.
3. Should Party complained against dispute existence a failure to comply or proportionality measure made effective by complaining Party or its failure to comply, it may initiate proceedings under Article 186(3) and Article 187 which shall be examined expeditiously. The complaining Party shall be required to remove or adjust measures only once arbitration panel has ruled on matter, and may maintain measures pending proceedings.
Article 189 Replacement arbitrators If in an arbitration proceeding under this Chapter original arbitration panel, or some its members, are unable to participate, withdraw, or need to be replaced because they do not comply with requirements Code Conduct set out in Annex VI, procedure set out in Article 177 shall apply. The time limit for delivery report may be extended for time necessary for appointment a new arbitrator but for no longer than 20 days.
Article 190 Suspension and termination arbitration and compliance procedures The arbitration panel shall, at request both Parties, suspend its work at any time for a period agreed by Parties not exceeding 12 consecutive months. The arbitration panel shall resume its work before end that period at written request both Parties or at end that period at written request either Party. The requesting Party shall notify chair Cooperation Committee and other Party accordingly. If a Party does not request resumption arbitration panel's work at expiry agreed suspension period, procedure shall be terminated. The suspension and termination arbitration panel's work are without prejudice to rights either Party in other proceedings subject to Article 197.
Article 191 Mutually agreed solution The Parties may reach a mutually agreed solution to a dispute under this Chapter at any time. They shall jointly notify Cooperation Committee and chairperson arbitration panel, where applicable, any such solution. If solution requires approval pursuant to relevant domestic procedures either Party, notification shall refer to this requirement, and dispute settlement procedure shall be suspended. If such approval is not required, or if completion any such domestic procedures is notified, dispute settlement procedure shall be terminated.
Article 192 Rules Procedure
1. Dispute settlement procedures under this Chapter shall be governed by Rules Procedure set out in Annex V and by Code Conduct set out in Annex VI.
2. Any hearing arbitration panel shall be open to public unless otherwise provided for in Rules Procedure set out in Annex V.
Article 193 Information and technical advice At request a Party, or on its own initiative, arbitration panel may request any information it deems appropriate for arbitration panel proceedings from any source, including Parties involved in dispute. The arbitration panel also has right to seek opinion experts, as it deems appropriate. The arbitration panel shall consult Parties before choosing such experts. Natural or legal persons established in territory a Party may submit amicus curiae briefs to arbitration panel in accordance with Rules Procedure set out in Annex V. Any information obtained under this Article shall be disclosed to each Party and submitted for their comments.
Article 194 Rules interpretation Any arbitration panel shall interpret provisions referred to in Article 173 in accordance with customary rules in terpretation public international law, including those codified in Vienna Convention 1969 on Law Treaties. The arbitration panel shall also take into account relevant interpretations WTO panels and Appellate Body adopted by WTO Dispute Settlement Body (hereinafter referred to as ‘DSB’). The reports arbitration panel cannot add to or diminish rights and obligations Parties under this Agreement.
Article 195 Decisions and reports arbitration panel
1. The deliberations arbitration panel shall be confidential. The arbitration panel shall make every effort to take any decision by consensus. Nevertheless, where a decision cannot be arrived at by consensus, matter at issue shall be decided by majority vote. In no case shall dissenting opinions arbitrators be disclosed.
2. The reports arbitration panel shall be drafted without presence Parties. The reports shall set out findings fact, applicability relevant provisions referred to in Article 173, and basic rationale behind any findings and conclusions that it makes.
3. The reports arbitration panel shall be unconditionally accepted by Parties. They shall not create any rights or obligations for natural or legal persons.
4. The Parties shall make arbitration panel report publicly available, subject to protection confidential information as provided for in Rules Procedure set out in Annex V.
Section 4 General prov isions Article 196 Lists arbitrators
1. The Cooperation Committee, on basis proposals made by Parties, shall, no later than six months after entry into force this Agreement, establish a list at least 15 individuals who are willing and able to serve as arbitrators. The list shall be composed three sub-lists: one sub-list for each Party and one sub-list individuals who are not nationals either Party and who may serve as chairperson arbitration panel. Each sub-list shall include at least five individuals. The Cooperation Committee will ensure that list is always maintained at that level.
2. Arbitrators shall have specialised knowledge and experience law and international trade. They shall be independent, serve in their individual capacities and not take instructions from any organisation or government, or be affiliated with government any Parties, and shall comply with Code Conduct set out in Annex VI.
3. The Cooperation Committee may establish additional lists 15 individuals with knowledge and experience in specific sectors covered by this Agreement. Subject to agreement Parties, such additional lists shall be used to compose arbitration panel in accordance with procedure set out in Article 177.
Article 197 Relation with W TO obligations
1. Recourse to dispute settlement provisions this Title shall be without prejudice to any action in WTO framework, including dispute settlement action.
2. However, a Party shall not, for a particular measure, seek redress for breach a substantially equivalent obligation under both this Agreement and WTO Agreement in both fora. In such a case, once a dispute settlement proceeding has been initiated, Party shall not bring a claim seeking redress for breach substantially equivalent obligation under other agreement to other forum, unless forum first selected fails for procedural or jurisdictional reasons to make findings on claim seeking redress that obligation.
3. For purposes this Article: (a) dispute settlement proceedings under WTO Agreement are deemed to be initiated by a Party's request for establishment a panel under Article 6 WTO Understanding on Rules and Procedures Governing Settlement Disputes;
(b) dispute settlement proceedings under this Chapter are deemed to be initiated by a Party's request for establishment an arbitration panel under Article 176(1).
4. Nothing in this Agreement shall preclude a Party from implementing suspension obligations authorised by DSB. The WTO Agreement shall not be invoked to preclude a Party from applying temporary remedies for noncompliance under this Chapter.
Article 198 Time limits
1. All time limits laid down in this Chapter, including limits for arbitration panels to deliver their reports, shall be counted in calendar days, first day being day following act or fact to which they refer, unless otherwise specified.
2. Any time limit referred to in this Chapter may be modified by mutual agreement Parties to dispute. The arbitration panel may at any time propose to Parties to modify any time limit referred to in this Chapter, stating reasons for that proposal.
Economic Dialogue Article 199 The Parties adhere to principles free market economy ensuring sound macroeconomic policies and shall develop and strengthen regular economic dialogue aimed at further expanding and deepening mutually beneficial economic ties, as well as sustainable development and economic growth.
Article 200 The Parties shall regularly review status bilateral cooperation and conduct regular exchange information, expertise and best practices in field economic policies, economic and financial development and statistics.
Cooperation in public finance management, including public audit and internal control Article 201 The Parties shall cooperate in area public finance management, including public audit and internal control, with aim further development a sound public finance management system, compatible with principles economy, efficiency and effectiveness as well as transparency and accountability.
Cooperation shall include: (a) promoting implementation acceptable and generally recognised international standards as well as convergence with good practices in this area;
(b) exchange information and experiences in this area.
Cooperation in area taxation Article 202 The Parties shall strive to improve international cooperation in area taxation, notably in area facilitating collection legitimate tax revenues, and to develop measures in line with international standards for effective implementation principles good governance in area taxation, including transparency and exchange information. The Parties shall strengthen dialogue and exchange experience with a view to avoiding harmful tax practices.
Cooperation in area statistics Article 203 The Parties shall promote harmonisation statistical methods and practice, including gathering and dissemina tion statistics. The statistical cooperation shall focus on exchange knowledge, fostering good practices and respect for UN Fundamental Principles Statistics and Statistics Code Practice.
The shall contribute to this end by rendering technical assistance to Republic Kazakhstan.
Cooperation in area energy Article 204 The Parties shall continue and intensify their current cooperation on energy matters with objective to enhance energy security, efficiency, sustainability, and competitiveness. The cooperation shall be based on a comprehensive partnership and shall be guided by principles mutual interest, reciprocity, transparency and predictability according to principles market economy and existing related multilateral and bilateral agreements.
Article 205 Cooperation shall include, inter alia, following areas: (a) implementation energy strategies and policies, elaboration forecasts and scenarios, including global market conditions for energy products, as well as improvement statistical system in energy sector;
(b) creation an attractive and stable investment climate and encouragement mutual investments in energy field on a non-discriminatory and transparent basis;
(c) effective cooperation with Investment Bank, Bank for Reconstruction and Development and other international financial institutions and instruments to support energy cooperation between Parties;
(d) enhancement scientific and technical cooperation and exchange information for development energy technologies with particular attention to energy efficient and environmentally friendly technologies, in accordance with Chapter 3 (Cooperation in Research and Innovation) Title VI;
(e) management and technical training in energy sector through, inter alia, facilitating exchange trainees specialised courses in higher education institutes in and Republic Kazakhstan as well as development joint training programmes in accordance with good practices;
(f) extension cooperation in multilateral energy fora, initiatives and institutions;
(g) cooperation in exchange knowledge and experience as well as technology transfer in innovation, including in areas management and energy technologies.
Article 206 Hydrocarbon energy Cooperation in field hydrocarbon energy shall cover following areas: (a) modernisation and enhancement existing, and development future, energy infrastructures common interest according to market principles, including those aimed at diversification energy sources, suppliers and transpor tation routes and transport methods, as well establishment new generation capacity and integrity, efficiency, safety and security energy infrastructures, including electric power infrastructures;
(b) development competitive, transparent and non-discriminatory energy markets in line with best practices through regulatory reforms;
(c) enhancement and strengthening long-term stability and security energy trade, including ensuring predict ability and stability energy demand, on a non-discriminatory basis, while minimising environmental impacts and risks;
(d) promotion a high level environmental protection and sustainable development in energy sector, including extraction, production, distribution and consumption;
(e) strengthening safety offshore hydrocarbon exploration and production activities, by means exchange experience in accident prevention, post-accident analysis, response and remediation policies, as well as best practices on liability and legal practice in case a disaster.
