Article 207 Renewable energy sources Cooperation shall be pursued in fields of: (a) development renewable energy sources in an economic and environmentally sound manner, including cooperation on regulatory issues, certification and standardisation as well as on technological development;
(b) facilitating exchanges between Republic Kazakhstan and institutions, laboratories and private sector entities, including through joint programmes, with aim implementing best practices towards creating energy future and green economy;
(c) conducting joint seminars, conferences and training programmes, and exchanging information and open statistical data on a regular basis, as well as information on development renewable energy sources.
Article 208 Energy eff iciency and energy savings Cooperation in promotion energy efficiency and energy savings, including in coal sector, gas f laring (and use associated gas), buildings, appliances and transport, shall be pursued, inter alia, through: (a) exchanging information about energy efficiency policies and legal and regulatory frameworks and action plans;
(b) facilitating exchange experiences and know-how in field energy efficiency and energy savings;
(c) initiating and implementing projects, including demonstration projects, for introduction innovative technologies and solutions in field energy efficiency and energy savings;
(d) training programmes and training courses in field energy efficiency in order to achieve objectives this Article.
Cooperation in area transport Article 209 The Parties shall cooperate on: (a) expanding and strengthening their transport cooperation in order to contribute to development sustainable transport systems;
(b) focusing on social and environmental aspects transport systems;
(c) promoting efficient, safe and secure transport operations;
(d) enhancing main transport links between their territories.
Article 210 The cooperation referred to in this Chapter shall cover, among others, following areas: (a) exchange best practices on transport policies;
(b) improvement movement passengers and goods, increasing f luidity transport f lows by removing adminis trative, technical and other obstacles, aiming at closer market integration, improving transport networks and upgrading infrastructure;
(c) information exchange and joint activities at regional and international level and implementation applicable inter national agreements and conventions;
(d) exchange best practices on safety and sustainable development maritime transport.
The Republic Kazakhstan shall bring its bilateral aviation agreements with Member States in accordance with legislation Union.
Article 211 A regular dialogue shall take place on issues covered by this Chapter.
Cooperation in area environment Article 212 The Parties shall develop and strengthen their cooperation on environmental issues, thereby contributing to sustainable development and good governance in environmental protection.
Cooperation shall be pursued in following fields: (a) environmental assessments, monitoring and control;
(b) environmental education and awareness raising, improving access to information, enhancing public participation in decision making and access to justice in environmental matters;
(c) legislation in field environmental protection;
(d) air quality;
(e) waste management;
(f) water quality management, including marine environment;
(g) integrated water resource management, including promotion advanced water saving technologies;
(h) conservation and protection biological and landscape diversity;
(i) sustainable forest management;
(j) industrial pollution and industrial emissions;
(k) classification and safe management chemicals;
(l) initiatives and Republic Kazakhstan in area green economy; and (m) mutual exchange experience regarding policies for sustainable development fisheries.
Article 213 Cooperation in field environmental protection shall be carried out by mutual consent Parties in, among others, following forms: (a) exchange technologies, scientific and technical information, and research activities in field environmental protection;
(b) exchange experience in improvement environmental legislation and methodologies.
Article 214 The Parties shall pay special attention to implementation of, and cooperation in, environmental issues in framework relevant multilateral environmental agreements and agree to intensify cooperation at regional level.
The Parties shall exchange experience in promoting integration environment into other sectors, including exchanging best practices, increasing knowledge and competence, environmental education and awareness raising in areas referred to in this Chapter.
Cooperation in area climate change Article 215 The Parties shall develop and strengthen their cooperation to combat and to adapt to climate change. Cooperation shall be conducted considering interests Parties on basis equality and mutual benefit and taking into account interdependence existing between bilateral and multilateral commitments in this field.
Article 216 Cooperation shall promote measures at domestic and international level, including in following areas: (a) mitigation climate change;
(b) adaptation to climate change;
(c) market and non-market approaches to addressing climate change;
(d) research, development, demonstration, deployment and diffusion new, safe and sustainable low-carbon and adaptation technologies;
(e) exchange climate expertise and support for other sectors;
(f) awareness raising, education and training.
Article 217 The Parties shall, inter alia, exchange information and expertise, implement joint research activities and exchanges information on cleaner technologies, implement joint activities at regional and international level, including with regard to multilateral environmental agreements applicable to Parties, such as UN Framework Convention on Climate Change, and joint activities in framework relevant agencies, as appropriate.
Cooperation in area industry Article 218 The Parties shall develop and strengthen their cooperation on industry, including issues development effective incentives and favourable conditions for further diversification and an increase in competitiveness manufac turing industry.
To that end, Parties shall cooperate, including through exchange best practices and experience, in following sectors: (a) productivity and efficiency resource use;
(b) public supporting measures for industry sectors, based on WTO requirements and other applicable rules Parties;
(c) implementation industrial policy within a context deepening integration;
(d) tools to enhance efficiency implementation industrial policy;
(e) investment activity in manufacturing industry, reduction its energy consumption, as well as exchange experiences in implementation labour productivity policies;
(f) conditions for development new production technologies, high-tech industries, and knowledge and technology transfer, as well as further development basic infrastructure and favourable environment for innovation clusters;
(g) investment and trade in mining and production raw materials, with objectives promoting mutual understanding and transparency, improving business environment, and promoting exchange information and cooperation in area non-energy mining, in particular metallic ores and industrial minerals;
(h) human resource capacity development in manufacturing industry;
(i) promotion business initiatives and industrial cooperation between enterprises and Republic Kazakhstan.
This Agreement does not exclude greater industrial cooperation between Parties, and separate arrangements may be concluded.
Cooperation in area small and medium-sized enterprises Article 219 The Parties shall develop and strengthen their cooperation in area small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) to foster a business environment conducive to successful development and creation SMEs.
To that end, Parties shall cooperate in following fields: (a) exchange information on SME development policy;
(b) exchange best practices on initiatives strengthening entrepreneurship as a key competence;
(c) promotion better contacts between business associations both Parties through closer dialogue;
(d) exchange experience in supporting capacity SMEs to access international markets;
(e) exchange experience in area improving regulatory framework impact on SMEs;
(f) exchange best practices on access to financing for SMEs.
Cooperation in area company law Article 220 The Parties recognise importance an effective set rules and practices in areas company law and corporate governance, as well as in accounting and auditing, in a functioning market economy with a predictable and transparent business environment, and underline importance promoting regulatory convergence in this field.
The Parties shall cooperate on following: (a) exchange best practices on ensuring availability and access to information regarding organisation and representation registered companies in a transparent and easily accessible way;
(b) further development corporate governance policy in line with international and particularly OECD standards;
(c) fostering implementation and consistent application International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) for consolidated accounts listed companies;
(d) approximation accounting rules and financial reporting, including as regards SMEs;
(e) regulation and oversight auditor and accountant professions;
(f) international auditing standards and Code Ethics International Federation Accountants (IFAC), with aim improving professional level auditors by means observance standards and ethical norms by professional organisations, audit organisations and auditors.
Cooperation in area banking, insurance and other financial services Article 221 The Parties agree on importance effective legislation and practices and to cooperate in area financial services with objectives of: (a) improving regulation financial services;
(b) ensuring effective and adequate protection investors and consumers financial services;
(c) contributing to stability and integrity global financial system;
(d) promoting cooperation between different actors financial system, including regulators and supervisors;
(e) promoting independent and effective supervision.
The Parties shall promote regulatory convergence with recognised international standards for sound financial systems.
Cooperation in area information society Article 222 The Parties shall promote cooperation on development information society to benefit citizens and businesses through widespread availability information and communication technologies (ICT) and through better quality services at affordable prices. This cooperation shall aim at promoting development competition in, and openness of, ICT markets as well as encouraging investments in this sector.
Article 223 Cooperation shall cover, inter alia, exchange information and best practices on implementation information society initiatives, focusing notably on: (a) developing an effective regulatory framework for ICT sector;
(b) promoting broadband access;
(c) developing interoperable electronic services;
(d) ensuring data protection; and (e) developing roaming services.
Article 224 The Parties shall promote cooperation between regulators in field ICT, including electronic communications, in and Republic Kazakhstan.
Cooperation in area tourism Article 225 The Parties shall cooperate in field tourism with aim strengthening development a competitive and sustainable tourism industry as a generator economic growth, empowerment, employment and exchanges in tourism sector.
Article 226 Cooperation shall be based on following principles: (a) respect for integrity and interests local communities, particularly in rural areas;
(b) importance preserving cultural and historical heritage; and (c) positive interaction between tourism and environmental preservation.
Article 227 Cooperation shall focus on following topics: (a) exchange information, best practices, experience and know-how, including on innovative technologies;
(b) establishment a strategic partnership between public, private and community stakeholders in order to support sustainable development tourism;
(c) promotion and development tourism products and markets, infrastructure, human resources and institutional structures as well as identification and elimination barriers to travel services;
(d) development and implementation efficient policies and strategies including appropriate legal, administrative and financial aspects;
(e) tourism training and capacity building in order to improve service standards; and (f) development and promotion tourism involving local population and other types tourism in a sustainable manner.
Cooperation in area agriculture and rural development Article 228 The Parties shall cooperate to promote agricultural and rural development, in particular through progressive convergence policies and legislation.
Article 229 Cooperation shall cover, among others, following areas: (a) facilitating mutual understanding agricultural and rural development policies;
(b) exchanging best practices in planning, evaluation and implementation agricultural and rural development policies;
(c) sharing knowledge and best practices with regard to rural development policies to promote social and economic well-being for rural inhabitants;
(d) promoting modernisation and sustainability agricultural production;
(e) improving competitiveness agricultural sector and efficiency and transparency markets;
(f) exchanging experience in geographical indications for agricultural products and foodstuffs, in quality policies and their control mechanisms, in ensuring food safety and in development production organic agricultural products;
(g) disseminating knowledge and promoting extension services to agricultural producers;
(h) promoting cooperation in agro-industrial investments projects, in particular in development livestock and crop sectors;
(i) exchanging experience in policies related to sustainable development agribusiness and processing and distribution agricultural products.
Cooperation on employment, labour relations, social policy and equal opportunities Article 230 The Parties shall promote development dialogue and cooperate on promoting ILO Decent Work Agenda, employment policy, living and working conditions and health and safety at work, social dialogue, social protection, social inclusion and anti-discrimination, as well as fair treatment workers legally residing and working in other Party.
Article 231 The Parties shall pursue goals covered by Article 230, including through cooperation and exchange practices in following areas: (a) improving quality life and ensuring a better social environment;
(b) enhancing social inclusion and level social protection for all workers and modernising social protection systems in terms quality, accessibility and financial sustainability;
(c) reducing poverty and enhancing social cohesion and protection vulnerable people;
(d) combating discrimination in employment and social affairs in accordance with each Party's obligations under inter national standards and conventions;
(e) promoting active labour market measures and improving efficiency employment services;
(f) aiming at more and better jobs with decent working conditions;
(g) improving living and working conditions, as well as level protection health and safety at work;
(h) enhancing gender equality by promoting participation women in social and economic life and ensuring equal opportunities between men and women in employment, education, training, economy, society and decision making;
(i) improving quality labour law and ensuring a better protection for workers;
(j) enhancing and promoting social dialogue, including increasing capacity social partners.
Article 232 The Parties reaffirm their commitments to effectively implement applicable ILO conventions.
The Parties, taking into account Ministerial Declaration UN Economic and Social Council on Generating Full and Productive Employment and Decent Work for All 2006, recognise that full and productive employment and decent work for all are key elements sustainable development.
The Parties shall encourage, in line with ILO Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work 1998, involvement all relevant stakeholders, in particular social partners, in their respective social policy development and in cooperation between and Republic Kazakhstan under this Agreement.
The Parties shall aim at enhancing cooperation on decent work, employment and social policy matters in all relevant fora and organisations.
Cooperation in area health Article 233 The Parties shall develop their cooperation in field public health with a view to raising level protection human health and reducing health inequalities, in line with common health values and principles, and as a precondition for sustainable development and economic growth.
Article 234 Cooperation shall address prevention and control communicable and non-communicable diseases, including through exchange health information, promoting a health-in-all-policies approach, cooperation with international organisations, in particular World Health Organisation, and by promoting implementation international health agreements, such as World Health Organisation Framework Convention on Tobacco Control 2003 and International Health Regulations.
Article 235 Rule law and respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms In their cooperation under this Title, Parties shall attach particular importance to promotion rule law, including independence judiciary, access to justice and right to a fair trial, and respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms.
The Parties shall cooperate in strengthening functioning institutions, including law enforcement, prosecution, administration justice and prevention of, and fight against, corruption.
Article 236 Legal cooperation The Parties shall develop cooperation in civil and commercial matters as regards negotiation, ratification and implementation relevant multilateral conventions on civil judicial cooperation and, in particular, conventions Hague Conference on Private International Law.
The Parties shall enhance cooperation in criminal matters, including on mutual legal assistance. This may include, where appropriate and subject to applicable procedures, accession to, and implementation of, Council Europe conventions in criminal proceedings by Republic Kazakhstan, implementation relevant UN international instruments, and cooperation with Eurojust.
Article 237 Protection personal data The Parties shall cooperate in order to ensure a high level protection personal data, through exchange best practices and experience, taking into account and international legal instruments and standards.
This may include, where appropriate and subject to applicable procedures, accession to, and implementation of, Council Europe Convention for Protection Individuals with regard to Automatic Processing Personal Data and its additional Protocol by Republic Kazakhstan.
Article 238 Cooperation on migration, asylum and border management
1. The Parties affirm importance that they attach to management migration f lows. Cooperation shall be based on mutual consultation between Parties and shall be implemented in accordance with relevant legislation in force.
2. Within framework cooperation to prevent and tackle irregular migration, Parties agree that: (a) Republic Kazakhstan shall readmit any its nationals irregularly present on territory a Member State Union, upon request by latter and without undue delay; and (b) each Member State shall readmit any its nationals irregularly present on territory Republic Kazakhstan, upon request by latter and without undue delay.
3. The Member States and Republic Kazakhstan shall provide their nationals with appropriate identity documents for purposes paragraph 2 without further formalities other than those referred to in this Article and without undue delay. Where person to be readmitted does not possess any documents or other proof his nationality, competent diplomatic and consular representations Member State concerned or Republic Kazakhstan shall, upon request by Republic Kazakhstan or Member State concerned, make arrangements to interview that person in order to establish his nationality without further formalities and without undue delay.
4. The Parties agree to establish a comprehensive dialogue on relevant migration-related issues in line with Global Approach to Migration and Mobility, inter alia, with a view to considering possible negotiation an agreement between and Republic Kazakhstan regulating specific obligations for Member States and Republic Kazakhstan on readmission, including an obligation for readmission nationals other countries and stateless persons, as well as with a view to considering possible negotiation, in parallel, an agreement on visa facilitation for citizens and Republic Kazakhstan.
Article 239 Consular protection The Republic Kazakhstan agrees that diplomatic and consular authorities any Member State represented in Republic Kazakhstan shall provide protection to any national a Member State that does not have an accessible permanent representation in Republic Kazakhstan, on same conditions as to nationals that Member State Union.
Article 240 Combating money laundering and financing terrorism The Parties shall cooperate in order to prevent use their financial and relevant non-financial sectors to launder proceeds criminal activities in general and drug offences in particular, as well as for purpose financing terrorism, in accordance with international standards on combating money laundering and financing terrorism, as adopted by Financial Action Task Force. This cooperation extends to recovery, seizure, confiscation and return assets or funds derived from proceeds crime.
Cooperation shall allow exchanges relevant information within framework relevant legislation and internat ional commitments Parties.
Article 241 Illicit drugs The Parties shall cooperate on a balanced and integrated approach to drug issues, notably on issues illicit trafficking in narcotic drugs, psychotropic substances and their precursors. Drug policies and actions shall be aimed at reinforcing structures for tackling supply of, and demand for, illicit drugs, psychotropic substances and their precursors, through enhancement coordination and strengthened cooperation between competent authorities aiming at reducing trafficking in, supply of, and demand for illicit drugs, enhancing preventive measures, treatment and rehabilitation, and with due regard to human rights.
Cooperation shall also aim to reduce drug-related harm, to address production and use synthetic drugs and to achieve effective prevention diversion drug precursors used for illicit manufacture drugs and psychotropic substances.
The Parties shall agree on means cooperation to attain those objectives. Actions shall be based on commonly agreed principles along lines relevant international conventions and instruments and — Central Asia Action Plan on Drugs.
Article 242 Fight against organised and transnational crime and corruption The Parties shall cooperate with aim preventing and fighting against all forms organised, economic, financial and transnational criminal activities, including smuggling and trafficking in human beings, drug trafficking, firearms trafficking, embezzlement, fraud, counterfeiting, forging documents, and public and private corruption, through full compliance with their existing international obligations in this field.
The Parties shall promote enhancement bilateral, regional and international cooperation among law enforcement bodies, including exchange best practices and possible cooperation with agencies Union.
The Parties are committed to effectively implementing relevant international standards, in particular those enshrined in UN Convention against Transnational Organised Crime (UNTOC) 2000 and its three Protocols, and UN Convention against Corruption 2003. Cooperation may include, where appropriate and subject to applicable procedures, accession to, and implementation of, Council Europe relevant instruments on preventing and combating corruption by Republic Kazakhstan.
Article 243 Fight against cybercrime The Parties shall strengthen cooperation, including through exchange best practices, with aim preventing and combating criminal acts committed using electronic communications networks and information systems or against such networks and systems.
Cooperation on education and training Article 244 The Parties shall cooperate in field education and training with a view to promoting modernisation education and training systems in Republic Kazakhstan and convergence with policies and practices Union. The Parties shall cooperate in order to promote lifelong learning and encourage cooperation and transparency at all levels education and training. The Parties shall, furthermore, place emphasis on measures designed to foster inter-institutional cooperation, encourage mobility for students, academic and administrative staff, researchers and young people, and encourage exchange information and experience.
The Parties shall promote unified coordination education system activity according to and international standards and best practices.
Cooperation in field culture Article 245 The Parties shall promote cultural cooperation that respects cultural diversity, in order to enhance mutual understanding and knowledge their respective cultures.
The Parties shall endeavour to take appropriate measures to promote cultural exchanges and encourage joint initiatives in various cultural spheres.
The Parties shall consult and develop mutually beneficial cooperation in framework multilateral international treaties and international organisations, such as United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organisation (Unesco). The Parties shall further exchange views on cultural diversity, aiming, inter alia, to promote principles Unesco Convention on Protection and Promotion Diversity Cultural Expressions 2005 and implement projects under International Decade for Rapprochement Cultures 2013 — 2022 proclaimed by UN General Assembly.
The Parties shall promote joint activities, programmes and plans, as well as exchange best practices in field training and capacity building for artists and cultural professionals and organisations.
Cooperation in research and innovation Article 246 The Parties shall promote cooperation: (a) in all areas civil research and scientific and technological development, on basis mutual benefit and subject to appropriate and effective protection intellectual property rights; and (b) to encourage development innovation.
Article 247 Cooperation shall include: (a) policy dialogue and exchange scientific and technological information;
(b) exchanging information and best practices regarding innovation and commercialisation research and development, including support instruments for technology-based business start-ups, cluster development and access to finance;
(c) facilitating adequate access to respective research and innovation programmes each Party;
(d) increasing research capacity in research entities Republic Kazakhstan and facilitating their participation in Framework Programme for Research and Innovation and in other potential initiatives financed by Union;
(e) developing and promoting joint projects for research and innovation;
(f) promoting commercialisation results obtained from joint research and innovation projects;
(g) facilitating new technology access to domestic markets Parties;
(h) arranging training activities and mobility programmes for scientists, researchers and other staff engaged in research and innovation activities in both Parties;
(i) facilitating, within framework applicable legislation, free movement research workers participating in activities covered by this Agreement and cross-border movement goods intended for use in such activities;
(j) other forms cooperation in research and innovation, including through regional approaches and initiatives, on basis mutual agreement.
Article 248 In carrying out cooperation activities set out in Article 247, synergies should be sought with regional and other activities carried out within broader framework financial cooperation between and Republic Kazakhstan as set out in Articles 261 and 262.
Cooperation in media and audiovisual fields Article 249 The Parties shall promote cooperation in media and audiovisual fields, including through exchange information and training for journalists and other media, cinema and audiovisual professionals.
Article 250 The Parties shall exchange information and best practices in promoting independence and professionalism media, based on standards set in applicable international conventions, including those Unesco and Council Europe, where appropriate.
Civil society cooperation Article 251 The Parties shall continue and strengthen their dialogue, in form meetings and consultations, and shall cooperate on role civil society, with following objectives: (a) to strengthen contacts and exchange information and experience between all sectors civil society in and in Republic Kazakhstan, enabling civil society representatives from a Party to become acquainted with processes consultation and dialogue with public institutions and social partners used by other Party, with a view, in particular, to further engaging civil society in public policy-making process;
(b) to ensure involvement civil society in relations between and Republic Kazakhstan, in particular in implementing this Agreement;
(c) to encourage greater capacity building, independence and transparency in civil society and to support its role in economic, social and political development Parties.
The Parties shall support development relations between non-governmental organisations from and Republic Kazakhstan.
The Parties shall give support to respective institutions and non-governmental organisations which implement activities in area human rights. The Parties shall share all relevant information on cooperation programmes, formally and regularly, at least once a year.
Cooperation in field sport and physical activity Article 252 The Parties shall promote cooperation in field sport and physical activity in order to help develop a healthy lifestyle among all age groups, to promote social functions and educational values sport, and to fight against threats to sport such as doping, racism, and violence. The cooperation shall include, in particular, exchange information and good practices.
Cooperation in area civil protection Article 253 The Parties recognise need to manage both domestic and global natural and man-made disaster risks.
In order to increase resilience their societies and infrastructure, Parties affirm their intention to improve prevention, mitigation, preparedness and response measures to natural and man-made disasters and to cooperate, as appropriate, at bilateral and multilateral political levels in order to improve global disaster risk-management outcomes.
Cooperation, subject to availability sufficient resources, shall support: (a) interaction competent bodies, other organisations and individuals that carry out activities in field civil protection;
(b) coordination mutual assistance, if requested, in case disasters;
(c) exchange experience in raising awareness populations on disaster preparedness;
(d) training, retraining, skills upgrading and specialist training in field civil protection and in using early warning systems.
Cooperation in space activities Article 254 The Parties shall promote, where appropriate, long-term cooperation in areas civil space research and development. The Parties shall pay particular attention to initiatives envisaging complementarity their respective space activities.
Article 255 The Parties may cooperate in areas satellite navigation, earth observation, space research and other areas in accordance with interests Parties.
Cooperation in area consumer protection Article 256 The Parties shall cooperate in order to ensure a high level consumer protection and to achieve compatibility between their systems consumer protection.
Cooperation may comprise, when appropriate: (a) exchanging best practices in consumer policy, including product quality and safety requirements, and organising a market surveillance system and an information exchange mechanism;
(b) promoting exchange experience in consumer protection systems, including consumer legislation and its enforcement, consumer product safety, raising consumer awareness and empowerment, and consumer redress;
(c) providing training activities for administration officials and other consumer interest representatives;
(d) encouraging development independent consumer organisations and contact between consumer represen tatives.
Regional cooperation Article 257 The Parties shall promote mutual understanding and bilateral cooperation in field regional policy with objective improving living conditions and increasing participation all regions in social and economic development Parties.
Article 258 The Parties shall support and strengthen involvement local and regional level authorities in regional cooperation, in accordance with existing international agreements and arrangements, in order to develop capacity building measures and to promote strengthening regional economic and business networks.
Article 259 The Parties shall strengthen and encourage development regional cooperation elements areas covered by this Agreement, inter alia, transport, energy, communication networks, culture, education, research, tourism, water resources and environment, civil protection and other areas which have a bearing on regional cooperation.
Cooperation in field civil service Article 260
1. The Parties shall facilitate exchange experience and knowledge in implementing international best practices within public and civil services and in capacity building for public and civil servants and their professional development and training.
2. The Parties shall facilitate dialogue on measures aimed at improving quality public services and on joint efforts to promote multilateral cooperation within framework regional civil service hub in Republic Kazakhstan.
3. In framework referred to in paragraph 2, Parties shall cooperate, inter alia, through facilitating: (a) exchange experts;
(b) organisation seminars; and (c) organisation training activities.
Article 261 The Parties shall continue and intensify current financial and technical cooperation, based on a comprehensive partnership and principles mutual interest, reciprocity, transparency, predictability and mutual protection interests Parties.
To achieve objectives this Agreement, Republic Kazakhstan may receive financial assistance from in form grants and loans, possibly in partnership with Investment Bank and other international financial institutions.
Financial assistance may be provided in accordance with relevant regulations governing multi-annual financial framework ( ), notably in form exchanges experts, conducting research, organising fora, conferences, seminars and training courses, grants in support development and implementation programmes and projects. The Financial Regulation ( ) and Implementing Rules ( ) shall apply to financing by Union.
Financial assistance shall be based on annual action programmes established by Union, following consulta tions with Republic Kazakhstan.
The and Republic Kazakhstan may co-finance programmes and projects. The Parties shall coordinate programmes and projects on financial and technical cooperation and shall exchange information on all sources assistance.
Aid effectiveness, as laid down in OECD Paris Declaration on Aid Effectiveness, ‘Backbone Strategy on Reforming Technical Cooperation’ Union, reports Court Auditors, and lessons learnt from implemented and ongoing cooperation programmes in Republic Kazakhstan, shall be basis for delivery financial assistance to Republic Kazakhstan.
Article 262 The Parties shall implement financial and technical assistance in accordance with principles sound financial management and cooperate in protection financial interests and Republic Kazakhstan. The Parties shall take effective measures to prevent and fight irregularities ( ), fraud, corruption and any other illegal activities to detriment budget and budget Republic Kazakhstan, by means mutual legal and other assistance, in fields covered by this Agreement.
Any further agreement or financing instrument to be concluded between Parties during implementation this Agreement shall provide for specific financial cooperation clauses covering on-the-spot inspections and controls.
Article 263 To make optimum use available resources, Parties commit themselves to ensuring that contributions are made in close coordination with contributions from other sources, third countries and international financial institutions.
Article 264 Prevention The Parties shall check regularly that operations financed by funds and co-financed by funds Republic Kazakhstan have been properly implemented and shall take all appropriate measures to prevent irregu larities, fraud, corruption, and any other illegal activities to detriment funds and co-financing funds Republic Kazakhstan. The Parties shall inform each other any preventive measures taken.
Article 265 Communication The Parties shall inform each other, notifying in particular Anti-Fraud Office and competent authorities Republic Kazakhstan, suspected or actual cases fraud, corruption, or any other irregularities in connection with implementation funds and co-financing funds Republic Kazakhstan.
The Parties shall inform each other any measures taken in relation to this Article.
Article 266 On-the-spot inspections On-the-spot inspections with respect to financial assistance shall be prepared and conducted by Anti-Fraud Office in close cooperation with competent authorities Republic Kazakhstan, in accordance with legislation Republic Kazakhstan.
Within framework this Agreement, Anti-Fraud Office shall be authorised to carry out on-the-spot inspections in order to protect financial interests Union, in accordance with Council Regulation (Euratom, EC) No 2185/96 ( ) and Regulation (EU, Euratom) No 883/2013 ( ) Parliament and Council.
Article 267 Investigation and prosecution The competent bodies Republic Kazakhstan shall investigate and prosecute, in accordance with legislation Republic Kazakhstan, suspected and actual cases fraud, corruption and any other illegal activities to detriment funds and co-financing funds Republic Kazakhstan. Where appropriate, and upon formal request, Anti-Fraud Office may assist competent authorities Republic Kazakhstan in this task.
Article 268 Cooperation Council
1. The Cooperation Council is hereby established. It shall supervise and regularly review implementation this Agreement. It shall meet once a year at ministerial level. It shall examine any major issues arising within framework this Agreement and any other bilateral or international issues mutual interest for purpose attaining objectives this Agreement.
2. For purpose attaining objectives this Agreement, Cooperation Council shall take decisions within scope this Agreement, in cases provided for therein. Such decisions shall be binding upon Parties, who shall take appropriate measures to implement decisions taken. The Cooperation Council may also make recommen dations. It shall adopt its decisions and recommendations by agreement between Parties following completion their respective internal procedures.
3. The Cooperation Council shall have power to update or amend Annexes to this Agreement, based on consensus between Parties, without prejudice to any specific provisions under Title III (Trade and Business).
4. The Cooperation Council may delegate any its powers to Cooperation Committee, including power to take binding decisions.
5. The Cooperation Council shall be composed representatives Parties.
6. The Cooperation Council shall be chaired alternately by a representative and a representa tive Republic Kazakhstan.
7. The Cooperation Council shall establish its rules procedure.
8. Either Party may refer any dispute relating to implementation or interpretation this Agreement to Cooperation Council, in accordance with Article 278.
Article 269 Cooperation Committee and specialised subcommittees
1. A Cooperation Committee is hereby established. It shall assist Cooperation Council in performance its duties.
2. The Cooperation Committee shall be composed representatives Parties, in principle at senior civil servant level.
3. The Cooperation Committee shall be chaired alternately by a representative and a represen tative Republic Kazakhstan.
4. The Cooperation Committee shall take decisions in cases provided for in this Agreement and in areas in which Cooperation Council has delegated powers to it. Those decisions shall be binding upon Parties, who shall take appropriate measures to implement decisions taken. The Cooperation Committee shall adopt its decisions by agreement between Parties, following completion their respective internal procedures. Its responsibilities shall include preparing meetings Cooperation Council.
5. The Cooperation Committee may meet in a special composition to address relevant issues related to Title III (Trade and Business).
6. The Cooperation Council may decide to set up specialised subcommittees or any other bodies that can assist it in carrying out its duties and shall determine composition and duties such subcommittees or bodies and how they shall function.
7. In its rules procedure, Cooperation Council shall determine duties and functioning Cooperation Committee and any subcommittee or body set up by Cooperation Council.
Article 270 Parliamentary Cooperation Committee
1. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee is hereby established. It shall consist Members Parliament, on one hand, and Members Parliament Republic Kazakhstan, on other, and shall be a forum for them to meet and exchange views. It shall meet at intervals which it shall itself determine.
2. The activity Parliamentary Cooperation Committee shall aim to develop mutually beneficial and effective parliamentary cooperation between Parliament and Parliament Republic Kazakhstan.
3. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee shall establish its rules procedure.
4. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee shall be presided over alternately by Parliament and Parliament Republic Kazakhstan, in accordance with provisions to be laid down in its rules procedure.
5. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee may request information regarding implementation this Agreement from Cooperation Council, which shall then supply Committee with requested information.
6. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee shall be informed decisions and recommendations Cooperation Council.
7. The Parliamentary Cooperation Committee may make recommendations to Cooperation Council.
Article 271 Access to courts and administrative organs Within scope this Agreement, each Party undertakes to ensure that natural and legal persons other Party have access, free discrimination and under similar conditions as their own natural and legal persons, to their competent courts and administrative organs to defend their individual and property rights.
Article 272 Delegation authority Unless otherwise specified in this Agreement, each Party shall ensure that a person that has been delegated regulatory, administrative or other governmental authority by a Party at any level government, such as power to grant import or export licences or licences for other economic activities, to approve commercial transactions or impose quotas, fees or other charges, acts, in exercise that authority, in accordance with that Party's obligations as set out under this Agreement.
Article 273 Restrictions in case balance-of-payments and external f inancial diff iculties
1. Where a Party experiences serious balance-of-payments or external financial difficulties, or where there is a threat thereof, it may adopt or maintain safeguard or restrictive measures which affect movements capital, payments or transfers.
2. The measures referred to in paragraph 1 shall: (a) not treat a Party less favourably than a non-Party in like situations;
(b) be consistent with Articles Agreement International Monetary Fund, as applicable;
(c) avoid unnecessary damage to commercial, economic and financial interests other Party;
(d) be temporary and be phased out progressively as situation specified in paragraph 1 improves.
3. In case trade in goods, a Party may adopt or maintain restrictive measures in order to safeguard its balanceof-payments or external financial position. Such measures shall be in accordance with GATT 1994 and Understanding on Balance Payment Provisions GATT 1994.
4. In case trade in services, a Party may adopt restrictive measures in order to safeguard its balance-ofpayments or external financial position. Such measures shall be in accordance with GATS.
5. Any Party maintaining or having adopted restrictive measures referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2 shall promptly notify other Party them and present, as soon as possible, a time schedule for their removal.
6. Where restrictions are adopted or maintained under this Article, consultations shall be held promptly in Cooperation Committee, if such consultations are not otherwise taking place outside scope this Agreement.
7. The consultations shall assess balance-of-payments or external financial difficulties that led to respective measures, taking into account, inter alia, such factors as: (a) nature and extent difficulties;
(b) external economic and trading environment; or (c) alternative corrective measures which may be available.
8. The consultations shall address compliance any restrictive measures with paragraphs 1 and 2.
9. In such consultations, all statistical findings and other facts presented by IMF relating to foreign exchange, monetary reserves and balance payments shall be accepted by Parties and conclusions shall be based on assessment by IMF balance- of-payments and external financial position Party concerned.
Article 274 Measures related to essential security interests Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed: (a) as requiring any Party to furnish any information, disclosure which it considers contrary to its essential security interests;
(b) as preventing any Party from taking any action which it considers necessary for protection its essential security interests: (i) connected with production or trade in arms, munitions or war material;
(ii) relating to economic activities carried out directly or indirectly for purpose provisioning a military establishment;
(iii) relating to fissionable and fusionable materials or materials from which they are derived;
(iv) relating to government procurement indispensable for national security or for defence purposes; or (v) taken in time war or other emergency in international relations; or (c) as preventing any Party from taking any action in pursuance obligations it has accepted for purpose maintaining international peace and security.
Article 275 Non-discrimination
1. In fields covered by this Agreement and without prejudice to any special provisions contained therein: (a) arrangements applied by Republic Kazakhstan in respect and its Member States shall not give rise to any discrimination between Member States or their natural or legal persons;
(b) arrangements applied by or its Member States in respect Republic Kazakhstan shall not give rise to any discrimination between natural or legal persons Republic Kazakhstan.
2. Paragraph 1 shall be without prejudice to right Parties to apply relevant provisions their fiscal legislation to taxpayers who are not in identical situations as regards their place residence.
Article 276 Taxation
1. This Agreement shall only apply to taxation measures in so far as such application is necessary to give effect to provisions this Agreement.
2. Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as preventing adoption or enforcement any measure aimed at preventing avoidance or evasion taxes pursuant to tax provisions agreements for avoidance double taxation, other tax arrangements or domestic fiscal legislation.
Article 277 Fulf ilment obligations
1. The Parties shall take any measures required to fulfil their obligations under this Agreement. They shall ensure that objectives set out in this Agreement are attained.
2. The Parties shall consult each other promptly through appropriate channels, at request either Party, in order to discuss any matter concerning interpretation or implementation this Agreement and other relevant aspects relations between Parties.
3. Each Party shall refer to Cooperation Council any dispute related to interpretation or implementation this Agreement in accordance with Article 278.
4. The Cooperation Council may settle a dispute in accordance with Article 278 and by means a binding decision.
Article 278 Dispute settlement
1. When a dispute arises between Parties concerning interpretation or implementation this Agreement, either Party shall submit to other Party and to Cooperation Council a formal request that matter in dispute be resolved. By way derogation, disputes concerning interpretation or implementation Title III (Trade and Business) shall be exclusively governed by Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business).
2. The Parties shall endeavour to resolve dispute by entering into good faith consultations within Cooperation Council as provided for in Article 268 with aim reaching a mutually acceptable solution as soon as possible. Con sultations on a dispute can also be held at meetings Cooperation Committee or any other relevant subcommittee or body set up on basis Article 269, as agreed between Parties or at request either Party. Consultations may also be held in writing.
3. The Parties shall provide Cooperation Council, Cooperation Committee or any other relevant subcommittee or body with all information required for a thorough examination situation.
4. A dispute shall be deemed to be resolved when Cooperation Council has taken a binding decision to settle matter as provided for in Article 277, or when it has declared that dispute has reached an end.
5. All information disclosed during consultations shall remain confidential.
Article 279 Appropriate measures in case non-fulfilment obligations
1. If matter is not resolved within three months date notification a formal request for a dispute settlement in accordance with Article 278, and if complaining Party considers that other Party has failed to fulfil an obligation under this Agreement, it may take appropriate measures, except in case disputes concerning in terpretation or implementation Title III (Trade and Business).
2. By way derogation from paragraph 1 this Article, any Party may immediately take appropriate measures with regard to this Agreement in accordance with international law in case of: (a) denunciation this Agreement not sanctioned by general rules international law within meaning Article 60(3) Vienna Convention 1969 on Law Treaties; or (b) violation by other Party any essential elements this Agreement referred to in Articles 1 and 11 this Agreement.
In those cases, appropriate measure shall be notified immediately to other Party. At request other Party, consultations shall be held for a period up to 20 days. After this period, measure shall apply.
3. In selection appropriate measures, priority shall be given to those which least disturb functioning this Agreement and are proportionate to nature and gravity breach. Those measures shall be notified immediately to Cooperation Council and shall be subject immediate consultations, during which each Party has right to remove violation in question.
Article 280 Public access to off icial documents The provisions this Agreement shall be without prejudice to application relevant legislation Parties regarding public access to official documents.
Article 281 Entry into force, provisional application, duration and termination
1. This Agreement shall enter into force on first day second month following date on which Parties notify General Secretariat Council through diplomatic channels completion procedures necessary for that purpose.
2. Title III (Trade and Business), unless otherwise specified therein, shall apply as date entry into force referred to in paragraph 1, provided that Republic Kazakhstan has become a Member WTO by that date. In case Republic Kazakhstan becomes a Member WTO after date entry into force this Agreement, Title III (Trade and Business), unless otherwise specified therein, shall apply as date Republic Kazakhstan has become a Member WTO.
3. Notwithstanding paragraphs 1 and 2, and Republic Kazakhstan may apply this Agreement provisionally in whole or in part, in accordance with their respective internal procedures and legislation, as applicable.
4. The provisional application begins on first day first month following date on which: (a) has notified Republic Kazakhstan completion necessary procedures, indicating, where relevant, parts this Agreement that shall be provisionally applied; and (b) Republic Kazakhstan has notified ratification this Agreement.
5. Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, unless otherwise specified therein, shall apply provisionally as date provisional application referred to in paragraph 4, provided that Republic Kazakhstan has become a Member WTO by that date. In case Republic Kazakhstan becomes a Member WTO after date provisional application this Agreement but before its entry into force, Title III (Trade and Business), unless otherwise specified therein, shall apply provisionally as date Republic Kazakhstan has become a Member WTO.
6. For purposes relevant provisions this Agreement, including Annexes and Protocols thereto, any reference in such provisions to ‘date entry into force this Agreement’ shall be understood to also refer to date from which this Agreement is provisionally applied in accordance with paragraphs 4 and 5.
7. Upon entry into force this Agreement, Partnership and Cooperation Agreement between Communities and their Member States, one part, and Republic Kazakhstan, other part, signed in Brussels on 23 January 1995 and in force from 1 July 1999, shall be terminated.
During period provisional application, in so far as provisions Partnership and Cooperation Agreement between Communities and their Member States, one part, and Republic Kazakhstan, other part, signed in Brussels on 23 January 1995 and which entered into force on 1 July 1999, are not covered by provisional application this Agreement, they continue to apply.
8. This Agreement replaces Agreement referred to in paragraph 7. References to that Agreement in all other agreements between Parties shall be construed as referring to this Agreement.
9. This Agreement is concluded for an unlimited period, with possibility termination by either Party by means a written notification delivered to other Party through diplomatic channels. The termination shall take effect six months after receipt by a Party notification to terminate this Agreement. Such termination shall not affect ongoing projects commenced under this Agreement prior to receipt notification.
10. Either Party may terminate provisional application by means a written notification delivered to other Party through diplomatic channels. The termination shall take effect six months after receipt by a Party notification to terminate provisional application this Agreement. Such termination shall not affect ongoing projects commenced under this Agreement prior to receipt notification.
Article 282 Existing agreements between Parties relating to specific areas cooperation falling within scope this Agreement shall be considered part their overall bilateral relations as governed by this Agreement and shall form part a common institutional framework.

29/115 Article 283
1. The Parties may by mutual consent amend, revise, and expand this Agreement with a view to enhancing level cooperation.
2. The Parties may complement this Agreement by concluding specific international agreements between them in any area falling within its scope. Such specific international agreements between Parties shall be an integral part their overall bilateral relations, as governed by this Agreement and shall form part a common institutional framework.
Article 284 Annexes and Protocols Annexes and Protocols to this Agreement shall form an integral part thereof.
Article 285 Definition Parties For purposes this Agreement, term ‘the Parties’ shall mean or its Member States, or and its Member States, in accordance with their respective powers, one part, and Republic Kazakhstan, other part.
Article 286 Territorial application This Agreement shall apply to territories in which Treaty on and Treaty on Functioning are applied and under conditions laid down in those Treaties, and to territory Republic Kazakhstan.
Article 287 Authentic texts This Agreement is drawn up in duplicate in Bulgarian, Croatian, Czech, Danish, Dutch, English, Estonian, Finnish, French, German, Greek, Hungarian, Italian, Latvian, Lithuanian, Maltese, Polish, Portuguese, Romanian, Slovak, Slovenian, Spanish, Swedish, Kazakh and Russian languages, each text being equally authentic.
, respective representatives have signed this Agreement.
Done at Astana on twenty first day December in year two thousand and fifteen.
The Republic Kazakhstan reserves right to maintain or adopt any measure inconsistent with national treatment commitments as set out below:
1. Subsurface sector
1.1. Use surface and subsurface in Republic Kazakhstan is subject to establishment in form a juridical person Republic Kazakhstan (i.e. subsidiary company).
1.2. The State has a priority right to purchase subsurface use right (or a part thereof) and/or an object related to subsurface use rights.
2. Strategic resources and objects The Republic Kazakhstan may refuse to permit juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan to carry out transactions on use strategic resources and/or purchase strategic objects in Republic Kazakhstan, if such use or purchase may lead to a concentration rights with one person or group persons from same countries. The compliance with this condition is also mandatory with respect to affiliates as defined in relevant legislation Republic Kazakhstan ( ). The Republic Kazakhstan may establish limits on ownership rights and transfer ownership rights for strategic resources and objects Republic Kazakhstan based on national security interests.
3. Real estate
3.1. Juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan cannot privately own lands used for farming/agricultural production or forest planning purposes. Juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan may be granted right temporary land use for farming/agricultural production purposes for a period up to 10 years, renewable.
3.2. Private ownership land plots located in border zone, in borderland and within seaports Republic Kazakhstan is prohibited for juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan.
3.3. Lease-holding land plots for agricultural purposes adjacent to State border Republic Kazakhstan is restricted for juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan.
3.4. The right permanent land use cannot be granted to juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons and their branches established in territory Republic Kazakhstan.
4. Fauna
4.1. Access to and use biological resources and fishing grounds situated in maritime and internal waters coming under sovereignty or within jurisdiction Republic Kazakhstan is restricted to fishing vessels f lying f lag Republic Kazakhstan and registered in territory Republic Kazakhstan unless otherwise provided for. Fishing vessels owned by subsidiaries juridical persons established in form a juridical person Republic Kazakhstan shall not be prohibited from f lying f lag Republic Kazakhstan.
4.2. The priority in granting usage wildlife in a particular area or water zone is given to juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan.
5. Establishment requirements for licensing purposes Companies producing goods subject to licensing due to important reasons public health, safety or national security shall be established in form a juridical person Republic Kazakhstan.
6. Continental shelf Limitations may be introduced within continental shelf Republic Kazakhstan.
The reserves right to maintain or adopt any measure inconsistent with national treatment commitments differentiated by its Member States, where applicable, as set out below.
1. Mining and quarrying including extraction oil and natural gas In some Member States restrictions may apply; may apply restrictions to juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan which account for more than 5 % Union's oil or natural gas imports.
2. Production petroleum products, gas, electricity, steam, hot water and heat In some Member States restrictions may apply; may apply restrictions to juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan which account for more than 5 % Union's oil or natural gas imports.
3. Fishing Access to and use biological resources and fishing grounds situated in maritime waters coming under sovereignty or within jurisdiction Member States is restricted to fishing vessels f lying f lag a Member State and registered in territory unless otherwise provided for.
4. Acquisition real estate including land In some Member States Union, restrictions may apply to acquisition real estate, including land, by juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan.
5. Agriculture including hunting In some Member States Union, national treatment is not applicable to juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan which wish to undertake an agricultural enterprise;
acquisition vineyards by juridical persons controlled by natural or juridical persons Republic Kazakhstan is subject to notification or, as necessary, authorisation.
6. Aquaculture activities National treatment does not apply to aquaculture activities in territory Union.
7. Extraction and processing fissionable and fusionable materials or materials from which they are derived In some Member States restrictions may apply.
A juridical person attracting intra-corporate transferees (ICTs) in a non-services sector must be engaged in production goods ( ).
Employment ICTs as managers and specialists shall meet requirements economic needs test ( ). Upon expiration a five-year period after Republic Kazakhstan's accession to WTO, economic needs test shall not be applied ( ).
The number ICTs is limited to 50 % total number executives, managers and specialists within each category in companies with a minimum three individuals.
The entry and temporary stay ICTs Party shall be permitted for three years, based on permits, annually issued by authorised body.

For Union:
1. Chapter 8 (Government Procurement) Title III (Trade and business) this Agreement does not cover: (a) procurement agricultural products made in furtherance agricultural support programmes and human feeding programmes (for example, food aid including urgent relief aid); and (b) procurement for acquisition, development, production or co-production programme material by broadcasters and contracts for broadcasting time.
2. Procurement by procuring entities covered under Parts 1 and 2 this Annex in connection with activities in fields drinking water, energy, transport and postal sector are not covered by this Agreement, unless covered under Part 3 this Annex.
3. In respect Åland Islands, special conditions Protocol No 2 on Åland Islands to Treaty Accession Austria, Finland and Sweden to shall apply.
4. As far as procurement by entities in field defence and security is concerned, coverage shall be limited to goods that are non-sensitive and non-warlike materials.
5. The procurement by procuring entities good or service components procurement which are not themselves covered by this Agreement shall not be considered as covered procurement.
For Republic Kazakhstan:
1. Chapter 8 (Government Procurement) Title III (Trade and business) this Agreement does not cover: (a) procurement agricultural products made in furtherance agricultural support programmes, including procurement for food security purposes, and human feeding programmes (for example food aid including urgent relief aid);
(b) procurement for acquisition, development, production or co-production programme material by broadcasters and contracts for broadcasting time;
(c) procurement goods, works and services pursuant to Article 41(3) Law No 303-III on Government Procurement 21 July 2007 when it involves information which constitutes a state secret;
(d) procurement in field research and exploration space for peaceful purposes, international cooperation in implementation joint projects and programs in field space activities;
(e) procurement goods, works and services that are exclusively provided by a natural or state monopoly; or (f) procurement financial services, unless specified in Part 5 this Annex.
2. Chapter 8 (Government Procurement) Title III (Trade and business) this Agreement does not apply to any setaside for benefit small or minority-owned businesses or businesses that employ people with special needs. A set-aside means any form preference, such as exclusive right to provide a good or service, or any price preference.
3. Chapter 8 (Government Procurement) Title III (Trade and business) this Agreement does not cover procurement made by a covered entity on behalf a non-covered entity.
4. The procurement by procuring entities good or service components procurement which are not themselves covered by this Agreement shall not be considered as covered procurement.
5. Procurement by procuring entities covered under Parts 1 and 2 this Annex in connection with activities in fields drinking water, energy, transport and postal sector are not covered by this Agreement, unless covered by Part 3 this Annex.
