1. In Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement and under these rules: (a) ‘adviser’ means a person retained by a Party to dispute to advise or assist that Party in connection with arbitration panel proceeding;
(b) ‘arbitrator’ means a member an arbitration panel established under Article 177 this Agreement;
(c) ‘assistant’ means a person who, under terms appointment an arbitrator, conducts research or provides assistance to that arbitrator;
(d) ‘complaining Party’ means any Party that requests establishment an arbitration panel under Article 176 this Agreement;
(e) ‘Party complained against’ means Party that is alleged to be in violation provisions referred to in Article 173 this Agreement;
(f) ‘arbitration panel’ means a panel established under Article 177 this Agreement;
(g) ‘epresentative a Party’ means an employee or any person appointed by a Party for purposes a dispute under this Agreement;
(h) ‘day’ means a calendar day;
(i) ‘working day’ means a day other than a public holiday, Saturday and Sunday.
2. The Parties shall share expenses derived from organisational matters, including remuneration and expenses arbitrators.
Notifications
3. The request for consultations and request for establishment an arbitration panel shall be delivered to other Party by electronic communication, facsimile transmission, registered post, courier, or any other means telecommunication that provides a record sending thereof.
4. Each Party to dispute and arbitration panel shall deliver any document other than request for consulta tions and request for establishment an arbitration panel by e-mail and by facsimile transmission, registered post, courier, or any other means telecommunication that provides a record sending thereof to other Party and, where relevant, to each arbitrators. Unless proven otherwise, an e-mail message shall be deemed to be delivered on date its sending. If any supporting documents is confidential or too big to be sent by e-mail, Party sending document may provide that document in another electronic format to other Party and, where relevant, to each arbitrators within one day delivery e-mail. In those cases Party delivering document shall inform by e-mail other Party and, where relevant, each arbitrators sending document and indicate its content.
5. All notifications shall be addressed to Government Republic Kazakhstan and to DirectorateGeneral for Trade Commission, respectively. Within 30 days start application Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, Parties shall exchange details for electronic communi cations pursuant to rules 3 and 4 these Rules Procedure. Any change e-mail addresses or other electronic communications shall be notified without delay to other Party and arbitration panel, where applicable.
6. Minor errors a clerical nature in any request, notice, written submission or other document related to arbitration panel proceeding may be corrected by prompt delivery a new document clearly indicating changes.
7. If last date for delivery a document falls on a Saturday, Sunday or a public holiday for or for Republic Kazakhstan, last date for delivery shall be next working day. When a document is delivered to a Party on a day which is a holiday for that Party, document shall be deemed delivered on next working day. The date receipt a document shall be deemed to be same date as date its delivery.
Commencing arbitration
8. (a) If pursuant to Article 177 this Agreement or to rules 19, 20 or 47 these Rules Procedure, any member arbitration panel is selected by lot, drawing lots shall be carried out at a time and place decided by complaining Party and promptly communicated to Party complained against. The Party complained against may, if it so chooses, be present during drawing lots. In any event, drawing lots shall be carried out with whichever Party/Parties is/are present.
(b) If, pursuant to Article 177 this Agreement or to rules 19, 20 or 47 these Rules Procedure, any member arbitration panel is selected by lot and there are two chairs Cooperation Committee, both chairs, or their delegates, or one chair alone in cases where other chair or his delegate does not accept to participate in drawing lots, shall perform selection by lot.
(c) The Parties shall notify selected arbitrators their appointment.
(d) An arbitrator who has been appointed according to procedure established in Article 177 this Agreement shall confirm his availability to serve as a member arbitration panel to Cooperation Committee within five days date in which he was informed his appointment.
(e) Unless Parties to dispute agree otherwise, Parties shall hold a meeting with arbitration panel in person or by other means communication within seven days establishment arbitration panel.
The Parties and arbitration panel shall determine such matters that Parties or arbitration panel deem appropriate, including remuneration and expenses to be paid to arbitrators. The remuneration and expenses shall be in accordance with WTO standards.
9. (a) Unless Parties agree otherwise within five days from date selection arbitrators, terms reference arbitration panel shall be: ‘to examine, in light relevant provisions Agreement invoked by parties to dispute, matter referred to in request for establishment arbitration panel, to rule on compatibility measure in question with provisions referred to in Article 173 and to deliver a report in accordance with Articles 180, 181, 182 and 195 this Agreement.’ (b) The Parties must notify agreed terms reference to arbitration panel within three days their agreement.
Initial submissions
10. The complaining Party shall deliver its initial written submission no later than 20 days after date establishment arbitration panel. The Party complained against shall deliver its written counter-submission no later than 20 days after date receipt initial written submission.
Working arbitration panels
11. The chairperson arbitration panel shall preside over all its meetings. An arbitration panel may delegate to chairperson authority to make administrative and procedural decisions.
12. Unless otherwise provided for in Chapter 14 ( Dispute Settlement ) Title III (Trade and Business this Agreement, arbitration panel may conduct its activities by any means, including telephone, facsimile transmissions or computer links.
13. Only arbitrators may take part in deliberations arbitration panel, but arbitration panel may permit its assistants to be present at its deliberations.
14. The drafting any report shall remain exclusive responsibility arbitration panel and must not be delegated.
15. Where a procedural question arises that is not covered by provisions Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement and Annexes V to VII to this Agreement, arbitration panel, after consulting Parties, may adopt an appropriate procedure that is compatible with those provisions.
16. When arbitration panel considers that there is a need to modify any time limits for its proceedings other than time limits set out in Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement or to make any other procedural or administrative adjustment, it shall inform Parties to dispute in writing reasons for change or adjustment and period time or adjustment needed.
Replacement
17. If an arbitrator is unable to participate in an arbitration proceeding under Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, withdraws from it, or must be replaced because non-compliance with requirements Code Conduct set out in Annex VI to this Agreement, a replacement shall be selected in accordance with Article 177 this Agreement and rule 8 these Rules Procedure.
18. Where a Party to dispute considers that an arbitrator does not comply with requirements Code Conduct and for this reason should be replaced, this Party shall deliver notification to other Party to dispute within 15 days date on which it obtained evidence circumstances underlying arbitrator's material violation Code Conduct.
19. Where a Party to dispute considers that an arbitrator other than chairperson does not comply with requirements Code Conduct, Parties to dispute shall consult and, if they agree on need to replace arbitrator, select a new arbitrator in accordance with Article 177 this Agreement and rule 8 these Rules Procedure.
If Parties to dispute fail to agree on need to replace an arbitrator, any Party to dispute may request that such matter be referred to chairperson arbitration panel, whose decision shall be final.
If, pursuant to such a request, chairperson finds that an arbitrator does not comply with requirements Code Conduct, new arbitrator shall be selected in accordance with Article 177 this Agreement and rule 8 these Rules Procedure.
20. Where a Party considers that chairperson arbitration panel does not comply with requirements Code Conduct, Parties shall consult and, if they agree on need to replace chairperson, select a new chairperson in accordance with Article 177 this Agreement and rule 8 these Rules Procedure.
If Parties fail to agree on need to replace chairperson, either Party may request that such matter be referred to one remaining members pool individuals from sub-list chairpersons referred to in Article 196(1) this Agreement. His name shall be drawn by lot by chair Cooperation Committee, or chair's delegate. The decision by so selectedperson on need to replace chairperson shall be final.
If so selected person decides that original chairperson does not comply with requirements Code Conduct, he shall select a new chairperson by lot among remaining pool individuals from sub-list chairpersons referred to under Article 196(1) this Agreement. The selection new chairperson shall be carried out within five days date decision referred to in this paragraph.
21. The arbitration panel proceedings shall be suspended for period taken to carry out procedures provided for in rules 18, 19 and 20 these Rules Procedure.
Hearings
22. The chairperson arbitration panel shall fix date and time hearing in consultation with Parties to dispute and other members arbitration panel, and shall confirm this in writing to Parties to dispute. This information shall also be made publicly available by Party in charge logistical administration proceedings, unless hearing is closed to public. Unless a Party disagrees, arbitration panel may decide not to convene a hearing.
23. Unless Parties agree otherwise, hearing shall be held in Brussels if complaining Party is Republic Kazakhstan and in Astana if complaining Party is Union.
24. The arbitration panel may convene additional hearings if Parties so agree.
25. All arbitrators shall be present during entirety any hearings.
26. The following persons may attend hearing, irrespective whether proceedings are open to public or not: (a) representatives Parties to dispute;
(b) advisers to Parties to dispute;
(c) administrative staff, interpreters, translators and court reporters; and (d) arbitrators' assistants.
Only representatives and advisers Parties to dispute may address arbitration panel.
27. No later than five days before date a hearing, each Party to dispute shall deliver to arbitration panel a list names persons who will make oral arguments or presentations at hearing on behalf that Party and other representatives or advisers attending hearing.
28. The arbitration panel shall conduct hearing in following manner, ensuring that complaining Party and Party complained against are afforded equal time: Argument (a) argument complaining Party (b) argument Party complained against Rebuttal Argument (a) argument complaining Party (b) counter-reply Party complained against
29. The arbitration panel may direct questions to either Party to dispute at any time during hearing.
30. The arbitration panel shall arrange for a transcript each hearing to be prepared and delivered as soon as possible to Parties to dispute. The Parties to dispute may comment on transcript and arbitration panel may consider those comments.
31. Each Party to dispute may deliver a supplementary written submission concerning any matter that arose during hearing within 10 days date hearing.
Questions in writing
32. The arbitration panel may, at any time during proceedings, address questions in writing to one Party or both Parties to dispute. Each Party to dispute shall receive a copy any questions put by arbitration panel.
33. A Party to dispute shall deliver a copy its written response to arbitration panel's questions to other Party to dispute. Each Party to dispute shall have opportunity to provide written comments on other Party's reply, to be delivered within five days date receipt such reply.
Confidentiality
34. Each Party to dispute and its advisers shall treat as confidential any information submitted to arbitration panel by other Party to dispute which that Party has designated as confidential. Where a Party to dispute submits a confidential version its written submissions to arbitration panel, Party shall also, upon request other Party, and no later than 15 days after date either request or submission, whichever is later, deliver a non-confidential summary information contained in its submissions that could be disclosed to public, and an explanation as to why non-disclosed information is confidential. Nothing in these Rules Procedure shall preclude a Party to dispute from disclosing statements its own positions to public to extent that, when making reference to information submitted by other Party, it does not disclose any information designated by other Party as confidential.
The arbitration panel shall meet in closed session when submission and arguments a Party contain confidential information. The Parties to dispute and their advisers shall maintain confidentiality arbitration panel hearings where hearings are held in closed session.
Non-confidential version arbitration panel report
35. If arbitration panel report contains information designated as confidential by a Party, arbitration panel shall prepare a non-confidential version report. The Parties shall be given opportunity to comment on non-confidential version and arbitration panel shall take their comments into account when producing final non-confidential version report.
Ex parte contacts
36. The arbitration panel shall not meet or communicate with a Party in absence other Party.
37. No member arbitration panel may discuss any aspect subject matter proceedings with one Party or both Parties to dispute in absence other arbitrators.
Amicus curiae submissions
38. Unless Parties agree otherwise within three days date establishment arbitration panel, arbitration panel may receive unsolicited written submissions from natural or legal persons established in territory a Party to dispute who are independent from governments Parties to dispute, provided that they are delivered within 10 days date establishment arbitration panel, that they are concise and in no case longer than 15 pages typed at double space and that they are directly relevant to a factual or a legal issue under consideration by arbitration panel.
39. The submission shall contain a description person making submission, whether natural or legal, including its nationality or place establishment, nature its activities, its legal status, general objectives and source its financing, and shall specify nature interest that person has in arbitration panel proceeding. It shall be drafted in languages chosen by Parties to dispute in accordance with rules 42 and 43 these Rules Procedure.
40. The arbitration panel shall list in its report all submissions it has received that conform to rules 38 and 39 these Rules Procedure. The arbitration panel shall not be obliged to address in its report arguments made in such submissions. Any such submission shall be delivered to Parties to dispute for their comments. The comments Parties to dispute shall be delivered within 10 days receipt submission, and any such comments shall be taken into consideration by arbitration panel.
Urgent cases
41. In cases urgency referred to in Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, arbitration panel, after consulting Parties, shall adjust time limits referred to in these Rules Procedure as appropriate and shall notify Parties such adjustments.
Translation and interpretation
42. During consultations referred to in Article 174 this Agreement, and no later than meeting referred to in rule 8(e) these Rules Procedure, Parties to dispute shall endeavour to agree on a common working language for proceedings before arbitration panel.
43. If Parties to dispute are unable to agree on a common working language, each Party shall make its written submissions in its chosen language. In such a case, that Party shall provide at same time a translation in language chosen by other Party, unless its submissions are written in one working languages WTO. The Party complained against shall arrange for interpretation oral submissions into languages chosen by Parties.
44. Arbitration panel reports shall be issued in language or languages chosen by Parties to dispute.
45. Any Party to dispute may provide comments on accuracy translation any translated version a document drawn up in accordance with these Rules Procedure.
46. Each Party shall bear costs translation its written submissions. Any costs incurred in translation an arbitration panel report shall be borne equally by Parties to dispute.
Other procedures
47. These Rules Procedure are also applicable to procedures established under Articles 174, 184(2), 185(2), 186(3) and 187(2) this Agreement. However, time limits laid down in these Rules Procedure shall be adjusted by arbitration panel in line with special time limits provided for adoption a report by arbitration panel in those other procedures.
Definitions
1. In this Code Conduct: (a) ‘arbitrator’ means a member an arbitration panel effectively established under Article 177 this Agreement;
(b) ‘candidate’ means an individual whose name is on list arbitrators referred to in Article 196 this Agreement and who is under consideration for selection as a member an arbitration panel under Article 177 this Agreement;
(c) ‘assistant’ means a person who, under terms appointment an arbitrator, conducts research or provides assistance to arbitrator;
(d) ‘proceeding’, unless otherwise specified, means an arbitration panel proceeding under Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement;
(e) ‘staff’, in respect an arbitrator, means persons under direction and control arbitrator, other than assistants;
(f) ‘mediator’ means a person who conducts a mediation procedure in accordance with Annex VII to this Agreement.
Responsibilities to process
2. Every candidate and arbitrator shall avoid impropriety and appearance impropriety, shall be independent and impartial, shall avoid direct and indirect conf licts interest and shall observe high standards conduct so that integrity and impartiality dispute settlement mechanism is preserved. Former arbitrators must comply with obligations established in rules 15, 16, 17 and 18 this Code Conduct.
Disclosure obligations
3. Prior to confirmation his selection as an arbitrator under Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, a candidate shall disclose any interest, relationship or matter that is likely to affect his independence or impartiality or that might reasonably create an appearance impropriety or bias in proceedings. To that end, a candidate shall make all reasonable efforts to become aware any such interests, relationships and matters.
4. A candidate or arbitrator shall communicate, in writing, matters concerning actual or potential violations this Code Conduct only to Cooperation Committee for consideration by Parties.
5. Once selected, an arbitrator shall continue to make all reasonable efforts to become aware any interests, relationships or matters referred to in rule 3 this Code Conduct and shall disclose them. The disclosure obligation is a continuing duty which requires an arbitrator to disclose any such interests, relationships or matters that may arise during any stage proceedings. The arbitrator shall disclose such interests, relationships or matters by informing Cooperation Committee, in writing, for consideration by Parties.
Duties arbitrators
6. Upon confirmation his selection, an arbitrator shall be available to perform and shall perform his duties thoroughly and expeditiously throughout course proceedings, with fairness and diligence.
7. An arbitrator shall consider only those issues raised in proceeding and necessary for an arbitration panel report, and shall not delegate this duty to any other person.
8. An arbitrator shall take all appropriate steps to ensure that his assistant and staff are aware of, and comply with, rules 2, 3, 4, 5, 16, 17 and 18 this Code Conduct.
9. An arbitrator shall not engage in ex parte contacts concerning proceedings.
Independence and impartiality arbitrators
10. An arbitrator shall be independent and impartial, avoid creating an appearance impropriety or bias, and shall not be inf luenced by self-interest, outside pressure, political considerations, public clamour and loyalty to a Party or fear criticism.
11. An arbitrator shall not, directly or indirectly, incur any obligation or accept any benefit that would in any way interfere, or appear to interfere, with proper performance his duties.
12. An arbitrator shall not use his position on arbitration panel to advance any personal or private interests. An arbitrator shall avoid actions that may create impression that he is in a position to be inf luenced by others.
13. An arbitrator shall not allow financial, business, professional, personal, or social relationships or responsibilities to inf luence his conduct or judgement.
14. An arbitrator shall avoid entering into any relationship or acquiring any financial interest that is likely to affect his impartiality or that might reasonably create an appearance impropriety or bias.
Obligations former arbitrators
15. All former arbitrators must avoid actions that may create appearance that they were biased in carrying out their duties or derived advantage from decision or report arbitration panel.
Confidentiality
16. No arbitrator or former arbitrator shall at any time disclose or use any non-public information concerning a proceeding or acquired during a proceeding except for purposes that proceeding and shall not, in any case, disclose or use any such information to gain personal advantage or advantage for others or to adversely affect interests others.
17. An arbitrator shall not disclose an arbitration panel report or parts thereof prior to its publication in accordance with Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement.
18. An arbitrator or a former arbitrator shall not disclose deliberations an arbitration panel, or any arbitrator's view at any time.
Expenses
19. Each arbitrator shall keep a record and render a final account time devoted to procedure and his expenses, as well as time and expenses his assistant and staff.
Mediators
20. The disciplines described in this Code Conduct as applying to arbitrators or former arbitrators shall apply, mutatis mutandis , to mediators.
Article 1 Objective The objective this Annex is to facilitate finding a mutually agreed solution through a comprehensive and expeditious procedure with assistance a mediator.
Section A Procedure under mediation mechanism Article 2 Request for information
1. At any time before initiation mediation procedure, a Party may deliver a written request for information regarding a measure adversely affecting trade or investment between Parties. The Party to which such request is made shall, within 20 days receipt request, deliver a written response containing its comments on information contained in request.
2. When responding Party considers it will not be able to deliver a response within 20 days receipt request, it shall promptly notify requesting Party, stating reasons for delay and providing an estimate shortest period within which it will be able to deliver its response.
Article 3 Initiation procedure
1. A Party may request that Parties enter into a mediation procedure at any time, by means a written request delivered to other Party. The request shall be sufficiently detailed to present concerns requesting Party clearly and shall: (a) identify specific measure at issue;
(b) provide a statement alleged adverse effects that requesting Party believes measure has, or will have, on trade or investment between Parties; and (c) explain how requesting Party considers that those effects are linked to measure.
2. The mediation procedure may only be initiated by mutual agreement Parties. When a request is made pursuant to paragraph 1, Party to which request is made shall accord sympathetic consideration to request and deliver its written acceptance or rejection to requesting Party within 10 days its receipt.
Article 4 Selection mediator
1. The Parties shall endeavour to agree on a mediator within 15 days delivery acceptance referred to in Article 3(2) this Annex.
2. In event that Parties are unable to agree on mediator within time frame laid down in paragraph 1 this Article, either Party may request chair Cooperation Committee, or chair's delegate, to select mediator by lot from list established under Article 196(1) this Agreement. Representatives both Parties shall be invited, with sufficient advance notice, to be present when lots are drawn. In any event, drawing lots shall be carried out with Party/Parties that is/are present.
3. The chair Cooperation Committee, or chair's delegate, shall select mediator within five days request made pursuant to paragraph 2.
4. Should list referred to in Article 196(1) this Agreement not be established at time a request is made pursuant to Article 3 this Annex, mediator shall be drawn by lot from individuals who have been formally proposed by one or both Parties.
5. A mediator shall not be a citizen either Party, unless Parties agree otherwise.
6. The mediator shall, in an impartial and transparent manner, assist Parties in bringing clarity to measure and its possible effects on trade, and in reaching a mutually agreed solution.
7. The Code Conduct for Members Arbitration Panels and Mediators set out in Annex VI to this Agreement shall apply to mediators, mutatis mutandis.
8. Rules 3 to 7 (Notifications) and 42 to 46 (Translation and interpretation) Rules Procedure for Arbitration set out in Annex V to this Agreement shall apply, mutatis mutandis .
Article 5 Rules Mediation Procedure
1. Within 10 days appointment mediator, Party which invoked mediation procedure shall deliver a detailed, written description its concerns to mediator and to other Party, in particular operation measure at issue and its effects on trade. Within 20 days receipt this description, other Party may deliver written comments on description. Either Party may include any information that it deems relevant in its description or comments.
2. The mediator may decide on most appropriate way bringing clarity to measure concerned and its possible effects on trade. In particular, mediator may organise meetings between Parties, consult Parties jointly or individually, seek assistance of, or consult with, relevant experts and stakeholders and provide any additional support requested by Parties. The mediator shall consult with Parties before seeking assistance of, or consulting with, relevant experts and stakeholders.
3. The mediator shall not advise or comment on consistency measure at issue with this Agreement. The mediator may offer advice and propose a solution for consideration Parties. The Parties may accept or reject proposed solution, or agree on a different solution.
4. The mediation procedure shall take place in territory Party to which request was addressed, or by mutual agreement in any other location or by any other means.
5. The Parties shall endeavour to reach a mutually agreed solution within 60 days appointment mediator. Pending a final agreement, Parties may consider possible interim solutions.
6. The mutually agreed solution or interim solution may be adopted by means a decision Cooperation Committee. Mutually agreed solutions shall be made publicly available. The version disclosed to public shall not contain any information a Party has designated as confidential.
7. On request Parties, mediator shall deliver a draft factual report to Parties, providing a brief summary of: (i) measure at issue; (ii) procedures followed; and (iii) any mutually agreed solution reached, including possible interim solutions. The mediator shall allow Parties 15 days to comment on draft report. After considering comments Parties received within that period, mediator shall, within 15 days, deliver a final factual report to Parties. The factual report shall not include any interpretation this Agreement.
8. The procedure shall be terminated by: (a) adoption a mutually agreed solution by Parties, on date adoption thereof;
(b) mutual agreement Parties at any stage procedure, on date that agreement;
(c) a written declaration mediator, after consultation with Parties, that further efforts at mediation would be to no avail, on date that declaration; or (d) a written declaration a Party after exploring mutually agreed solutions under mediation procedure and after having considered any advice and proposed solutions by mediator, on date that declaration.
Section B Implementa tion Article 6 Implementation a mutually agreed solution
1. Where Parties reach agreement on a solution, each Party shall take measures necessary to implement mutually agreed solution within agreed timeframe.
2. The implementing Party shall notify other Party, in writing, any steps or measures taken to implement mutually agreed solution.
Section C General prov isions Article 7 Confidentiality and relationship to dispute settlement
1. Unless Parties agree otherwise, and without prejudice to Article 5(6) this Annex, all steps procedure, including any advice or proposed solution, are confidential. However, any Party may disclose to public fact that mediation is taking place.
2. The mediation procedure is without prejudice to Parties' rights and obligations under Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement, or any other agreement.
3. Consultations under Chapter 14 (Dispute Settlement) Title III (Trade and Business) this Agreement are not required before initiating mediation procedure. However, a Party should normally avail itself other relevant cooperation or consultation provisions provided for in this Agreement before initiating mediation procedure.
4. A Party shall not rely on, or introduce as evidence, in other dispute settlement procedures under this Agreement or any other agreement, nor shall a panel take into consideration: (a) positions taken by other Party in course mediation procedure or information gathered under Article 5(2) this Annex;
(b) fact that other Party has indicated its willingness to accept a solution to measure subject to mediation; or (c) advice given or proposals made by mediator.
5. A mediator may not serve as a member a panel in a dispute settlement proceeding under this Agreement or under WTO Agreement involving same matter for which he has been a mediator.
Article 8 Time limits Any time limit referred to in this Annex may be modified by mutual agreement between Parties.
Article 9 Costs
1. Each Party shall bear its own expenses derived from participation in mediation procedure.
2. The Parties shall share jointly and equally expenses derived from organisational matters, including remuneration and expenses mediator. The remuneration mediator shall be in accordance with that provided for a chairperson an arbitration panel in accordance with rule 8(e) Rules Procedure set out in Annex V to this Agreement.
PROTOCOL on mutual administrative assistance in customs matters Article 1 Definitions For purposes this Protocol: (a) ‘customs legislation’ means legal or regulatory provisions applicable in territories Parties, governing import, export and transit goods and their placing under any other customs regime or procedure, including measures prohibition, restriction and control thereof;
(b) ‘applicant authority’ means a competent administrative authority which makes a request for assistance pursuant to this Protocol and which has been designated by a Party for that purpose;
(c) ‘requested authority’ means a competent administrative authority which receives a request for assistance pursuant to this Protocol and which has been designated by a Party for that purpose;
(d) ‘personal data’ means any information relating to an identified or identifiable individual;
(e) ‘operation in breach customs legislation’ means any violation or attempted violation customs legislation.
Article 2 Scope
1. The Parties shall assist each other, in areas their competence, in manner and under conditions laid down in this Protocol, to ensure correct application their customs legislation by preventing, investigating and combating operations in breach that legislation.
2. The assistance in customs matters, as provided for in this Protocol, shall apply to any administrative authority Parties which is competent for application this Protocol. It shall not prejudice rules governing mutual assistance in criminal matters and shall not cover exchange information obtained under powers exercised at request a judicial authority, except where communication such information is authorised by that authority.
3. The assistance to recover duties, taxes or fines is not covered by this Protocol.
Article 3 Assistance on request
1. At request applicant authority, requested authority shall provide former with all relevant information which may enable it to ensure that customs legislation is correctly applied, including information regarding activities noted or planned which are or could be operations in breach customs legislation.
2. At request applicant authority, requested authority shall inform former of: (a) circumstances (facts and conditions) export goods from territory a Party and import into territory other Party, specifying, where appropriate, customs procedure applied to goods;
(b) circumstances (facts and conditions) import goods into territory a Party and export from territory other Party, specifying, where appropriate, customs procedure applied to goods.
3. At request applicant authority, requested authority shall, within framework its legal or regulatory provisions, take necessary steps to ensure special surveillance of: (a) natural or legal persons in respect whom there are reasonable grounds for believing that they are or have been involved in operations in breach customs legislation;
(b) places where goods are stocked in respect which there are reasonable grounds to suspect that operations in breach customs legislation take place;
(c) goods transported or intended for transportation in respect which there are reasonable grounds to suspect that operations in breach customs legislation take place;
(d) means transport carrying goods in respect which there are reasonable grounds to suspect that operations in breach customs legislation take place.
Article 4 Spontaneous assistance The Parties shall assist each other, at their own initiative and in accordance with their legal or regulatory provisions, if they consider that to be necessary for correct application customs legislation, in particular by providing information pertaining to: (a) activities which are or appear to be operations in breach customs legislation and which may be interest to other Party;
(b) new means or methods employed in carrying out operations in breach customs legislation;
(c) goods known to be subject to operations in breach customs legislation;
(d) natural or legal persons in respect whom there are reasonable grounds for believing that they are or have been involved in operations in breach customs legislation;
(e) means transport in respect which there are reasonable grounds for believing that they have been, are, or may be used in operations in breach customs legislation.
Article 5 Delivery and notification At request applicant authority, requested authority shall, in accordance with legal or regulatory provisions applicable to latter, take all necessary measures in order to deliver any documents or to notify any decisions, originating from applicant authority and falling within scope this Protocol, to an addressee residing or established in territory requested authority.
Requests for delivery documents or notification decisions shall be made in writing in an official language requested authority or in a language acceptable to that authority.
Article 6 Form and substance requests for assistance
1. Requests pursuant to this Protocol shall be made in writing. They shall be accompanied by documents necessary to enable compliance with request. When required because urgency situation, oral requests may be accepted, but must be confirmed in writing immediately.
2. Requests pursuant to paragraph 1 shall include following information: (a) applicant authority;
(b) object and reason for request;
(c) requested measure;
(d) legal or regulatory provisions and other legal elements involved;
(e) indications as exact and comprehensive as possible on natural or legal persons who are target enquiries;
(f) a summary relevant facts and enquiries already carried out;
(g) any other relevant information that is needed to execute request.
3. Requests shall be submitted in an official language requested authority or in a language acceptable to that authority. This requirement shall not apply to any documents that accompany request under paragraph 1.
4. If a request does not meet formal requirements set out in paragraphs 1 to 3, its correction or completion may be requested; in meantime precautionary measures may be ordered.
Article 7 Execution requests
1. In order to comply with a request for assistance, requested authority shall proceed, within limits its competence and available resources, as though it were acting on its own account or at request other authorities that same Party, by supplying information already in authority's possession, by carrying out appropriate enquiries or by arranging for them to be carried out. This shall also apply to any other authority to which request has been addressed by requested authority when latter cannot act on its own.
2. Requests for assistance shall be executed in accordance with legal or regulatory provisions requested Party. In case request cannot be executed, requesting Party shall be informed this without delay.
3. Duly authorised officials a Party may, with agreement other Party and subject to conditions laid down by latter, be present in offices requested authority or any other concerned authority in accordance with paragraph 1 to obtain information relating to activities that are or may be operations in breach customs legislation which applicant authority needs for purposes this Protocol.
4. Duly authorised officials a Party may, with agreement other Party and subject to conditions laid down by latter, be present at enquiries carried out in latter's territory.
Article 8 Form in which information is to be communicated
1. The requested authority shall communicate results enquiries to applicant authority in writing together with relevant documents, certified copies or other items.
2. This information may be in a computerised form.
3. Original documents shall be transmitted only upon request in cases where certified copies would be insufficient.
These originals shall be returned at earliest opportunity.
Article 9 Exceptions to obligation to provide assistance
1. Assistance may be refused or may be subject to satisfaction certain conditions or requirements, in cases where a Party is opinion that assistance under this Protocol would: (a) be likely to prejudice sovereignty Republic Kazakhstan or that a Member State which has been requested to provide assistance under this Protocol; or (b) be likely to prejudice public policy, security or other essential interests, in particular in cases referred to under Article 10(2) this Protocol; or (c) violate an industrial, commercial or professional secret.
2. Assistance may be postponed by requested authority on grounds that it will interfere with an ongoing investigation, prosecution or proceeding. In such a case, requested authority shall consult with applicant authority to determine if assistance can be given subject to such terms or conditions as requested authority may require.
3. Where applicant authority seeks assistance which it would itself be unable to provide if so requested, it shall draw attention to that fact in its request. It shall then be for requested authority to decide how to respond to such a request.
4. For cases referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2, decision requested authority and reasons therefor must be communicated to applicant authority without delay.
Article 10 Information exchange and confidentiality
1. Any information communicated in whatsoever form pursuant to this Protocol shall be a confidential or restricted nature, depending on rules applicable in each Party. It shall be covered by obligation official secrecy and shall enjoy protection extended to similar information under relevant laws Party that received it and corresponding provisions applying to institutions Union.
2. Personal data may be exchanged only where Party which may receive them undertakes to protect such data in a manner that is considered adequate by Party that may supply them.
3. The use, in administrative or judicial proceedings instituted in respect operations in breach customs legislation, information obtained under this Protocol is considered to be for purposes this Protocol. Therefore, Parties may, in their records evidence, repor ts and testimonies and in proceedings and charges brought before courts, use as evidence information obtained and documents consulted in accordance with provisions this Protocol. The competent authority which supplied that information or gave access to those documents shall be notified such use.
4. The information obtained under this Protocol shall be used solely for purposes set out in this Protocol. Where a Party wishes to use such information for other purposes, it shall obtain prior written consent authority which provided information. Such use shall then be subject to any restrictions laid down by latter authority.
Article 11 Experts and witnesses An official a requested authority may be authorised to appear, within limitations authorisation granted, as an expert or a witness in administrative or judicial proceedings regarding matters covered by this Protocol, and may produce such objects, documents or certified copies thereof, as may be needed for proceedings. The request to official shall be made by applicant authority and must indicate specifically before which administrative or judicial authority official will have to appear, on what matters and in what capacity (title or qualification).
Article 12 Assistance expenses The Parties shall waive all claims on each other for reimbursement expenses incurred pursuant to this Protocol, except, as appropriate, for expenses related to experts and witnesses, and those related to interpreters and translators who are not public service employees.
Article 13 Implementation
1. The implementation this Protocol shall be entrusted, on one hand, to customs authorities Republic Kazakhstan and, on other hand, to competent services Commission and customs authorities Member States Union, as appropriate. They shall decide on all practical measures and arrangements necessary for its application, taking into consideration rules in force in particular in field data protection.
2. The Parties shall consult each other and subsequently keep each other informed detailed rules implemen tation which are adopted in accordance with provisions this Protocol.
Article 14 Other agreements
1. Taking into account respective competencies and Member States Union, provisions this Protocol shall: (a) not affect obligations Parties under any other international agreement or convention;
(b) be deemed complementary to agreements on mutual assistance which have been or may be concluded between individual Member States and Republic Kazakhstan; and (c) not affect provisions governing communication between competent services Commission and customs authorities Member States any information obtained under this Protocol which could be interest to or to Member States Union.
2. Notwithstanding paragraph 1 this Article, provisions this Protocol shall take precedence over provisions any bilateral Agreement on mutual assistance which has been or may be concluded between individual Member States and Republic Kazakhstan insofar as provisions latter are incompatible with those this Protocol.
3. In respect questions relating to applicability this Protocol, Parties shall consult each other in order to resolve matter in framework a regular dialogue on customs matters between Parties.
Po zaznajomieniu się z powyższą umową, w imieniu Rzeczypospolitej Polskiej oświadczam, że: została ona uznana za słuszną zarówno w całości, jak i każde z postanowień w niej zawartych, jest przyjęta, ratyfikowana i potwierdzona, będzie niezmiennie zachowywana.
na dowód czego wydany został akt niniejszy, opatrzony pieczęcią Rzeczypospolitej Polskiej.
dano w Warszawie dnia 10 stycznia 2017 r.

#### Rozdział 14 sekcja 3 podsekcja 2 (Rozstrzyganie sporów) niniejszego tytułu nie ma zastosowania do sporów objętych zakresem niniejszego rozdziału. W odniesieniu do każdego takiego sporu, po tym jak organ arbitrażowy przedstawi swoje sprawozdanie końcowe zgodnie z art. 180 i 182, Strony, uwzględniając wspomniane sprawozdanie, poddają dyskusji odpowiednie środki, jakie mają zostać wdrożone. Komitet Współpracy monitoruje wdrażanie takich środków i śledzi tę sprawę, w tym za pośrednictwem mechanizmu, o którym mowa w art. 154 ust. 3.
